### [1] [Wilsonian exact renormalization group flow in the gauged Nambu--Jona-Lasinio model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00559)
*Sorato Nagao,Hiroshi Suzuki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies the gauge-invariant gradient-flow exact renormalization group (GFERG) to the gauged NJL model, showing that chiral symmetry breaking is triggered solely by strong U(1) gauge coupling—no nontrivial fixed point with finite e² exists; the Gaussian and NJL fixed points merge at e_c² ≈ 17, beyond which the gauge coupling flows to zero in the IR.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve whether chiral symmetry breaking in the gauged NJL model arises from gauge dynamics alone—and whether a nontrivial interacting fixed point exists—while preserving manifest gauge invariance, avoiding gauge-dependent artifacts of conventional RG methods.

Method: Uses the gradient-flow exact renormalization group (GFERG), a Wilsonian RG framework preserving gauge invariance; employs a gauge-invariant truncation of the 1PI Wilson action including general photon kinetic function K_τ(k²), fermion mass, and scalar/vector four-Fermi couplings; treats both four-Fermi couplings and gauge coupling e² as flowing parameters.

Result: For fixed e², Gaussian and NJL (quasi-)fixed points merge at e_c² ≈ 17; when e² is promoted to a running coupling, its beta function is fully determined by the photon anomalous dimension γ_A > 0, implying no IR-stable fixed point with e² ≠ 0; strong UV gauge coupling alone drives chiral symmetry breaking even with vanishing four-Fermi interactions; in any continuum limit, e² → 0 within the truncation.

Conclusion: Chiral symmetry breaking is dynamically induced by strong U(1) gauge interaction, but the theory is asymptotically free-like in the IR: no interacting conformal fixed point exists, and the only viable continuum limit is the trivial (free) one with vanishing gauge coupling.

Abstract: We study the Wilsonian exact renormalization group flow of the gauged Nambu--Jona-Lasinio (NJL) model, a four-dimensional $U(1)$ gauge theory with chirally invariant four-Fermi interactions, by using the gradient flow exact renormalization group~(GFERG). Our gauge-invariant ansatz (truncation) for the 1PI Wilson action keeps a general photon kinetic function~$\mathcal{K}_\tau(k^2)$, the fermion mass, and the scalar and vector four-Fermi couplings. For a fixed gauge coupling~$e^2$, the Gaussian and NJL (quasi-)fixed points on the critical surface merge at~$e_c^2\simeq17$, as anticipated by the ladder Schwinger--Dyson equation and the conventional Wilsonian renormalization group. We then let $e^2$ itself flow. Owing to the manifest gauge invariance of the GFERG, the flow of~$e^2$ is governed solely by the photon anomalous dimension~$\gamma_A$, which remains positive along both branches up to the merging point. Hence, no fixed point with~$e^2\neq0$ exists. Flows from a sufficiently large~$e^2$ in the ultraviolet (UV) reach the chirally broken phase even for vanishing four-Fermi couplings, so the gauge interaction does trigger chiral symmetry breaking. The merging point, however, is not a fixed point, and the gauge coupling vanishes in any continuum limit within our truncation.

</details>


### [2] [Thermal Festina Lente](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02559)
*Fayez Abu-Ajamieh,Nobuchika Okada,Roman Pasechnik,Victoria Venken,Zhi-Wei Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper strengthens the Festina Lente bound on light charged particles in de Sitter space by incorporating thermal enhancement of black hole discharge, yielding a tighter mass bound m ≳ ℓ(cV/σ)^(1/4), with logarithmic dependence on coupling and charge. It derives concrete lower limits for neutrino masses in B−L models and millicharges, and shows Standard Model electrons violate the bound during inflation unless photon mass generation or coupling suppression occurs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To refine the Festina Lente bound by including thermal effects on black hole discharge in de Sitter space with radiation, making it quantitatively robust and phenomenologically predictive for current cosmological parameters (e.g., observed vacuum energy, CMB constraints).

Method: Analyzes Schwinger pair production and thermal emission rates for charged particles near near-extremal de Sitter black holes; incorporates temperature-dependent discharge timescales; computes mass thresholds ensuring cosmic censorship is preserved; applies bounds to B−L gauge models, neutrino millicharges, and SM EFT during inflation.

Result: Derives improved mass bound m ≳ ℓ(cV/σ)^(1/4) with ℓ ∼ log(gq); finds 0.03–0.2 eV lower bounds for particles with gq ∈ [10⁻²⁶, e]; predicts m_ν,lightest ≳ 10–43 meV for unbroken B−L with g_B−L ∈ [10⁻²⁹, 10⁻²⁴], conflicting with DESI DR2 for g_B−L ≳ 7×10⁻²⁸; constrains neutrino millicharge ε_ν ≲ 2×10⁻²⁷; shows SM electron violates bound during slow-roll inflation unless photon is massive or coupling suppressed.

Conclusion: The thermally enhanced Festina Lente bound imposes stringent, testable constraints on light charged particles in our universe — ruling out broad regions of parameter space for B−L extensions, millicharged neutrinos, and standard inflationary scenarios with unmodified electrodynamics.

Abstract: According to the Festina Lente bound, particles charged under a $U(1)$ gauge field cannot be too light. Otherwise, the largest charged black holes of de Sitter space would discharge in a way that leads to a crunching singularity in violation of cosmic censorship. The Festina Lente bound has previously been extended to also include a background of thermal radiation, but where vacuum energy still dominates cosmic evolution, which imposes a maximal temperature $T\leq T_{\max}=(cV/\sigma)^{1/4}$. We extend this analysis by accounting for thermal enhancement of the discharge process and making the analysis more quantitative. The result is a bound $m\gtrsim\ell (cV/\sigma)^{1/4}$ on the masses of charged particles. Here, $\ell$ is a term that depends logarithmically on the gauge coupling and particle charge. At weak coupling this leads to a significantly stronger constraint than the $\sqrt{gq}$ scaling of the original Festina Lente bound. As a result, the present vacuum energy requires every particle of charge $10^{-26}\lesssim gq\lesssim e$ to be heavier than $0.03$--$0.2$ eV, provided its gauge boson is lighter than the Hubble scale. An unbroken gauged $B-L$ with Dirac neutrinos gives $m_{\nu,{\rm lightest}}\gtrsim10$--$43$ meV for $10^{-29}\lesssim g_{B-L}\lesssim10^{-24}$, in tension with the DESI DR2 limit once $g_{B-L}\gtrsim7\times10^{-28}$. The same limit bounds a neutrino millicharge to $\epsilon_\nu\lesssim2\times10^{-27}$. We also revisit the Standard Model effective field theory constraints from the electroweak phase transition. During slow-roll inflation the Standard Model electron violates the bound over the whole range of Hubble rates allowed by the CMB tensor limit and by nucleosynthesis, unless the photon is Higgsed, its coupling is suppressed, or one considers very fine-tuned versions of Higgs inflation.

</details>


### [3] [The Devil Went Down to de Sitter: Divergent Effective Field Theory Expansions in Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00189)
*Daniele Dorigoni,Laura Engelbrecht,Nadine Nussbaumer,Guilherme L. Pimentel*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies resurgence theory to cosmological effective field theories (EFTs) in de Sitter space, showing that the asymptotic nature of the EFT expansion—caused by particle production—encodes non-perturbative physical data; it demonstrates how to extract exact predictions via resurgent completion and proposes a general prescription linking cosmological EFTs to the de Sitter S-matrix.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Cosmological EFTs in time-dependent backgrounds like de Sitter space exhibit asymptotic (not convergent) perturbative expansions due to long-range correlations from spontaneous particle production—a flat-space EFT paradigm fails to capture this. The motivation is to understand and exploit the physical information encoded in the large-order behavior of such expansions.

Method: Resurgence analysis is applied to the asymptotic EFT expansion in de Sitter space; the authors reconstruct non-perturbative contributions (e.g., instanton-like transseries) from the perturbative coefficients and organize the resurgent completion using the de Sitter S-matrix structure.

Result: Exact, non-perturbative predictions (e.g., correlators or observables) are recovered from the divergent perturbative series; the resurgent completion is shown to be naturally expressed in terms of the de Sitter S-matrix, leading to a concrete conjecture for extracting S-matrix data from generic cosmological EFTs.

Conclusion: The asymptoticity of cosmological EFT expansions is not a limitation but a feature: it hides non-perturbative physics recoverable via resurgence; this establishes a systematic bridge between perturbative EFT calculations and exact S-matrix–like observables in accelerating spacetimes.

Abstract: Effective field theories (EFTs) in de Sitter space, and in accelerating cosmologies more generally, must capture phenomena absent in flat space, most notably long-range correlations generated by spontaneous particle production. When heavy physics is integrated out in such time-dependent backgrounds, these effects leave an imprint on the EFT expansion: even at tree level, this is generically an asymptotic rather than convergent series. This is not a failure of EFT. The large-order behavior of the expansion coefficients encodes physical information inaccessible at any finite order, so cosmological EFTs contain hidden data. Using resurgence analysis, we show how this information can be systematically extracted to recover exact and non-perturbative predictions from the perturbative expansion. In the example we study, the resurgent completion is naturally organized in terms of the de Sitter $S$-matrix. This leads us to conjecture a prescription for extracting $S$-matrix data from cosmological EFTs in general. The framework extends to any setting where particle production, or a similar non-perturbative phenomenon, renders the EFT expansion asymptotic. A longer companion paper will provide a more detailed and pedagogical treatment.

</details>


### [4] [Non-Invertible Charges for M-Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00196)
*Kenneth Intriligator,Aiden Sheckler,Eric Y. Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a 'pseudo 6-group' model for non-invertible charge defects in continuous 2- and 6-groups to describe the gauge charges of M2 and M5 branes in M-theory, successfully reproducing key physical phenomena including Hanany-Witten moves, a_{N−1} charge structure, higher-form symmetries, singleton decomposition of M5 worldvolume theories, and M5/anti-M5 couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formalize the non-invertible fusion structure of M-theory M2 and M5 brane gauge charges—which resemble but do not strictly satisfy 6-group axioms—by developing a consistent algebraic framework for non-invertible topological defect operators in higher-gauge theory.

Method: Constructs a 'pseudo 6-group' 𝔊 via categorical and higher-algebraic methods, modeling non-invertible charge defects for continuous 2-groups and 6-groups; analyzes its representation-theoretic and topological properties, and matches them against established phenomenology of M-branes in string/M-theory.

Result: Identifies a concrete pseudo 6-group 𝔊 whose charge defect algebra reproduces Hanany-Witten transitions, 𝔞_{N−1} root lattice charges, higher-form symmetries acting on M2/M5 operators, singleton decomposition of (2,0) tensor multiplets, and consistent couplings between M5 and anti-M5 states.

Conclusion: The gauge charge structure of M2 and M5 branes is effectively captured—not by a strict 6-group—but by a pseudo 6-group with non-invertible fusion, providing a unified higher-categorical framework for their topological and symmetry properties in M-theory.

Abstract: The gauge charges of M-theory M2 and M5 branes almost resemble the charges of a 6-group. The charges of such a 6-group are expected to be modeled by operators with non-invertible fusion. We here consider non-invertible charge defects for continuous 2-groups and 6-groups, aiming to capture the gauge charges of M2 and M5 branes. We find a model for charges of a "pseudo 6-group" $\frak{G}$ which possesses remarkable similarities with known features of M2 and M5 branes, including: Hanany-Witten moves, the $\frak{a}_{N-1}$ charges, higher-form symmetry, and singleton decomposition of M5-brane worldvolume theories, and couplings of M5/anti-M5 states.

</details>


### [5] [The Multi-trace Eigenstate Thermalization Hypothesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00197)
*Ahmed I. Abdalla,Gabriele Di Ubaldo,Elisa Tabor*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces multi-trace ETH—a generalization of eigenstate thermalization that governs connected correlations of operator matrix elements with arbitrary index cycle structures—and links it to local rotational invariance, free probability, and gravitational path integrals across 2D and 3D gravity, with evidence from chaotic quantum systems and semiclassical wormholes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify the description of connected higher-order operator correlations in chaotic quantum systems and multi-boundary gravitational amplitudes (e.g., wormholes), which standard ETH fails to capture due to its restriction to single-trace observables.

Method: Derives multi-trace ETH from local rotational invariance; maps its data to higher-order free cumulants; constructs a cyclic matrix model; computes multi-trace correlations using semiclassical gravity tools—including JT gravity with quartic couplings, Virasoro TQFT, and conformal mean-field theory—alongside periodic orbit analysis in few-body systems.

Result: Multi-trace ETH successfully describes linear ramp contributions to spectral form factors via correlated periodic orbits; yields interaction-induced multi-trace corrections in 2D JT gravity tied to winding matter diagrams; and generates non-Gaussian multi-trace effects in 3D gravity from topology-changing saddles and light matter, explicitly computed for knot/link complements and torus wormholes.

Conclusion: Multi-trace ETH provides a universal framework bridging quantum chaos (connected correlators) and holographic gravity (multi-boundary wormhole amplitudes), unifying disparate phenomena under free-probabilistic and cyclic matrix model structures.

Abstract: We introduce a multi-trace eigenstate thermalization hypothesis, an extension of ETH that governs connected correlations of operator matrix elements with arbitrary index structures, including several disjoint index cycles. We derive multi-trace ETH from local rotational invariance, relate its independent data to higher-order free cumulants, and construct a cyclic matrix model that encodes the resulting multi-cycle data. We provide evidence for multi-trace ETH in chaotic quantum systems and compute multi-trace correlations in low-dimensional gravity. In few-body systems, multi-trace contributions arise from correlated periodic orbits, including a linear ramp contribution to the one-point spectral form factor. In two dimensions, we compute interaction-induced multi-trace correlations from a quartically coupled bulk scalar in JT gravity, relating winding matter diagrams to corrections of matter-channel weights and anomalous dimensions. In three dimensions, we show that multi-trace non-Gaussianities arise from both topology-changing saddles and light matter. Using Virasoro TQFT and conformal mean-field theory, we compute examples involving knot and link complements and torus wormholes with matter loops. Across these settings, multi-trace ETH provides a common framework for both connected correlators in chaotic quantum systems and multi-boundary wormhole amplitudes in gravity.

</details>


### [6] [Generalized Toric Threefolds from Scattering Diagrams and Broken Lines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00206)
*Antoine Bourget,Ignacio Carreño Bolla,Quentin Lamouret*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper provides an explicit, geometric description of Generalized Toric Calabi–Yau threefolds—arising from five-brane webs on seven-branes—using scattering diagrams and broken lines; it constructs affine charts via local theta-function algebras and interprets deformation parameters physically, including under Hanany–Witten transitions and tessellation changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend toric geometry tools to non-toric Calabi–Yau threefolds arising in F-theory and brane-web constructions, enabling explicit coordinate descriptions and physical interpretation of moduli.

Method: Uses scattering diagrams and broken lines (inspired by Gross–Siebert program and recent work arXiv:2410.04714) to define local theta-function algebras and their transition maps, yielding explicit affine charts for resolved brane webs; incorporates physical input from 7-brane monodromy and Hanany–Witten moves.

Result: Constructs explicit affine atlases for generalized toric CY3s associated with opened/resolved five-brane webs on 7-branes; identifies deformation parameters with physical moduli (e.g., positions, couplings), and derives their transformation laws under Hanany–Witten transitions and tessellation refinements.

Conclusion: Scattering diagrams and theta functions provide a robust, computable framework for non-toric Calabi–Yau geometries in string dualities, unifying mathematical mirror symmetry techniques with physical brane dynamics.

Abstract: We present an explicit and practical description of Generalized Toric Calabi--Yau threefolds, which are associated with five-brane webs ending on seven-branes, using scattering diagrams and broken lines, based on ideas from arXiv:2410.04714 [hep-th]. Local theta-function algebras and their transition maps give explicit affine charts for opened (resolved) webs, extending familiar toric constructions to non-toric situations. We give a physical interpretation of the deformation parameters, in the case of one or several 7-branes, and deduce behavior under Hanany-Witten transitions and changes of tesselation.

</details>


### [7] [An obstruction to generalised discrete torsion in string theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00209)
*Carlo Angelantonj,Giorgio Leone,Diego Perugini*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates that generalized discrete torsion—assigning distinct discrete torsion phases to different fixed points within a twisted sector—is inconsistent in string theory, as it violates genus-two modular invariance despite satisfying genus-one modular invariance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the theoretical viability of generalized discrete torsion, i.e., assigning independent discrete torsion phases to distinct fixed points in a given twisted sector of orbifold compactifications.

Method: Explicit computation and analysis of genus-one and genus-two vacuum amplitudes for the $T^6/\mathbb{Z}_2^2$ and $T^7/\mathbb{Z}_2^3$ orbifold models; consistency checked via modular invariance and physical interpretation of the spectrum.

Result: Generalized discrete torsion fails at genus two: although genus-one amplitudes remain modular invariant, the spectrum interpretation becomes inconsistent, breaking genus-two modular invariance.

Conclusion: Generalized discrete torsion is ruled out in string theory; this aligns with recent mathematical constraints from equivariant cohomology classifying allowed topological deformations.

Abstract: We reconsider the possibility of turning on generalised discrete torsion in orbifold compactifications, whereby different discrete torsion phases are attached to different fixed points of a given twisted sector. By an explicit inspection of the genus one and genus two vacuum amplitudes for the $T^6/\mathbb{Z}_2^2$ and $T^7/\mathbb{Z}_2^3$ cases, we show that this is not possible in String Theory. In fact, this generalised discrete torsion, although modular invariant at genus one, leads to an inconsistent interpretation of the spectrum which, in turn, implies a loss of modular invariance on genus-two Riemann surfaces. Our finding is in agreement with a recent classification of allowed topological deformations in equivariant cohomology, which does not include this possibility.

</details>


### [8] [The pinning-field line defect at finite temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00210)
*Julien Barrat,Simone Giombi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the pinning-field line defect in the critical O(N) model at finite temperature using OPE, analyticity, and thermal periodicity; it computes thermal correlators and one-point coefficients in ε-expansion, provides a Padé estimate for the 3D Ising model, conjectures accuracy of truncated periodic OPE, validates it numerically at large N, and extends to finite spatial volume with antipodal defects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand static symmetry-breaking impurities (line defects) at quantum critical points in finite-temperature critical O(N) models, bridging non-perturbative thermal CFT techniques with defect physics.

Method: Combines operator product expansion (OPE) with thermal periodicity and analyticity to construct a manifestly periodic expansion; employs ε-expansion and Padé resummation; performs large-N saddle-point numerics; extends to spherical geometry with antipodal defects.

Result: Derives thermal correlators and defect one-point coefficients in ε-expansion; obtains Padé estimate ⟨ϕ̂⟩ ∼ 1.701/β^{Δ̂_ϕ̂} for 3D Ising; confirms high accuracy of periodic OPE truncated to identity + lowest singlet via large-N numerics; computes tilt and longitudinal singlet two-point functions in d=3 with excellent agreement; generalizes framework to finite-volume sphere.

Conclusion: The periodic OPE provides an efficient, accurate, and systematically improvable framework for thermal defect CFTs; truncation to few low-lying operators suffices for quantitative predictions; the approach unifies perturbative, large-N, and geometric methods across dimensions.

Abstract: We study the pinning-field line defect in the critical $\mathrm{O}(N)$ model at finite temperature, describing a static symmetry-breaking impurity at a quantum critical point. Using the operator product expansion (OPE), analyticity, and thermal periodicity, we construct a manifestly periodic expansion relating heavy-operator asymptotics to low-lying thermal OPE data. We determine thermal correlators and one-point coefficients in the $\varepsilon$-expansion and, combining these results with the two-dimensional Ising endpoint, obtain the Padé estimate $\langle \hat{\phi} \rangle \sim 1.701/\beta^{\smash{\hat{\Delta}_{\smash{\hat{\phi}}}}}$ for the lowest defect operator in the three-dimensional Ising model. We conjecture that truncating the periodic OPE to a few low-lying operators accurately approximates defect correlators. To test this, we numerically solve the large-$N$ thermal saddle and extract defect one-point coefficients across $2<d<4$. In three dimensions, we compute the tilt and longitudinal singlet two-point functions, finding very good agreement with the periodic OPE truncated to the identity and lowest singlet. Finally, we extend the analysis to finite spatial volume using antipodal defects on the sphere.

</details>


### [9] [A local action for Kodaira--Spencer gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00213)
*Olaf Hohm*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper formulates Kodaira--Spencer gravity via a local action using a (1,1)-form as the gravity field, yielding an unconstrained double field theory with $L_\infty$ gauge algebra rooted in a Courant algebroid; the free limit matches Fierz--Pauli + Kalb--Ramond on a 3D slice, and the full theory admits a cyclic $L_\infty$ structure with a double copy interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a local, unconstrained formulation of Kodaira--Spencer gravity that unifies geometric moduli deformations with double field theory structures, bypassing level-matching constraints while preserving gauge invariance and connecting to known low-energy gravitational actions.

Method: Solving the integrability constraint on complex structure deformations of a Calabi--Yau threefold in terms of a (1,1) form; deriving the action and gauge transformations from a Courant algebroid-based $L_\infty$ algebra; analyzing linearized and non-linear limits, including parity-odd three-derivative interactions and reduction to 3D real slice.

Result: A local, gauge-invariant action for Kodaira--Spencer gravity with a (1,1) form as dynamical field; emergence of double field theory gauge transformations and algebra without level-matching; free limit reproducing Fierz--Pauli + Kalb--Ramond actions; full theory encoded in a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra admitting a double copy interpretation.

Conclusion: Kodaira--Spencer gravity can be recast as an unconstrained, geometrically grounded double field theory governed by a cyclic $L_\infty$ algebra derived from a Courant algebroid, providing a unified framework linking complex geometry, stringy gravity, and double field theory with concrete low-energy and structural implications.

Abstract: A local action is given for Kodaira--Spencer gravity by solving a constraint on the complex structure deformation of a Calabi--Yau threefold in terms of a $(1,1)$ form as the new gravity field. The gauge algebra becomes a genuine $L_{\infty}$ algebra based on a Courant algebroid, while the gravity field and its linearized gauge transformations coincide with those of double field theory. On a real three-dimensional slice, the free action reduces to the Fierz--Pauli action for massless spin-2 plus the Kalb--Ramond action for a B-field. Non-linearly, the theory carries parity-odd interactions with three derivatives, and it is gauge invariant without a level-matching constraint, in this sense defining an unconstrained double field theory. The full theory is encoded in a cyclic $L_{\infty}$ algebra, for which a double copy interpretation is given.

</details>


### [10] [Reconstructing string dualities through symmetries of moduli spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00235)
*Ignacio Ruiz,Germán Varona*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes duality descriptions in quantum gravity moduli spaces using symmetry and the Emergent String Conjecture, matching known top-down string/M-theory compactifications for high-supersymmetry cases (32/16 supercharges), and constraining — but not fully determining — dualities for lower-supersymmetry theories, suggesting possible new corners of the string landscape.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically identify and constrain possible dual descriptions of quantum gravity at infinite-distance limits in moduli space, guided by scalar manifold symmetries and the Emergent String Conjecture.

Method: Bottom-up analysis of asymptotic regions of quantum gravity moduli spaces, leveraging isometries of scalar manifolds and the Emergent String Conjecture to classify light state towers and associated dual theories.

Result: For 32- and 16-supercharge theories, duality frames are uniquely fixed and match toroidal compactifications of M/II and heterotic strings; for <16 supercharges, symmetries weaken and quantum corrections restrict moduli space access, yet duality arrangements remain partially constrained — with some novel, previously unexplored possibilities emerging.

Conclusion: Symmetry and the Emergent String Conjecture provide powerful bottom-up constraints on duality structure across quantum gravity moduli spaces; while high-supersymmetry cases are fully reconstructed, lower-supersymmetry regimes admit constrained but not unique dualities — hinting at undiscovered regions of the string landscape.

Abstract: We perform a bottom-up analysis of the possible duality descriptions characterizing the perturbative regimes associated to the asymptotic regions of quantum gravity moduli spaces. Our approach relies on the symmetries of such moduli spaces as scalar manifolds, as well as the Emergent String Conjecture, which constrains the possible towers of states becoming light, and thus the interpretation of the dual theories emerging at infinite distance. For supergravity theories with 32 and 16 supercharges, our reconstruction uniquely matches those duality frames associated to top-down toroidal compactifications of M-theory/Type II string theory and heterotic string theory, respectively. For theories with less supersymmetry, moduli spaces exhibit fewer symmetries, and quantum corrections can render certain directions of the classical moduli space inaccessible; nonetheless, we are still able to constrain the possible arrangements of dualities. While these are mostly realized by known top-down constructions, there are some intriguing additional possibilities which might lay in unexplored corners of the string landscape.

</details>


### [11] [5d SCFTs and Intersection Theory of Generalized Toric Threefolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00366)
*Hülya Argüz,Antoine Bourget,Pierrick Bousseau,Julius F. Grimminger,Sakura Schafer-Nameki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a combinatorial algorithm to compute triple intersection numbers—and thus the cubic prepotential—for 5D superconformal field theories realized via M-theory on generalized toric Calabi-Yau threefolds with 7-branes, encoded in generalized toric polygons (GTPs). It introduces 'exuberant divisors'—non-toric, non-compact divisors represented by extra lines in the GTP—and leverages local divisor analysis and associativity of intersection products to determine the full prepotential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the existing toric-combinatorial method for computing triple intersection numbers (and hence the prepotential) from toric polygons to generalized toric polygons (GTPs), which encode 5-brane webs with 7-branes and correspond to generally non-toric Calabi-Yau threefolds lacking a known intersection number prescription.

Method: Introduces exuberant divisors—non-toric, non-compact divisors associated with additional lines in the GTP—and performs a local geometric analysis of their intersections; combines this with the associativity constraint of the triple intersection product to derive a fully combinatorial, algorithmic procedure for computing all triple intersection numbers directly from GTP data.

Result: A complete, explicit combinatorial algorithm for computing the cubic prepotential of 5D SCFTs from GTPs, validated on examples and implemented as a publicly available Mathematica code.

Conclusion: The prepotential—and thus the low-energy effective theory on the extended Coulomb branch—of 5D SCFTs with 7-branes is fully determined by the combinatorial structure of the GTP, including exuberant divisors; this bridges a key gap between geometry, combinatorics, and quantum field theory in five dimensions.

Abstract: Five-dimensional superconformal field theories are intrinsically strongly coupled fixed points that can be realized as M-theory on local Calabi-Yau threefolds with canonical singularities. The low-energy effective theory on their extended Coulomb branch is governed by a cubic prepotential, determined by the triple intersection numbers of divisors in the resolved geometry. For toric threefolds, dual to 5-brane webs, these numbers are computed combinatorially from the toric polygon. However, no such description has been available for generalized toric polygons (GTPs), dual to 5-brane webs with 7-branes, since the corresponding generalized toric Calabi-Yau threefolds are generally non-toric. We show that the 7-brane data are encoded by additional lines in the GTP, which correspond to non-toric, non-compact divisors that we call exuberant divisors. Building on a local analysis of these divisors and on the associativity of the intersection product, we develop an algorithm that computes the triple intersection numbers, and hence the prepotential, directly from the combinatorial data of the GTP. We illustrate it in examples and provide a Mathematica code implementation of the intersection algorithm.

</details>


### [12] [Doubly-periodic instanton via the ADHM method and the self-dual monopole-center vortex chain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00639)
*Yui Hayashi,Yuya Tanizaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes a duality between doubly-periodic Yang-Mills instantons on ℝ²×T² and a 2D Abelian Hitchin system with impurities, and identifies an alternating chain of BPS and Kaluza–Klein monopoles in the far-field limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the caloron–monopole decomposition paradigm—where calorons on ℝ³×S¹ decompose into fundamental monopoles—to doubly-periodic instantons on ℝ²×T², seeking a tractable dual description and constituent structure.

Method: Starts from the ADHM construction of instantons; derives the 2D Abelian Hitchin system with delta-function impurities as a dual description; reconstructs the instanton via the Green’s function of that system under a scaling ansatz for UV divergences; analyzes the far-field limit with strongly anisotropic torus geometry and 't Hooft twisting.

Result: Derives the 2D Abelian Hitchin system with impurities as the dual description; provides a reconstruction prescription linking its Green’s function to the instanton field; identifies, in the anisotropic far-field limit, an alternating chain of BPS and KK monopoles—'monopole-center vortices'—arising from the non-Abelian self-dual field under 't Hooft twist that effectively fragments the torus into 1/N sectors.

Conclusion: Doubly-periodic instantons admit a monopole-based constituent picture analogous to calorons, realized via a dual impurity-decorated Hitchin system; the 't Hooft twist induces topological fragmentation and governs the alternating monopole-vortex chain structure.

Abstract: Calorons, i.e., instantons on $\mathbb{R}^3\times S^1$, with nontrivial asymptotic holonomy decompose into $N$ fundamental monopole constituents. This suggests that a doubly-periodic Yang--Mills instanton on $\mathbb{R}^2\times T^2$ may admit a description in terms of a periodic array of monopole constituents. Starting from the Atiyah--Drinfeld--Hitchin--Manin (ADHM) construction, we derive the $2$d Abelian Hitchin system with impurities as a dual description of the doubly-periodic instanton. We then give a prescription for reconstructing the instanton from the Green's function of this system, assuming a scaling ansatz for the ultraviolet divergences induced by the delta-function impurities. In the far-field limit with strongly anisotropic torus periods, we extend the analysis of Bruckmann and van Baal for multi-calorons and identify an alternating chain of BPS and Kaluza--Klein (KK) monopole constituents: The monopole-center vortex chain arises from the non-Abelian self-dual field especially when an 't Hooft twist cuts the torus into the $1/N$ fragment.

</details>


### [13] [A note on the quantization of M2-branes on local Calabi-Yau manifolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00687)
*Yutong Huang,Qinjian Lou,Jiahua Tian,Yi-Nan Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes BPS states from M2-branes wrapping 2-cycles in local Calabi-Yau manifolds via brane quantization, systematically interpreting refined Gopakumar-Vafa invariants for both CY3s (via 1d N=4 sigma models) and CY4s (via N=2B quantum mechanics), with applications to 5d N=1 SCFTs and gauge theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide physical, systematic interpretations of refined Gopakumar-Vafa invariants—especially for curve classes with fixed or jumping geometric genus—and to link BPS state counting in M-theory to geometry and field theory data of local Calabi-Yau threefolds and fourfolds.

Method: Brane quantization of M2-branes wrapping holomorphic 2-cycles; analysis of supersymmetric quantum mechanics (1d N=4 non-linear sigma model for CY3s, N=2B for CY4s); case studies including generalized conifolds, local CY3s engineering 5d N=1 gauge theories, and singularities with crepant resolutions containing singular divisors.

Result: Refined GV invariants are physically interpreted for fixed-genus and genus-jumping curve classes; BPS states of 5d N=1 SCFTs are geometrically realized; refined GV invariants are explicitly computed for generalized conifolds, local CY3 gauge theory geometries, and a singular-divisor-containing resolution; framework established for M2-branes on genus-0 curves in CY4s.

Conclusion: Brane quantization provides a unified physical framework for refined GV invariants across dimensions, bridging enumerative geometry, M-theory BPS spectra, and 5d superconformal/gauge theories—with extensions to CY4s opening new avenues for higher-dimensional BPS state counting.

Abstract: In this note we present a detailed computation of the BPS states from M2-branes wrapping 2-cycles in local Calabi-Yau manifolds, at the level of brane quantization. For the cases of local Calabi-Yau threefolds, the supersymmetric quantum mechanics is the 1d $\mathcal{N}=4$ non-linear sigma model. We achieve systematic, physical interpretations of refined Gopakumar-Vafa (GV) invariants, first for the curve classes with fixed geometric genus, and then for curves whose geometric genus jumps. The BPS states of 5d $\mathcal{N}=1$ superconformal field theories are interpreted from the local geometries, which are presented in a number of case studies. In particular, we compute the refined GV invariants for generalized conifolds, local CY3 giving 5d $\mathcal{N}=1$ gauge theories and a singularity whose crepant resolution contains a singular divisor. For the cases of local Calabi-Yau fourfolds, we point out the supersymmetric quantum mechanics is of type $\mathcal{N}=2B$. We set up the framework for studying M2-branes wrapping 2-cycles, and discuss a few cases of M2-brane wrapping genus-0 curves with this new approach.

</details>


### [14] [Quantum Black Holes in Non-Linear Electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00808)
*Jana Menšíková,David Kubiznak,Robert B. Mann*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs novel charged quantum black holes in Einstein gravity coupled to Regge-Maxwell (RegMax) nonlinear electrodynamics using AdS/CFT and braneworld holography, and analyzes their thermodynamics and horizon structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the semi-classical gravity problem where quantum matter back-reacts on classical spacetime geometry, leveraging holographic tools.

Method: Application of AdS/CFT correspondence and braneworld holography to Einstein gravity coupled to RegMax nonlinear electrodynamics.

Result: Construction of novel charged quantum black hole solutions; derivation and analysis of their thermodynamic properties and horizon structure.

Conclusion: Holographic techniques successfully enable the construction and thermodynamic characterization of charged quantum black holes in a nonlinear electrodynamics-gravity system.

Abstract: Techniques of AdS/CFT correspondence and braneworld holography have proven to be a useful tool for solving the problem of semi-classical gravity where quantum matter provides the back-reaction to classical spacetime geometry. In this paper, we apply this approach to Einstein gravity coupled to RegMax nonlinear electrodynamics and construct novel type of charged quantum black holes. We then discuss their thermodynamics and horizon structure.

</details>


### [15] [Chiral magnetic wave and axial charge relaxation in flavor branes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01671)
*Masataka Matsumoto,Kensei Tanaka*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the chiral magnetic wave and axial charge relaxation in the chiral symmetry broken phase of the holographic D3/D7 model with massless quarks, finding that the phenomenological chiral magnetic wave description remains valid in the quasi-hydrodynamic regime, with axial relaxation rate exhibiting temperature-dependent monotonic/non-monotonic dependence on vector charge density and chiral condensate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether the phenomenological chiral magnetic wave (CMW) framework applies beyond the chiral symmetric phase—specifically in the chiral symmetry broken phase—and to quantify how axial charge relaxation, mediated by transfer from flavor to adjoint sector, depends on physical parameters like vector charge density and chiral condensate.

Method: Holographic analysis of the D3/D7 brane model in the probe limit; computation of quasi-normal modes (QNMs) in the chiral symmetry broken phase; comparison of dispersion relations with the phenomenological CMW expression; parametric study of axial relaxation rate versus vector charge density and chiral condensate across temperatures.

Result: The CMW dispersion relation accurately describes QNMs when axial relaxation is slow; the axial relaxation rate increases monotonically with vector charge density and decreases with chiral condensate at high T, but exhibits non-monotonic behavior at low T.

Conclusion: The phenomenological chiral magnetic wave description extends to the chiral symmetry broken phase within the quasi-hydrodynamic regime, validating its use in strongly coupled systems with spontaneous chiral symmetry breaking, albeit with nontrivial temperature-dependent relaxation dynamics.

Abstract: We investigate the chiral magnetic wave and axial charge relaxation in the chiral symmetry broken phase of the holographic D3/D7 model with massless quarks, where the axial charge in the flavor sector can relax through transfer to the adjoint sector in the probe limit. We find that the dispersion relations of the quasi-normal modes are well described by the phenomenological expression for the chiral magnetic wave when the relaxation rate is small. We then determine how this rate depends on vector charge density and the chiral condensate. At higher temperatures, it increases with charge density and decreases with increasing condensate, while these dependences become non-monotonic at lower temperatures. These results show that the phenomenological description of the chiral magnetic wave also applies in the chiral symmetry broken phase in the quasi-hydrodynamic regime.

</details>


### [16] [Quasinormal Modes Criticality across Black Hole Extremality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01869)
*Zi-Yu Hou,Yu-Qi Lei,Xian-Hui Ge,Bum-Hoon Lee,Li-Jun Tian*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies critical behavior in Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg AdS black holes near a threshold λ_c where extremal black holes vanish; it finds linear scaling of minimum temperature and quasinormal mode damping rates, with zero-damped modes exhibiting near-horizon AdS₂ spectral signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how thermodynamic and dynamical criticality persist when extremal black hole solutions disappear — specifically, whether near-horizon AdS₂ physics governs the transition from extremal to nonextremal branches in nonlinearly charged AdS black holes.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of black hole thermodynamics and quasinormal modes (QNMs) of massless scalar perturbations along the lowest-temperature branch; extraction of critical exponents α_T and α_n via scaling fits near λ_c; comparison of QNM localization and damping with near-horizon AdS₂ predictions.

Result: A critical threshold λ_c exists beyond which extremal black holes vanish; T_min ∝ (λ − λ_c)^α_T with α_T ≈ 1; two QNM branches coalesce on the imaginary axis and yield purely imaginary modes whose decay rates Γ_n ∝ (λ − λ_c)^α_n with α_n ≈ α_T; zero-damped modes localize near the horizon and obey AdS₂ overtone spacing.

Conclusion: Thermodynamic and dynamical criticality remain tightly coupled even without extremal solutions, confirming the universality of near-horizon AdS₂ structure in governing critical black hole physics.

Abstract: We investigate the extremal solution branch to nonextremal branch transition in Einstein-Euler-Heisenberg anti-de Sitter black holes, where the nonlinear electromagnetic parameter $\lambda$ governs the existence of the extremal branch solution. At fixed pressure and charge, extremal black hole only exist for $\lambda\leq\lambda_c$. For $\lambda>\lambda_c$, the lowest Hawking temperature keeps nonzero. Near this threshold, the lowest temperature follows a linear critical scaling, $T_{\min}\propto(\lambda-\lambda_c)^{\alpha_T}$. We investigate the corresponding dynamical behavior by computing the quasinormal modes of a massless scalar perturbation along the lowest-temperature black hole branch. As the critical threshold is approached, two complex-frequency quasinormal modes branches collide on the imaginary axis and reorganize into distinct purely imaginary branches. Then the imaginary parts of these modes, together with higher overtones, reach zero at the critical threshold, described by $\Gamma_n\propto(\lambda-\lambda_c)^{\alpha_n}$, with $\alpha_n\simeq\alpha_T$. The resulting zero-damped modes become increasingly localized near the event horizon, and their relative damping rates closely follow the overtone spacing predicted by the near-horizon AdS$_2$ spectrum. Our results show that the thermodynamic and dynamical critical behavior remain closely connected even when extremal black hole solutions cease to exist, providing further evidence for the relevance of near-extremal spectral behavior in this distinct critical regime.

</details>


### [17] [An Amplitudes Approach to Spinning Black Hole Scattering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02272)
*Dogan Akpinar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This thesis advances classical black hole scattering theory by incorporating spin effects systematically using scattering amplitudes, resolving classical-quantum ambiguities via a spin interpolation method, and deriving gauge-invariant observables (e.g., impulses) up to quartic order in spin at 3PM order; it identifies spin-shift symmetry and proves asymptotic integrability and radial action uniqueness under that symmetry and aligned-spin input.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational wave detection demands precise modeling of compact binary dynamics, especially including spin—a key feature of astrophysical Kerr black holes—requiring a robust classical framework beyond standard PN/PM approximations.

Method: Models Kerr black holes as massive spinning point particles in finite-spin representations; computes classical scattering amplitudes perturbatively in the post-Minkowskian (PM) expansion; resolves classical-quantum mixing ambiguity via spin interpolation; extracts gauge-invariant radial action; computes observables (linear/angular impulses) using covariant Dirac brackets; analyzes symmetries (spin-shift invariance) and integrability.

Result: Obtains the first classical scattering amplitude for Kerr black holes through quartic order in spin at third post-Minkowskian (3PM) order; derives the corresponding gauge-invariant radial action; computes non-aligned-spin observables; proves asymptotic integrability of probe black hole dynamics up to quartic spin; establishes uniqueness of the quartic-in-spin radial action in a Kerr background given aligned-spin data, asymptotic integrability, and spin-shift symmetry.

Conclusion: Scattering amplitudes, augmented with spin interpolation and symmetry analysis, provide a consistent, systematic, and predictive framework for classical spinning black hole dynamics at high PM orders; spin-shift symmetry is fundamental and constrains dynamics strongly, enabling reconstruction of generic spin effects from simpler aligned configurations.

Abstract: The detection of gravitational waves has opened a new era of precision studies of gravity, demanding advances in our understanding of compact binary dynamics. Scattering amplitudes provide a powerful framework for studying these systems using methods from high-energy physics. In this thesis, we study classical black hole scattering, systematically including spin---an important feature of real astrophysical systems---to describe Kerr black hole dynamics. We model Kerr black holes as massive point particles in finite-spin representations and compute their scattering dynamics perturbatively. We resolve an ambiguity mixing classical and quantum contributions using our spin interpolation method, obtaining a classical amplitude for Kerr black hole scattering through quartic order in spin at third post-Minkowskian order. From this amplitude, we extract the gauge-invariant radial action, enabling the computation of classical non-aligned-spin observables---such as the linear and angular impulses---using covariant Dirac brackets. The amplitude exhibits spin-shift symmetry, remaining invariant under a shift of the black hole spin by the momentum transfer. Motivated by this property, we study the conserved quantities governing the scattering process and show that the dynamics of a probe black hole remains integrable at asymptotic infinity through quartic order in probe spin. Finally, we show that the quartic-in-spin radial action for a probe black hole in a Kerr background, at any order in perturbation theory, is completely fixed by the aligned-spin dynamics, provided that it satisfies asymptotic integrability and spin-shift symmetry.

</details>


### [18] [Summing over Seifert Manifolds in Narain Ensemble Averages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02515)
*Kei Kaneda,Masahito Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the ensemble average of Narain CFTs over moduli space—previously linked to lens spaces and Abelian Chern-Simons theory—to Seifert manifolds, interpreting the average as a restricted sum over topologies relevant to 3D quantum gravity; it yields convergent, polynomial expressions in vector-valued Eisenstein series, identifies wormhole contributions (including those with exceptional fibers), and draws parallels to 2D topological gravity via fibration reduction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the known correspondence between ensemble averages of Narain CFTs and topological invariants of lens spaces to broader 3-manifold classes—specifically Seifert manifolds—as a step toward understanding topology summation in 3D quantum gravity.

Method: The authors compute the ensemble average of Narain CFT partition functions over moduli space for boundary tori of a Seifert manifold's core (punctured surface × S¹); they glue solid tori with marked presentations (fixed base genus, fiber count, Seifert invariant), interpret the average as a sum over such gluings, analyze convergence and modular structure (vector-valued Eisenstein series), identify wormhole contributions via rational Lagrangian sublattices in Siegel-Eisenstein expansions, and reduce along the S¹ fiber to compare with Marolf–Maxfield 2D topological gravity.

Result: The ensemble average over Seifert manifold gluings converges absolutely and yields a polynomial in components of a vector-valued real-analytic Eisenstein series; wormholes appear as connected corrections when boundary tori share a modulus, and rational Lagrangian sublattices of the Siegel-Eisenstein series realize wormholes containing exceptional fibers; fiber reduction recovers structural parallels to 2D topological gravity, with the base surface playing the role of spacetime.

Conclusion: Modularity—manifested through Eisenstein series and their generalizations—serves as an organizing principle for summing over geometries (topologies) in three-dimensional quantum gravity, with Seifert manifolds providing a concrete, computable setting where wormholes and topological restrictions emerge naturally from CFT ensemble averaging.

Abstract: The ensemble average of Narain conformal field theories over their moduli space is a real-analytic Eisenstein series, whose Poincaré expansion can be interpreted as a sum over lens spaces weighted by the partition functions of Abelian Chern-Simons theory. In this paper we extend this correspondence from lens spaces to Seifert manifolds, which gives an example of a restricted sum over topologies of the kind that arises in three-dimensional quantum gravity. A Seifert manifold is obtained from a fixed core, a punctured surface times a circle, by gluing solid tori to its boundary tori, and we place a copy of the Narain theory on each of these tori. The ensemble average supplies the sum over the solid tori, or more precisely over marked presentations with fixed base genus, number of fibers and integer Seifert invariant. The resulting sum converges absolutely and is a polynomial in the components of the vector-valued Eisenstein series. When all the boundary tori share a single modulus, the average acquires connected corrections, which we identify with wormholes gluing the core to itself; for two boundary tori, the terms with rational Lagrangian sublattices of the Siegel-Eisenstein series are realized by wormholes containing an exceptional fiber. After reduction along the fiber, the construction closely parallels the two-dimensional topological gravity of Marolf and Maxfield, with the base of the fibration playing the role of their spacetime. We also discuss Dehn fillings of 3-manifolds with torus boundary, non-orientable bases, and the surgery formula. Our results suggest that modularity could be an organizing principle in the summation of geometries in quantum gravity.

</details>


### [19] [No smooth real instanton saddles in self-dual Yang-Mills on AdS$_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02655)
*Bayram Tekin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the self-dual limit of Yang-Mills theory on Euclidean AdS₄, showing that only the vacuum is a real, smooth saddle point under the first-order boundary equation; previously proposed self-dual solutions are ruled out as saddles, and their charges are corrected by including a conformal boundary surface term.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the boundary conditions and saddle-point structure of self-dual Yang-Mills theory on Euclidean AdS₄, particularly clarifying whether non-vacuum self-dual configurations qualify as valid saddles and how conserved charges should be defined in this setting.

Method: Analysis of boundary conditions for first- and second-order formulations of Yang-Mills action in Euclidean AdS₄; examination of smoothness and reality conditions for gauge fields up to the conformal boundary; explicit evaluation and correction of conserved charges using boundary surface terms.

Result: The vacuum is the unique real, smooth saddle point satisfying the non-trivial first-order boundary equation; the Sarioglu–Tekin (2009) self-dual solutions fail to satisfy this condition and thus are not saddles; their computed charges are incomplete without inclusion of a conformal boundary surface term.

Conclusion: The self-dual limit enforces triviality of real, smooth saddles in Euclidean AdS₄ Yang-Mills theory; consistent charge definitions require careful treatment of boundary contributions; complex or singular configurations remain unexplored.

Abstract: In the self-dual limit of Yang-Mills theory on Euclidean AdS$_4$ studied by Heuveline, Ruzziconi, Sheta and Strominger, the boundary condition of the second-order action becomes vacuous, but the boundary equation of the first-order action is non-trivial. We show that, with this equation, the vacuum is the only saddle with a real gauge field when both fields are smooth up to the conformal boundary, for any compact gauge group. In particular, the self-dual solutions of Sarioglu and Tekin (2009) are not saddles; we also correct their charges, which omitted a surface term at the conformal boundary. Complex saddles and singular limits remain open.

</details>


### [20] [General Form Factors from String Amplitudes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02730)
*Qu Cao,Liang Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Extends string-amplitude methods to multi-insertion form factors, relates them to scattering amplitudes via boundary deformation, reveals novel finite-α′ structures (2-splits, hidden/bulk zeros, decoupling splits), and identifies contact-term contributions in the field-theory limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize string-theoretic form factor constructions beyond single operator insertions and uncover structural analogies/differences with scattering amplitudes.

Method: Deforms bulk closed-string punctures onto the open-string boundary to relate multi-insertion stringy form factors to scattering amplitudes; analyzes field-theory limit and finite-α′ behavior.

Result: Field-theory form factors contain both separate insertion and local contact-term contributions; finite-α′ stringy form factors exhibit 2-splits, hidden zeros (like amplitudes), bulk zeros (form-factor-specific), and a decoupling split (unique to multiple insertions).

Conclusion: Multi-insertion stringy form factors possess rich, previously unobserved structures at finite α′, bridging scattering amplitude theory and form factor physics while highlighting intrinsic differences due to operator locality and off-shell kinematics.

Abstract: We extend the string-amplitude construction of single-insertion form factors to multiple operator insertions carrying independent off-shell momenta. By deforming the bulk closed-string punctures onto the open-string boundary, we establish a relation between stringy general form factors and scattering amplitudes. Using this relation, we evaluate the field-theory limit and find that the resulting form factors receive contributions from both separate operator insertions and local contact terms. Furthermore, we uncover several hidden structures of stringy general form factors at finite $\alpha'$: $2$-splits and hidden zeros analogous to those of scattering amplitudes, bulk zeros specific to form factors, and a decoupling split unique to multiple insertions.

</details>


### [21] [A Matrix View of Holographic Null-State Equations in $d\geq 2$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02855)
*Kuo-Wei Huang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reformulates AdS/CFT null-state PDEs using structured finite-dimensional matrices, yielding infinite towers of CFT differential equations; it recovers known 2D Virasoro results at large c via sl2 algebra and extends to richer 4D structures involving sl2 generator products, with associated recursion and factorization properties.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically organize and generalize the recently derived null-state-type PDEs from AdS gravity with a black hole, enabling efficient derivation of CFT differential constraints across dimensions and uncovering underlying algebraic structure.

Method: Matrix representation of null-state PDEs using finite-dimensional, systematically off-diagonal matrices; algebraic analysis in terms of sl2 generators and their products; dimensional comparison (d=2 vs d=4); study of recursion relations and factorized forms.

Result: A unified matrix framework generating infinite-level CFT differential equations; explicit d=2 matrix reproducing large-c Virasoro null-state results with sl2 + identity decomposition; d=4 matrix exhibiting more complex structure with quadratic sl2 products; identification of recursion and factorization patterns in holographic PDEs.

Conclusion: The matrix formalism reveals deep algebraic structure in holographic null-state equations—spanning dimensions and central charge dependence—and provides a powerful, systematic tool for deriving and classifying CFT differential constraints from bulk gravity.

Abstract: We show that the null-state-type partial differential equations recently derived from AdS gravity with a black hole can be efficiently organized using finite-dimensional matrices with systematic off-diagonal structures. This matrix formulation generates an infinite tower of CFT differential equations at arbitrary levels. The $d=2$ matrix reproduces a known result based on Virasoro null-state vectors at large central charge and admits an algebraic representation in terms of a linear combination of the identity matrix and $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ generators, with a heavy-state deformation encoding the central charge contribution. The $d=4$ governing matrix exhibits a similar yet richer structure involving products of $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ generators. We also discuss recursion relations and factorized product structures associated with these holographic differential equations.

</details>


### [22] [The generalized entropy in holographic semiclassical de Sitter spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02898)
*Akihiro Ishibashi,Kengo Maeda,Takashi Okamura*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a quantum-corrected de Sitter spacetime within holographic semiclassical gravity, unifying entanglement and Wald entropies; it shows finiteness of renormalized entropy without boundary cutoffs and vanishing first-order variation of generalized entropy under dynamical conformal boundary metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the divergence and regularization ambiguities in de Sitter entropy by unifying entanglement and gravitational contributions in a holographic semiclassical framework, and to examine consistency of entropy extremization under boundary dynamics.

Method: Perturbative construction of quantum-corrected de Sitter spacetime via static deformations of the classical solution; application of the Noether charge formula to compute renormalized generalized entropy in an AdS bulk where Dirichlet boundary conditions are relaxed.

Result: Renormalized entanglement and Wald entropies remain finite without UV cutoffs; the first-order variation of generalized entropy with respect to the de Sitter horizon vanishes when the conformal boundary metric is treated as dynamical.

Conclusion: Generalized de Sitter entropy is well-defined and extremal in the semiclassical holographic setup, supporting a thermodynamic interpretation of the de Sitter horizon even without strict AdS boundary conditions.

Abstract: We consider generalized de Sitter entropy which includes both entanglement entropy of the strongly coupled quantum field and the gravitational Wald entropy in the framework of holographic semiclassical gravity. The quantum corrected de Sitter spacetime is constructed perturbatively by considering static deformations of the classical solution. By applying the Noether charge formula to our semiclassical model, we show that both the renormalized entropies remain finite without introducing a cutoff, even when the bulk AdS gravitational field does not satisfy the Dirichlet boundary condition. We show that the first order variation of the generalized entropy with respect to the de Sitter horizon always vanishes when the conformal boundary metric is promoted to a dynamical variable.

</details>


### [23] [Transgression branes in seven dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03031)
*Pablo Mora*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies a 7D brane theory based on a Transgression Action, revealing connections to fractional-statistics models and black hole solutions, with analysis of Euclidean/Lorentzian static solutions and topological quantum statistical implications.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the physical implications of a seven-dimensional brane theory constructed via a Transgression Action—particularly its structural similarities to known models of particles with fractional statistics and higher-dimensional generalizations thereof.

Method: Analytical construction and comparison of static Euclidean and Lorentzian solutions using transgression forms; factorization of the trace in the transgression form is central; solutions are benchmarked against Nepomechie–Wu–Zee models and Bañados-type constant-curvature black holes (as extended by Guilleminot–Olea–Petrov).

Result: Demonstrates that the transgression-based 7D brane theory exhibits structural analogies to fractional-statistics models; constructs explicit Euclidean and Lorentzian static solutions; identifies topological phases linked to quantum statistics.

Conclusion: The transgression action framework yields a geometrically rich 7D brane theory whose topological structure encodes quantum statistical behavior, bridging higher-dimensional gravity, brane physics, and topological quantum phases.

Abstract: A seven dimensional theory with branes, described by a Transgression Action is investigated. The theory has special properties owing to the factorization of the trace used to define the Transgression form. It is shown that in some cases the theory is similar to the models of branes proposed by Nepomechie, Wu and Zee as a higher dimensional generalization of models of particles with fractional statistics of Wilczek, Wu and Zee. Static Euclidean solutions are presented, and compared with similar solutions of Nepomechie,Wu and Zee. Then Lorentzian static solutions are considered, based in solutions of constant curvature black holes presented by Bañados and further studied by Guilleminot, Olea and Petrov. Topological phases and their implications for quantum statistics are discussed.

</details>


### [24] [Higher Groupoids of Gauge Potentials in Higher Gauge Theories admitting Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03064)
*Grigorios Giotopoulos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs an explicit small ∞-groupoid model for null-concordances of higher gauge curvatures by algorithmically trivializing nonlinear Bianchi identities, proving equivalence to the full ∞-groupoid via a total-form intermediate model, and relating homotopy groups to cohomology of a weighted gauge complex.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide an explicit, computationally tractable ∞-groupoid model for null-concordances of higher gauge curvatures—objects central to higher gauge theory and mathematical physics—by resolving the complexity of nonlinear Bianchi identities.

Method: Constructs a minimal Sullivan-type ∞-groupoid from a finite-type weighted minimal Bianchi algebra; organizes weighted potentials and gauge parameters into a linear-gauge-transformation ∞-groupoid; establishes natural equivalence via an intermediate total-form model with explicit projection, section, and simplicial homotopies.

Result: The constructed model is naturally equivalent to the ∞-groupoid of null-concordances; its connected components (when nonempty) form a torsor over degree-zero cohomology of the associated weighted gauge complex; higher homotopy groups are isomorphic to the corresponding higher cohomology groups.

Conclusion: A canonical, cohomologically interpretable small model for null-concordances is achieved, enabling concrete homotopical analysis of higher gauge-theoretic constraints via weighted Lie-algebraic data.

Abstract: We construct an explicit small model for the $\infty$-groupoid of null-concordances of higher gauge curvatures by algorithmically trivializing their nonlinear Bianchi identities. More precisely, for a finite-type Sullivan minimal Bianchi algebra equipped with positive integer weights, we organize weighted potentials and higher gauge parameters at each fixed curvature into an $\infty$-groupoid whose gauge transformations are linear in the parameters. We prove that this model is naturally equivalent to the $\infty$-groupoid of null-concordances through an intermediate total-form model, with explicit projection and section maps and simplicial homotopies. When nonempty, its connected components form a torsor for the degree-zero cohomology of an associated weighted gauge complex, and its higher homotopy groups are computed by the higher cohomology of this complex.

</details>


### [25] [Universality and Emergent Classicality in Krylov Space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03188)
*Debarghya Chakraborty*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives a general spectral formula for late-time linear growth of Krylov complexity and its higher moments, applicable to systems with narrow energy distributions; validated on critical XY chain and double-scaled SYK.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize universal late-time behavior of Krylov complexity without relying on full Lanczos coefficient sequences, especially for quantum chaotic or integrable systems with structured spectra.

Method: Spectral analysis of the Hamiltonian in the Krylov basis; leverages narrow-energy-distribution approximation to map Krylov dynamics to classical motion; applies to critical XY chain and double-scaled SYK with one-loop chord-number moment computation.

Result: Obtains closed-form spectral expressions for leading growth coefficients of Krylov complexity and higher moments; confirms linear late-time growth and computes chord-number moments at one-loop order in double-scaled SYK.

Conclusion: Late-time Krylov complexity growth is spectrally determined and universal for states with narrow energy support; the classical-trajectory analogy holds broadly, bridging spectral properties and operator growth dynamics.

Abstract: Krylov complexity grows linearly at late times for many systems. In this regime, we give a general spectral formula for the leading growth coefficients of Krylov complexity and its higher moments, without requiring detailed knowledge of the Lanczos coefficients. For states with a narrow energy distribution compared to the bandwidth, dynamics in the Krylov basis resembles motion along a classical trajectory. We demonstrate our results for the critical XY chain and double-scaled SYK. For the latter, we compute the leading late-time behavior of the chord-number moments through one-loop order.

</details>


### [26] [What crossing symmetry forbids just above $c=1$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03217)
*Yu Nakayama*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Using numerical conformal bootstrap, the paper analyzes unitary 2D CFTs just above c=1, excluding certain scalar primary dimensions Δ_ϕ (0.17 ≲ Δ_ϕ ≲ 1), and shows that the lightest primary must satisfy Δ_gap < 0.17 at c ≈ 1.0005–1.001; it further identifies sharp features near Δ_ϕ = 1/2 and 4/7 aligning with the Z₅ parafermion CFT at c = 8/7.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the landscape of unitary two-dimensional conformal field theories (CFTs) infinitesimally above the critical central charge c = 1, where known solvable models (e.g., compact boson) exist but perturbative or structural constraints become nontrivial.

Method: Numerical conformal bootstrap applied to the four-point function of a scalar Virasoro primary ϕ with no lighter primaries in ϕ × ϕ OPE; combined with modular bootstrap bounds to constrain the spectrum.

Result: A forbidden interval 0.17 ≲ Δ_ϕ ≲ 1 is identified just above c = 1; Δ_gap < 0.17 is enforced for c = 1.0005 and 1.001; at fixed Δ_ϕ, the maximal excluded c exhibits sharp minima near Δ_ϕ = 1/2 and 4/7, coinciding with c = 8/7 — the central charge of the ℤ₅ parafermion and its orbifold.

Conclusion: The space of unitary 2D CFTs near c = 1 is highly constrained: generic compact-boson-like spectra are ruled out just above c = 1, and extremal or saturated bounds point to specific algebraic CFTs (e.g., ℤ₅ parafermion) as landmarks in the theory space.

Abstract: What does a unitary two-dimensional conformal field theory look like just above $c=1$? Using the numerical conformal bootstrap, we study the four-point function of a scalar Virasoro primary $\phi$ such that no primary lighter than $\phi$ appears in its operator product expansion with itself. Slightly above $c=1$, crossing symmetry excludes the interval $0.17\lesssim\Delta_\phi\lesssim1$, although the compact boson realizes every such $\Delta_\phi$ at $c=1$. Combining this exclusion with results from the modular bootstrap, we show that the lightest primary of a compact unitary conformal field theory at $c=1.0005$ or $1.001$ has dimension $\Delta_{\rm gap}<0.17$. Further from $c=1$, the largest excluded central charge at fixed $\Delta_\phi$ has two sharp minima near $\Delta_\phi=1/2$ and $4/7$. At these values it lies within $2\times10^{-7}$ of $c=8/7$, where the $\mathbb{Z}_5$ parafermion and its charge-conjugation orbifold have scalars of exactly these dimensions.

</details>


### [27] [From photons to fractons: Casimir effect in higher-rank electrodynamics and its fractonic branch](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03269)
*Nicola Maggiore*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Casimir effect in fracton gauge theories, contrasting a full Lorentz-covariant higher-rank symmetric tensor theory with its scalar-charge fractonic branch; it finds that only the fractonic branch yields a stable vacuum energy—specifically 3/2 the Maxwell Casimir energy—due to removal of an unstable vector sector by the Gauss constraint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how restricted mobility (fractonicity), arising from simultaneous conservation of charge and dipole moment, affects physical observables like vacuum energy, using the Casimir effect as a probe of allowed propagating modes between boundaries.

Method: Comparative analysis of Euclidean slab Casimir geometries in three settings: standard Maxwell electrodynamics, a Lorentz-covariant rank-2 symmetric tensor gauge theory, and its scalar-charge fractonic limit (selected via timelike background); includes boundary determinant computation, Lorentzian Hamiltonian stability analysis, and constraint imposition (Gauss law) to isolate physical modes.

Result: The full covariant higher-rank theory contains five modes: three Maxwell-like and two coupled unstable vector modes (unbounded Hamiltonian); the fractonic branch eliminates the unstable vector sector via Gauss constraint, leaving three stable massless modes (two TT tensor + one scalar trace), yielding Casimir energy = 3/2 × Maxwell value for ideal reflectors under both equal and complementary boundary conditions.

Conclusion: The Casimir effect cleanly isolates fractonic restrictions on the radiative spectrum; only the constrained fractonic branch—not the parent covariant theory—supports a well-defined, stable vacuum energy, confirming that physical fracton phases require careful constraint enforcement to eliminate unphysical, unstable sectors.

Abstract: The Casimir effect probes the modes that can propagate between boundaries. This makes it useful for fracton gauge theories, where restricted mobility follows from the simultaneous conservation of charge and dipole moment and changes the physical sector of the theory. We compare ordinary Maxwell electrodynamics with a Lorentz-covariant higher-rank theory of a symmetric tensor gauge field and with the scalar-charge fractonic branch selected from it by a preferred timelike background. The comparison shows why the distinction matters. In the full covariant theory, the Euclidean slab problem contains three independently propagating Maxwell-like modes and two coupled vector sectors that do not reduce to independent ordinary modes. Their boundary determinants are well defined, but a Lorentzian analysis shows that the vector sector has an unbounded Hamiltonian, so these determinants do not describe the vacuum energy of a stable fractonic phase. On the fractonic branch, the Gauss constraint removes that vector sector and leaves three stable massless modes: two transverse-traceless tensor modes and one scalar trace mode. For ideal reflectors, the physical Casimir energy is therefore 3/2 of the corresponding Maxwell result, for both equal and complementary boundary conditions. The calculation isolates the effect of the fractonic restriction on the radiative spectrum and clarifies which features of the full higher-rank boundary problem survive in the physical fracton sector.

</details>


### [28] [Functional Dimensional Regularization for the Lee-Yang Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03318)
*Piero Beretta,Carlos A. Sánchez-Villalobos,Alessandro Codello*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper applies Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR) to the non-unitary Lee-Yang universality class, computing critical exponents in 3 ≤ d ≤ 6 dimensions; results agree with resummed ε-expansion, Conformal Bootstrap, and Fuzzy Sphere methods, confirming FDR’s efficacy for non-unitary theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test whether Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR), previously successful for unitary theories (e.g., Ising, O(N)), remains efficient and accurate for non-unitary critical theories—specifically the Lee-Yang universality class, a paradigmatic example of non-unitarity.

Method: Functional Dimensional Regularization (FDR) combined with a second-order derivative expansion and polynomial field expansions to compute critical exponents across dimensions 3 ≤ d ≤ 6.

Result: Computed critical exponents (e.g., ν, η, Δ_σ) for the Lee-Yang class are compatible with resummed ε-expansion, high-precision Conformal Bootstrap, and Fuzzy Sphere estimates—demonstrating quantitative agreement and rapid convergence.

Conclusion: FDR is robust and effective for non-unitary quantum field theories: it retains efficiency, rapid convergence, and accuracy in predicting critical exponents, extending its applicability beyond unitary models to foundational non-unitary universality classes.

Abstract: We apply functional dimensional regularization (FDR) to the Lee-Yang universality class, a paradigmatic non-unitary theory, and compute its main critical exponents across the dimension range $3\leq d\leq 6$. By combining a second-order derivative expansion with polynomial field expansions, we obtain results that are compatible with resummed $\varepsilon$-expansion as well as with state-of-the-art estimates from Conformal Bootstrap and Fuzzy Sphere techniques. This demonstrates that, in the context of non-unitary theories, FDR retains the efficiency and rapid convergence to accurate critical exponents already observed in the Ising and O(N) cases.

</details>


### [29] [Smoothing the Bouncing-Geodesic Singularity in AdS/CFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03347)
*Mingxuan Liu,Shan-Ming Ruan*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how quantum corrections (finite $c$ or $G_N$) smear out complex-time singularities in boundary correlators that semiclassically encode black hole singularity geometry in AdS/CFT, using Liouville CFT as an exact example and identifying two smoothing mechanisms: QNM trajectory deformation and Gaussian thermal smearing.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve how finite-$G_N$ (or finite-$c$) quantum gravity effects modify the holographic signature of classical black hole singularities, which appear as complex-time divergences in analytically continued boundary correlators in the semiclassical limit.

Method: Analyzes analytically continued boundary correlators in AdS/CFT; studies near-singularity probing via spacelike extremal surfaces and bouncing geodesics; computes finite-$c$ deformations of quasinormal mode (QNM) spectra and pole-zero structure explicitly in Liouville CFT; identifies and quantifies a Gaussian smoothing mechanism from finite-energy thermal spectral fluctuations.
