Result: Finite-$c$ induces nonlinear deformations of QNM trajectories that generate a suppression kernel smoothing complex-time singularities; Liouville CFT provides explicit demonstration of this deformation and its pole-zero structure; a complementary Gaussian smoothing arises from thermal spectral decomposition at finite energy; both mechanisms show how quantum corrections regularize semiclassical singularity imprints.

Conclusion: Classical black hole singularity signatures in boundary correlators are not sharp features but are smoothed by finite-$G_N$ quantum effects—via deformed QNM spectra and thermal fluctuations—indicating that holography intrinsically resolves singularities through quantum gravitational UV completion.

Abstract: Black hole singularities remain a central open problem in quantum gravity. In the AdS/CFT correspondence, bulk spacelike extremal surfaces can probe the black hole interior, and information about the near-singularity geometry is encoded in analytically continued boundary correlators. As the bouncing geodesic approaches the black hole singularity, it becomes nearly null and gives rise to characteristic divergences at complex times. Importantly, these complex-time singularities emerge only in the strict semiclassical limit ($G_N\to 0$ or $c\to\infty$) and are governed by the asymptotically linear quasinormal mode (QNM) spectrum. At finite $c$, nonlinear deformations of the QNM trajectories produce suppression kernel that smooths out the complex-time singularities. Using Liouville CFT as an analytically solvable example, we explicitly demonstrate the finite-$c$ deformation of the QNM spectrum and its associated pole-zero structure. We also identify a complementary Gaussian smoothing mechanism arising from finite-energy fluctuations in the thermal spectral decomposition. These results show how finite $G_N$ quantum corrections modify the holographic imprints of the classical black hole singularity.

</details>


### [30] [Geometric duality between effective field theories. Part II. Exact classical solutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03383)
*Tomáš Brauner,Diederik Roest,Tianzhi Wang,Zhihao Zhang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends geometric duality to map exact classical solutions across several effective field theories—including Yang-Mills-scalar, nonlinear sigma model, Dirac-Born-Infeld, and special Galileon—enabling explicit solution transfers and higher-dimensional uplifts to pure Yang-Mills and gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize geometric duality beyond scattering amplitudes (established in prior work) to exact classical solutions, thereby enabling systematic construction of nontrivial solutions across physically distinct but geometrically related theories.

Method: Leverages geometric duality rooted in shared symmetric coset structures; constructs explicit field redefinitions and parametrizations that map elementary scalar fields between theories; employs dimensional uplifts to extend dualities to higher-dimensional pure Yang-Mills and gravity.

Result: Explicit duality maps for exact classical solutions between: (i) nonlinear sigma model ↔ Yang-Mills-scalar theory, (ii) special Galileon ↔ Dirac-Born-Infeld, (iii) Dirac-Born-Infeld ↔ nonlinear sigma model coupled to dynamical gravity; plus uplifted exact solutions for pure Yang-Mills and pure gravity in higher dimensions.

Conclusion: Geometric duality is a powerful solution-generating tool—not only for amplitudes but also for classical solutions—and provides a unified framework connecting diverse effective theories through shared geometry and symmetry.

Abstract: Geometric duality reveals a novel bridge between a series of well-known scalar effective field theories: the Yang-Mills theory coupled to a specific scalar sector (aka the Yang-Mills-scalar theory), the nonlinear sigma model on a symmetric coset space, the Dirac-Born-Infeld theory, and the special Galileon theory. In our previous work, we used the geometric duality to map scattering amplitudes in the various theories to one another. In this sequel, we show that the duality makes it also possible to transport exact classical solutions. Thus, exact solutions of the nonlinear sigma model are mapped to a subset of solutions of the Yang-Mills-scalar theory. Likewise, solutions of the special Galileon theory map to solutions of the Dirac-Born-Infeld theory, which in turn map to solutions of the nonlinear sigma model coupled to dynamical gravity. For all these duality maps, we provide explicit examples of exact solutions of the source theory and their corresponding image in the target theory. While the duality maps are manifest at the level of certain geometric building blocks of the theories, we show that with a suitable choice of parametrisation, it is also possible to map directly the elementary scalar fields of the various theories to one another. This offers a practically convenient tool for constructing new exact solutions of the target theories. Finally, the duality sequence including the nonlinear sigma model and the Yang-Mills-scalar theory can be extended by uplifting the solutions of the latter to a higher-dimensional spacetime. One thus arrives at a class of exact solutions of pure Yang-Mills theory in higher dimensions. In a similar vein, it is possible to obtain exact solutions of higher-dimensional pure gravity by uplifting solutions of the nonlinear sigma model, coupled to dynamical gravity, obtained via a duality map from solutions of the special Galileon theory.

</details>


### [31] [Symmetry breaking from conformality loss on holographic Rényi defects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03413)
*Siqing Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies Rényi entropies of a ball via a conformal defect (Rényi defect) dual to a hyperbolic black hole in AdS/CFT. It identifies a critical Rényi index $n_*$ where a charged operator merges with its alternate-quantization partner, triggering loss of conformality and spontaneous U(1) symmetry breaking at large $N$. Beyond $n_*$, a source-free RG flow leads to a symmetry-breaking conformal defect; below $n_*$, a symmetry-breaking source is required. Scalar hair preserves bulk symmetries except near the entangling surface, ensuring universality of the Rényi entropy’s logarithmic term. The mechanism is explicitly demonstrated in the probe Abelian Higgs model in AdS$_5$ at the Bogomolny point, and rigorously established on a sphere for $n > n_*$ with sufficient size. A finite scalar mass window is required; for sufficiently attractive potentials, symmetry breaking can precede conformality loss. Operators with low scaling dimension may break symmetry already at $n=2$. Classical backreaction does not shift $n_*$, and flows persist at first order away from $n_*$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how Rényi entropy universal terms arise from symmetry breaking and conformality loss in holographic CFTs, particularly how spontaneous U(1) breaking emerges at large $N$ via defect RG flows triggered by operator merging at a critical Rényi index $n_*$.

Method: Holographic duality (AdS/CFT), conformal defect analysis, RG flow study of the Rényi defect, exact saddle-point solutions in the probe Abelian Higgs model at the Bogomolny point in AdS$_5$, numerical confirmation of flows, analytical proof of existence/uniqueness of positive source-free flows on a sphere, stability and backreaction analysis at leading order.

Result: Identification of $n_*$ as the merger point of charged operator and alternate-quantization partner; demonstration that conformality loss triggers spontaneous U(1) breaking at large $N$; proof that beyond $n_*$ a unique, source-free flow ends at a symmetry-breaking conformal defect; confirmation that scalar hair respects hyperbolic symmetry outside a thin layer near the entangling surface—ensuring universality of the Rényi entropy’s logarithmic term; establishment of necessary conditions (scalar mass window, potential attraction) for early symmetry breaking; verification that classical backreaction leaves $n_*$ unchanged and flows persist at first order.

Conclusion: The universal Rényi entropy term arises from a symmetry-breaking conformal defect reached via a source-free RG flow triggered by conformality loss at $n_*$, which itself stems from operator-quantization merger. Spontaneous symmetry breaking is generic for $n > n_*$ at large $N$, and occurs already at $n = 2$ for sufficiently relevant operators. The mechanism is robust under classical backreaction and analytically tractable in specific holographic setups.

Abstract: The Rényi entropies of a ball are computed by a conformal defect, the Rényi defect, dual in holography to a hyperbolic black hole. At an index $n_*$ its charged operator merges with its alternate-quantization partner, and the defect loses conformality. Beyond $n_*$ it flows with no source to a conformal defect that breaks the U(1) symmetry; below $n_*$ that defect needs a symmetry-breaking source, so at large $N$, conformality loss makes the breaking spontaneous. The scalar hair beyond $n_*$, expected to break the symmetries of hyperbolic space, thus preserves them outside a thin layer at the entangling surface, as conformal symmetry requires. The region outside the layer sets the universal term of the Rényi entropy. We demonstrate the mechanism in the probe Abelian Higgs model in AdS$_5$ at the Bogomolny point, where the saddles are in closed form and numerical methods confirm the flows. On a sphere we prove that a positive source-free flow exists exactly when $n>n_*$ and the sphere is large enough; it is then unique and ends at the symmetry-breaking defect. The mechanism needs a scalar mass in a finite window, and for potentials attractive at small amplitude, the symmetry can break before conformality is lost. For operators of low enough dimension, already the second Rényi entropy lies beyond the merger. Classical backreaction does not move the merger at any order; we argue that away from $n_*$ the flows persist at first order.

</details>


### [32] [Fractional $θ$-dependence from the sum over all instanton sectors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03467)
*Yui Hayashi,Mithat Ünsal*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper resolves the apparent contradiction between integer-charged instantons and the multibranched θ-vacuum structure in supersymmetric CP^{N−1} and SU(N) Yang-Mills theories by showing that summing over all instanton sectors reproduces the correct multibranched vacuum dependence via moduli-space volume integration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the integer topological charge of individual instantons with the multibranched (2π-periodic but non-analytic) θ dependence of the Yang-Mills vacuum, which suggests fractional or collective topological behavior.

Method: Path integral evaluation via exact summation over all instanton sectors; reduction to integrals over instanton moduli spaces in self-dual supersymmetric CP^{N−1} (2D) and SU(N) Yang-Mills (4D) with chiral mass; use of supersymmetry and localization techniques.

Result: The full path integral yields a multibranched vacuum energy E(θ) ∝ |cos(θ/2)| (or similar non-analytic periodic form), confirming consistency with expected θ-structure; in 4D, generalizes Nekrasov’s instanton counting beyond Seiberg–Witten theory to a scalar-free, QCD-like setting.

Conclusion: Instantons—despite carrying integer topological charge—collectively generate the correct multibranched θ dependence when all sectors are included; supersymmetry and chiral mass enable exact control, revealing that moduli-space geometry encodes vacuum topology.

Abstract: An instanton carries integer topological charge, seemingly at odds with the multibranched $\theta$ dependence of the Yang-Mills vacuum. We show that summing over all instanton sectors resolves this mismatch in self-dual supersymmetric $\mathbb{C}P^{N-1}$ and $\mathrm{SU}(N)$ Yang-Mills theories with a chiral mass, in two and four dimensions. The path integral reduces to a sum of instanton moduli-space volumes, yielding the multibranched vacuum structure. In four dimensions, this extends Nekrasov's instanton counting for Seiberg-Witten theory to a QCD-like theory without scalars.

</details>


### [33] [I want to Break Free: The Positive Side of Symmetry Breaking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03510)
*Anubha Bal,Claudia de Rham*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes positivity bounds on Wilson coefficients of derivative operators in multi-scalar EFTs by leveraging internal field-space symmetries, showing that deviations from enhanced symmetries (e.g., O(N)) are quantitatively constrained—even if the symmetry is not imposed—and that constraints cascade hierarchically down symmetry-breaking subgroups.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how internal field-space symmetries constrain low-energy EFTs beyond standard positivity bounds, especially when enhanced symmetries (like O(N)) are absent in the Lagrangian but may be emergent or preferred by UV consistency.

Method: Classification of derivative operators in N-scalar EFTs by their preserved internal symmetry groups (O(N) and its subgroups); derivation of positivity bounds via forward-limit dispersion relations and crossing-symmetric 2→2 and 3→3 scattering amplitudes involving indefinite-field superpositions; systematic analysis of coefficient hierarchies across symmetry-breaking cascades.

Result: Wilson coefficients of operators breaking a larger symmetry are bounded above by those preserving it; such bounds persist even without imposing the enhanced symmetry a priori; hierarchical constraints propagate along subgroup chains (e.g., O(N) ⊃ O(N−1) ⊃ ...), tightening as symmetry decreases; verified explicitly in 2→2 and 3→3 scattering examples.

Conclusion: Internal symmetry structure fundamentally shapes the allowed parameter space of scalar EFTs via positivity, enforcing nontrivial correlations among Wilson coefficients and providing a symmetry-based organizing principle for UV-completable effective theories.

Abstract: We study the role of internal field-space symmetries in Effective Field Theories constrained by positivity bounds. For theories with $N$ real scalar fields, we classify derivative operators according to the internal symmetries they preserve, organising them into hierarchies based on the maximal symmetry $O(N)$ and its subgroups. We find that the Wilson coefficients of operators that depart from an enhanced symmetry are bounded in terms of those that respect it. This means that departures from an enhanced internal symmetry cannot be made arbitrarily large while maintaining a standard UV completion, even when the enhanced internal symmetry was not imposed in constructing the low-energy theory. Moreover, along a cascade of progressively smaller preserved subgroups, the coefficients of operators preserving lower levels of symmetry are bounded by those preserving larger subgroups in the cascade, and are consequently subject to progressively stronger constraints. We demonstrate these results through a variety of examples involving $2-2$ and $3-3$ scattering of indefinite states formed from superpositions of multiple scalar species.

</details>


### [34] [The Virasoro Bootstrap: from the $c\sim1$ desert to coupled Potts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03565)
*Stefanos R. Kousvos,Alessandro Piazza,Alessandro Vichi*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a numerical conformal bootstrap method for 2D CFTs with c > 1 using Virasoro blocks (via Zamolodchikov recursion and optimal polynomial interpolation), isolates islands/peninsulas for decoupled/coupled Ising and Potts models, and analyzes the collapse of scalar gap bounds near c = 1—supporting the nonexistence of compact CFTs in that region.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the numerical conformal bootstrap to irrational 2D CFTs with c > 1, where standard analytic techniques fail and Virasoro symmetry is essential; to probe existence and uniqueness of CFTs (e.g., coupled Potts models) and test conjectures about the landscape near c = 1.

Method: Numerical conformal bootstrap using high-precision Virasoro conformal blocks computed via Zamolodchikov's recursion relation and optimized polynomial interpolation; analysis of mixed correlators and single-correlator bounds; systematic use of gap assumptions informed by perturbation theory.

Result: An isolated island for three decoupled Ising models; a peninsula containing the coupled 3-state Potts fixed point but excluding the decoupled one; collapse of the scalar gap bound for c just above 1, excluding certain theories and supporting the conjecture of no compact CFTs in that regime.

Conclusion: The Virasoro bootstrap is effective for probing irrational 2D CFTs with c > 1; geometric features (islands/peninsulas) distinguish decoupled vs. interacting fixed points; the collapse of scalar bounds near c = 1 provides strong evidence against the existence of compact unitary CFTs in that region.

Abstract: We develop a numerical conformal bootstrap for two-dimensional CFTs with $c>1$ based on Virasoro conformal blocks, computed with Zamolodchikov's recursion and optimal polynomial interpolation. As a first test we isolate an island around three decoupled Ising models. We then consider three 3-state Potts models coupled through their energy operators, a candidate irrational CFT. Mixed correlators isolate the decoupled theory in an island, and different gap assumptions, motivated by perturbation theory, yield a peninsula that contains the coupled fixed point but not the decoupled one. Finally, we explore the standard single-correlator bound on the lowest-dimension scalar primary in the OPE of two identical scalars. We find that for central charges just above $c=1$ the bound collapses over a wide range of external dimensions. This excludes any theory whose lightest scalar is absent from its own OPE and lies in this range, and supports the conjecture that no compact CFT exists in this region. We explain several features of these bounds analytically.

</details>


### [35] [Nonlinear gauge maps and the structure of Goldstone loops in canonical inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03571)
*Cheng-Jun Fang,Zong-Kuan Guo*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper clarifies the nonlinear mapping between scalar-field and Goldstone descriptions in single-field inflation, shows how potential interactions reorganize into derivative Goldstone interactions via an exact clock map, connects dilation symmetry across slicings, and demonstrates—using mode-equation identities—that one-loop Goldstone two-point function contributions nontrivially cancel (excluding boundary terms) when counterterms are included, all without solving mode functions; the leading long-mode response reduces to a total derivative in loop momentum, necessitating extra regularization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in the nonlinear relation between scalar and Goldstone pictures of canonical single-field inflation, and to understand how symmetries and quantum corrections (specifically at one-loop) behave consistently across descriptions without relying on explicit mode solutions.

Method: Exact clock mapping between spatially flat scalar and Goldstone frames; slicing transformation to relate dilation symmetries; use of general mode-equation identities (not explicit mode-function solutions) to analyze the one-loop Goldstone two-point function in the decoupling limit; systematic inclusion of counterterms to isolate and cancel non-boundary loop contributions.

Result: Nonlinear equivalence between scalar and Goldstone descriptions is made precise via clock map and slicing change; at one loop, seemingly independent diagrammatic contributions combine such that their non-boundary parts cancel exactly upon inclusion of appropriate counterterms; the leading adiabatic long-mode response simplifies to a total derivative in loop momentum—implying scheme dependence and requiring additional regularization beyond standard dimensional or cutoff schemes.

Conclusion: The scalar and Goldstone descriptions are nonlinearly equivalent through geometric mappings preserving symmetry structure; quantum consistency at one loop is maintained not by diagram-by-diagram finiteness but by cancellation among nonlocal contributions enforced by symmetry-derived identities—highlighting the centrality of symmetry and regularization in inflationary EFTs.

Abstract: We clarify the nonlinear relation between the spatially flat scalar and Goldstone descriptions of canonical single-field inflation. An exact clock map shows how potential interactions are reorganized into derivative Goldstone interactions. A change of slicing further connects their dilation symmetry to its comoving realization. We then study the Goldstone two-point function at one loop in the decoupling limit. Using identities derived from the general mode equation, we reveal how seemingly independent loop contributions combine and their non-boundary parts cancel once the associated counterterms are included. This reduction requires no explicit mode-function solution. The leading adiabatic long-mode response reduces to a total derivative in loop momentum, which requires an additional regularization prescription.

</details>


### [36] [Mutual information to all orders from modular flow: a replica-free approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03630)
*Hewei Frederic Jia*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Derives an all-orders long-distance expansion of vacuum mutual information between two disjoint balls in generic d-dimensional CFTs using radial sewing and modular flow—no replica analytic continuation—yielding modular integrals of flowed correlators; explicitly computed up to 10th order for 2D Ising CFT and matches lattice data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compute vacuum mutual information between disjoint balls in CFTs beyond the two-point sector and without relying on replica trick analytic continuation, enabling access to higher-point contributions and improved convergence/control in long-distance regime.

Method: Novel radial sewing expansion of the joint vacuum density matrix, combined with modular response formula for relative entropy and geometric modular flow of a single ball; expansion coefficients expressed as modular integrals of modular-flowed vacuum correlators.

Result: All-orders long-distance expansion of mutual information; full two-point sector resums into a modular integral of thermal partition function on S^{d−1} with configuration-dependent effective temperature; explicit 10-term expansion for 2D Ising CFT (involving up to 20-spin sphere correlators), validated against lattice data and crossing symmetry; analytic recovery of known coefficients for 2D free boson (self-dual radius) and free scalars in d > 2.

Conclusion: Provides a rigorous, replica-free framework for mutual information in disjoint regions, unifying and extending prior results, with practical computational power for strongly coupled CFTs and potential applications to entanglement geometry and holography.

Abstract: We derive an all-orders long-distance expansion of vacuum mutual information between two disjoint balls in generic $d$-dimensional CFTs. The derivation uses a novel radial sewing expansion of the joint vacuum density matrix, together with a modular response formula for relative entropy and the geometric modular flow of a single ball, without analytic continuation in replica number. The expansion coefficients are modular integrals of modular-flowed vacuum correlators. Our formula provides a systematic treatment of arbitrary multi-point sectors, and recovers known results on two-point sector derived from replica methods. The full two-point sector resums into a modular integral of the thermal partition function on $S^{d-1}$, with an effective temperature depending on ball configuration and modular time. We compute the expansion explicitly in CFTs. In two-dimensional Ising CFT, using analytic reductions and multidimensional numerical integration, we obtain the first ten radial orders in the long-distance expansion, which involve sphere correlators with up to twenty spin insertions. The truncated expansion agrees well with lattice data at finite separation, and approximately satisfies crossing symmetry. Analytic calculations also reproduce known expansion coefficients for 2d free compact boson at self-dual radius and its current sector, as well as for free scalars in higher dimensions.

</details>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [37] [Spectral instability of charged scalar quasinormal modes and superradiance: Stabilizing the unstable](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03070)
*Kyriakos Destounis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how small localized perturbations to the scattering potential of Reissner–Nordström and Reissner–Nordström–de Sitter black holes affect superradiant energy extraction, finding that while quasinormal modes migrate strongly in the complex plane, superradiant amplification is only weakly altered in flat spacetime—but can be stabilized (i.e., suppressed modal growth) without eliminating superradiance in de Sitter spacetimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether the known spectral instability of black hole quasinormal modes under small potential perturbations translates to significant changes in superradiant energy extraction—particularly whether such perturbations can suppress or enhance superradiance without violating its fundamental frequency bounds.

Method: The authors introduce a localized positive potential bump (small in mass-scaled height) displaced outward from the light ring on Reissner–Nordström (asymptotically flat) and Reissner–Nordström–de Sitter backgrounds, preserving radiative boundary conditions. They compute quasinormal mode spectra and analyze superradiant amplification factors, tracking mode migrations, overtakings, and stability transitions—especially for monopole modes in de Sitter cases.

Result: In asymptotically flat RN: a damped quasinormal branch enters the superradiant regime; the least-damped positive-frequency mode also enters it after overtakings, but real-frequency amplification remains weak and non-resonant. In RNdS: a small bump can stabilize initially growing monopole modes (move them to Im(ω) < 0), yet peak superradiant gain stays >100% unless the bump is far out—showing decoupling between modal stability and scattering amplification.

Conclusion: Spectral instability does not imply strong sensitivity of superradiant amplification; small positive potential perturbations can stabilize modally unstable black holes without suppressing superradiance itself—highlighting a robustness of the superradiant frequency condition and a nuanced role of quasinormal-mode migration in energy extraction.

Abstract: The spectral instability of black holes has become a central topic of the black-hole spectroscopy program. Small changes to the black-hole scattering potential are known to produce disproportionately large migrations of its quasinormal modes in the complex plane, but it is unclear whether this sensitivity carries over to energy extraction through superradiance. We study this relation for massless charged scalar fields on Reissner--Nordström and Reissner--Nordström--de Sitter backgrounds. A localized positive potential bump, small in mass-scaled height, is displaced outward from the light ring while the original radiative boundary conditions are preserved. In the asymptotically flat case, a quasinormal branch enters the superradiant frequency regime while remaining damped. By searching for competing modes, we show that the least-damped positive-frequency mode also enters this regime, after a series of mode overtakings driven by quasinormal-mode migration. The real-frequency amplification, nevertheless, is only weakly affected and develops no pronounced narrow resonance. In de Sitter black holes, the bump can move an initially growing monopole mode into the damped half of the complex plane, stabilizing selected configurations that are initially modally unstable. After stabilization, the peak superradiant amplification can remain above both its unperturbed value and a gain of $100\%$; it falls below $100\%$ only for more distant bump locations. A prescribed positive perturbation can thus suppress modal growth without changing the superradiant frequency bounds or immediately suppressing the scattering peak.

</details>

Abstract: We study a real scalar field nonminimally coupled to curvature on a fixed Ellis wormhole background. This yields the exact threshold spectrum $ \zeta_{n\ell}=(n+\ell+1)^2/2 $, closed-form Gegenbauer profiles, and an exact count of negative radial eigenvalues in each angular sector. The s-wave thresholds are zero-energy resonances at which the growth rate rises linearly, whereas the $ \ell \geq1\ $ threshold states are square integrable and the growth rate rises as a square root. At the first threshold we also obtain the next-order term, $ \sigma a=(\pi/4)g-(3\pi/16)g^2+O(g^3) $ with $g=2\zeta-1 $. For a probe field with quartic self-interaction, we show that no nontrivial spherical static solution exists for $\zeta\leq1/2 $ and derive local branch expansions near the s-wave thresholds. These nonlinear results refer to the probe-field model; they do not establish the existence or stability of fully backreacted scalarized wormholes.

Abstract: We study the real-time response of a Schwarzschild black hole in the Hartle-Hawking state to an external massless scalar field. Using a complexified radial contour to represent the Schwinger-Keldysh closed time path, we describe dissipation, thermal fluctuations and conservative tidal response within a common framework. Before performing tidal matching, we derive the equilibrium fluctuation-dissipation relation directly from the radial construction for every scalar multipole at arbitrary real frequency. In the low-frequency tidal expansion, we recover the known relation between the logarithmic running of the conservative dynamical Love coefficient at order $\omega^2$ and the leading dissipative response at order $\omega$. We explain how the same leading absorptive coefficient enters both relations at low frequency and trace the unit coefficient of the running relation to the Schwarzschild horizon slope. In the Hartle-Hawking state, this common coefficient relates the logarithmic running directly to the zero-frequency thermal noise. Kubo-Martin-Schwinger analyticity determines the thermal noise from the dissipative response, whereas the near-zone radial equation and tidal matching determine the conservative logarithmic running.

</details>

Abstract: We derive the parallel-transport holonomies of circles centered on the symmetry axis in the homogeneous Gödel-type family of Rebouças and Tiomno, which includes the Gödel universe, the causal member with $m^2=4\Omega^2$, and the Som--Raychaudhuri limit. We determine the number of parabolic radii across the family and prove that, whenever the azimuthal circles become closed timelike curves (CTCs), the chronology radius satisfies $\Delta(r_c)=-\cosh(mr_c)<0$. We also express this invariant in terms of Killing-vector scalars, derive the vector and spinor Wilson loops, give the exact condition for trivial holonomy after a finite number of turns, and analyze Fermi--Walker transport on circular CTCs. Finally, we discuss an analogy with the effective metric of a rotating superfluid, valid under stated conditions, and derive exact expressions for the radial $L^2$ localization of Klein--Gordon Landau modes relative to the Som--Raychaudhuri chronology radius. </details>


### [42] [Unitary implementability of Darboux transformations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00724)

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the unitary implementability of Darboux transformations—canonical transformations in the Hamiltonian formalism for gauge-invariant black hole perturbations—and shows they are unitarily implementable under general conditions on effective potentials; it further demonstrates inequivalence between the privileged Fock representation selected by unitary dynamics and the standard massless representation of Regge-Wheeler variables, underscoring the critical role of master variable choice, and reveals that not all dynamically admissible variable choices arise from Darboux transformations alone but require more general canonical transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify how classical Darboux transformations—used extensively in black hole perturbation theory—translate into quantum field theory, particularly regarding unitary implementability and representation equivalence in the quantization of gauge-invariant perturbations.

Method: Hamiltonian analysis of gauge-invariant perturbations in the black hole interior; examination of canonical transformations induced by Darboux maps; assessment of unitary implementability via criteria on effective potentials; comparison of Fock representations using unitary equivalence criteria (e.g., Shale’s theorem); classification of admissible master variables via canonical transformation structure.

Result: Darboux transformations are unitarily implementable under mild conditions on effective potentials; the Heisenberg-unitary-selected Fock representation is not unitarily equivalent to the standard massless Regge-Wheeler representation; only a subset of unitarily implementable variable choices arises from Darboux transformations—others require broader canonical transformations.

Conclusion: The quantum representation of black hole perturbations depends critically on the choice of master variables, and classical isospectral relations (e.g., Darboux) must be generalized to ensure full unitary implementability across all angular momentum sectors in QFT.

Abstract: We investigate the unitary implementability of the canonical transformations corresponding to Darboux transformations in the Hamiltonian formalism recently developed for gauge invariant perturbations of nonrotating black holes. Starting from the formalism in the black hole interior, we demonstrate that these canonical transformations are unitarily implementable under general conditions on the effective potentials appearing in the master equations of the perturbations. We also show that the privileged massless Fock representation of the perturbative variables selected by admitting (Heisenberg) unitary dynamics, employed in our quantization, is not unitarily equivalent to the simple massless representation of the master variables satisfying the effective Regge-Wheeler equation. This highlights the importance of the choice of master variables in which the quantum representation is initially defined. Furthermore, we show that not all such choices of variables possessing unitarily implementable dynamics can be reached through Darboux transformations, but rather through more general canonical transformations. These results advance our understanding of the relationship between well-established classical relations in black hole perturbation theory and their quantum representations, explaining how isospectral Darboux transformations can be extended to include all the infinitely many possible values of the angular momentum such that they are unitarily implementable in a quantum field theory context.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile regular black-hole models with thermodynamic consistency—particularly addressing hidden entropy associated with inner horizons—and to understand how rotation and regularization affect entropy distribution, horizon structure, and curvature behavior during black-hole formation.

Method: Analytical derivation within general relativity: constructs a regular rotating black-hole family with matched charge thermodynamics; defines hidden entropy via inner-horizon area deviation from Kerr; proves inequalities using regularity and interior-profile assumptions; derives exact evolution equations for inner-root dynamics; examines stationary curvature integrability and horizon regularity across a parametrized family (indexed by n).

Result: Derives sharp bound 0 < S_exc < 4πM√(M²−a²); proves r_−^Kerr < h_c < h; shows S_exc → 0 at Kerr endpoint and S_exc = πh_c² in Schwarzschild limit; establishes Ricci-scalar density integrability for −1 < n ≤ 2; identifies n = 0 as Minkowski-breaking threshold independent of inner-horizon loss; finds quasi-extremal configurations approach but cannot saturate entropy bound without losing regular Killing horizon.

Conclusion: Hidden entropy is a well-defined, bounded geometric-thermodynamic quantity tied to inner-horizon regularization; the Kerr solution is thermodynamically special as the zero-hidden-entropy endpoint; regularity constraints tightly govern allowable entropy, curvature, and horizon dynamics—implying fundamental limits on how closely regular black holes can emulate extremal Kerr.

Abstract: \noindent We develop a charge-matched thermodynamic framework for the regular rotating black-hole family underlying an exact model of Kerr formation. Since the Kerr endpoint already possesses a Cauchy horizon, hidden entropy is identified with the excess inner-horizon area relative to Kerr at fixed mass $M$ and angular momentum $J=Ma$: $\Sexc=\pi[h_c^2-(r_-^{\Kerr})^2]$, where $h_c$ is the regular inner-horizon radius and $r_-^{\Kerr}=M-\sqrt{M^2-a^2}$. Under regularity and interior-profile assumptions, we prove $r_-^{\Kerr}<h_c<h$ and establish the sharp bound $0<\Sexc<4\pi M\sqrt{M^2-a^2}$. The Schwarzschild limit yields $\Sexc=\pi h_c^2$, while $\Sexc=0$ at the Kerr endpoint. The Kerr reference entropy satisfies $S_+^{\Kerr}+S_-^{\Kerr}=4\pi M^2$, so rotation redistributes its two-horizon contributions at fixed mass. For the time-dependent model, we derive exact evolution equations for the inner root and $\Sexc$, interpreted as root-bookkeeping identities unless the roots are established as dynamical horizons. In the minimal $N=1$ trajectory, the apparent pole at $n=2$ is removable in the mass profile, giving a logarithmic continuation of the root equation, although the stationary curvature is singular there. The stationary Ricci-scalar density is locally absolutely integrable for $-1<n\leq2$, with the Kerr endpoint $n=-1$ treated separately. Minkowski breaking occurs at $n=0$, independently of inner-horizon disappearance. Quasi-extremal regular configurations approach, but cannot attain, the entropy bound without losing a regular Killing horizon.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the physical resources—particularly external energy—required for the idealized process of collapsing Hawking radiation into a second black hole, as used in ER=EPR thought experiments.

Conclusion: The conversion process is energetically costly and constrained by semiclassical entropy bounds; successful purification (exact or approximate) imposes stricter conditions than mere entropy matching, highlighting a gap between information-theoretic and operational feasibility in black hole reconstruction.

Abstract: A thought experiment in the ER=EPR and related literature involves collecting the Hawking radiation of an old black hole, then collapsing it into a second, entangled black hole. In this short note, we examine the resources required by this idealized conversion. More specifically, we are interested in answering the question: how much external energy is needed to form the second black hole? Assuming a semiclassical bound on the information capacity of the newly formed black hole, we derive a conditional lower bound on external energy and a complementary constraint on correlations retained by the exterior degrees of freedom. We also distinguish entropy-level feasibility from the requirements for exact and approximate purification. We illustrate these requirements via a Page curve analysis.

</details>


### [45] [Black Hole Love Numbers with Physics-Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02084)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a robust, mesh-free, and differentiable numerical framework for computing black-hole tidal Love numbers—particularly for cases where analytic solutions are unavailable (e.g., frequency-dependent or non-separable perturbations)—while overcoming limitations of traditional spectral or finite-difference methods in handling boundary conditions and asymptotics.

Method: A physics-informed neural network is constructed to solve static perturbation equations of the Kerr black hole; it enforces exact horizon boundary conditions analytically and employs an H²-Sobolev loss that penalizes both the PDE residual and its first and second derivatives; tidal Love numbers are extracted directly from asymptotic coefficients of the learned solution, bypassing analytic connection formulas.

Result: The method recovers finite dissipative tidal responses with relative errors < 10⁻⁵ across scalar, vector, and tensor perturbations; higher-derivative loss terms improve accuracy by orders of magnitude; conservative (vanishing) response is constrained by the numerical error floor; extraction procedure is validated independently to isolate solver vs. coefficient recovery errors.

Conclusion: PINNs provide an accurate, flexible, and validated framework for computing black-hole tidal response; this work establishes a foundation for extending to more complex, physically realistic scenarios—including dynamical, frequency-dependent, and non-separable perturbations—where conventional methods face challenges.

Abstract: We develop and validate a physics-informed neural network (PINN) framework for computing the tidal Love numbers of black holes directly from their perturbation equations. We consider static perturbations of the Kerr black hole, whose exact hypergeometric solutions provide a controlled benchmark for both the numerical solver and the extraction of the physical response. The framework combines exact enforcement of the horizon boundary conditions with an $H^2$-Sobolev loss that incorporates derivatives of the differential-equation residual. The Love number is then obtained by extracting the asymptotic coefficients of the learned solution, without using the analytic connection formula. Across the tested scalar, vector, and tensor perturbations, the method recovers the finite dissipative response with relative errors below $10^{-5}$. Controlled comparisons show that the higher-derivative loss terms improve solution accuracy by orders of magnitude, while independent tests of the extraction procedure distinguish solver errors from errors in recovering the response coefficients. The conservative response, which vanishes analytically, is constrained by the numerical error floor. These results demonstrate that PINNs can accurately compute black-hole tidal response and establish a validated framework for future extensions to frequency-dependent and non-separable perturbation problems.

</details>


### [46] [Dimensionally Regulated Graviton Self-Energy from Gravitons on de Sitter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02120)
*B. Yesilyurt,N. C. Tsamis,R. P. Woodard*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper computes the 1-loop graviton self-energy on de Sitter background using dimensional regularization and BPHZ renormalization, then derives quantum corrections to gravitational wave mode functions and the Newtonian potential—finding no secular growth in modes but a cubic e-folding growth in the potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantum-correct the linearized Einstein equation via the graviton self-energy — enabling inference of quantum effects on gravitational radiation and static gravity — particularly on cosmologically relevant de Sitter spacetime.

Method: Computes the 1PI graviton 2-point function (self-energy) at 1-loop in pure quantum gravity on de Sitter background, using dimensional regularization and BPHZ renormalization in the simplest gauge; solves the resulting effective field equations for plane-wave mode functions and the Newtonian potential.

Result: 1-loop corrections yield finite, renormalizable mode function corrections (potentially free of secular growth), but the Newtonian potential exhibits unbounded growth ∝ N³, where N is the number of e-foldings.

Conclusion: While gravitational radiation mode functions may remain stable under renormalization, the static gravitational potential suffers severe infrared-driven secular growth at 1-loop in de Sitter quantum gravity, signaling breakdown of perturbative stability or need for non-perturbative resummation.

Abstract: The graviton self-energy $-i [\mbox{}^{\mu\nu} \Sigma^{\rho\sigma}](x;x')$ is the 1PI 2-point function of quantum gravity. It can be used to quantum-correct the linearized Einstein equation in order to infer quantum corrections to gravitational radiation and to the force of gravity. As long as the two points are non-coincident, the graviton self-energy does not need regularization at 1-loop order, however, solving the 1-loop effective field equations requires integrating $[\mbox{}^{\mu\nu} \Sigma^{\rho \sigma}](x;x')$ over ${x'}^{\mu}$, including ${x'}^{\mu} = x^{\mu}$. To facilitate this we employ dimensional regularization and BPHZ renormalization to compute the 1-loop contribution to the graviton self-energy, in pure quantum gravity, on de Sitter background, in the simplest gauge. We then solve the effective field equations to derive 1-loop corrections to the plane wave mode functions of gravitational radiation, and to the Newtonian potential. Although it seems possible to renormalize in such a way that the mode function shows no secular growth, the Newtonian potential grows like the third power of the number of e-foldings.

</details>


### [47] [Static Vacuum Geometry Encodes Gravitational Collapse and Cosmology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02358)

Abstract: We show that, within covariant Hamiltonian gravity in spherical symmetry, a prescribed one-parameter family of static vacuum geometries uniquely determines the local dynamics of gravitational collapse coupled to irrotational dust, despite the nonuniqueness of the underlying gravitational Hamiltonian. We further derive necessary and sufficient conditions under which the resulting dynamics admits a homogeneous and isotropic cosmological sector. These conditions establish a two-way correspondence between static vacuum geometries and modified Friedmann dynamics and impose a universal restriction on the spatial-curvature dependence of the latter. Our results provide a criterion for determining whether independently proposed black-hole and cosmological models can arise from a common covariant gravitational theory.

</details>


### [48] [A simple flux criterion for trapped surface formation by short pulses of gravitational radiation and matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02569)

Abstract: We discuss the formation of trapped surfaces induced by short pulses of incoming gravitational radiation and matter fields, in a double-null formulation. We identify a hierarchy of orders in the pulse width for the geometric and matter quantities consistent with the evolution equations. Under the assumption that this hierarchy holds throughout the region considered, the incoming fluxes undergo approximately conservative inward transport and are thereby amplified. The Raychaudhuri equation then gives an initial flux criterion for trapped surface formation. The conditions on the matter are expressed through the Ricci components and their derivatives, which allows the argument to be developed without specifying a particular matter model.

</details>


### [49] [Cloudy black-hole configurations of the composed Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs field theory: Analytic treatment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02656)

  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To analytically characterize the onset of hair formation (i.e., existence of spatially regular linearized field configurations) on magnetically charged black holes in Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs theory, specifically probing the 'cloudy' black-hole-field bound states that violate the no-hair conjecture.

Abstract: Non-Abelian black holes that violate the non-hair conjecture are known to exist in the Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs field theory. The onset of hair formation in this composed physical system is marked by the presence of `cloudy' black-hole spacetimes, magnetically charged Reissner-Nordström black holes that support spatially regular linearized field configurations. In the present paper we explore, using {\it analytical} techniques, the physical and mathematical properties of the composed black-hole-linearized-field bound-state configurations of the Einstein-Yang-Mills-Higgs theory. In particular, we derive a remarkably compact analytical formula for the discrete resonance spectrum $\{\mu_k\}^{k=\infty}_{k=1}$ of the dimensionless mass-radius parameter $\mu\equiv m_{\text{w}} r_+$ that characterizes the cloudy black-hole-field configurations in the regime $1\ll r_+\ll m^{-1}_{\text{w}}$ of small-mass weakly-magnetized black holes (here $r_+$ is the horizon radius of the central supporting black hole and $m_{\text{w}}$ is the mass of the Yang-Mills field due to the Higgs effect). Interestingly, our results reveal that the excited black-hole-field resonances are characterized by the asymptotic $k$-independent dimensionless ratio ${\mu_{k+1}/{\mu_{k}}}=e^{-{{2\pi}/{\sqrt{3}}}}$.

</details>


### [50] [A new toric ALF gravitational instanton](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03221)
*Alfie Jones*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper constructs a new explicit toric ALF gravitational instanton with χ=4, τ=1, asymptotic topology L(3,1), derived from the Belinski–Zakharov four-soliton solution; it is smooth, positive definite, and the first known non-Hermitian ALF (non-asymptotically flat) gravitational instanton.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct the first explicit example of a non-Hermitian ALF (asymptotically locally flat but not asymptotically flat) gravitational instanton, expanding the known landscape beyond Hermitian or AF cases.

Method: Constructs the instanton explicitly using toric geometry and the Belinski–Zakharov inverse scattering method applied to a four-soliton solution; verifies smoothness and positive definiteness via axis analysis and metric regularity checks.

Result: A one-parameter family of smooth, positive-definite toric ALF gravitational instantons with Euler characteristic χ = 4, Hirzebruch signature τ = 1, and asymptotic lens space topology L(3,1).

Conclusion: This work provides the first explicit non-Hermitian ALF gravitational instanton, demonstrating existence beyond Hermitian or AF constraints and enriching the classification of Ricci-flat 4-manifolds with ALF asymptotics.

Abstract: We construct a new explicit toric ALF gravitational instanton. The instanton has Euler characteristic $\chi = 4$ and Hirzebruch signature $\tau = 1$. The topology at infinity is a lens space $L(3,1)$. The solution depends on one parameter and is constructed from the Belinski--Zakharov four-soliton solution. We prove that the instanton is smooth and positive definite on and away from the axis. This is the first example of a non-Hermitian ALF (not AF) gravitational instanton.

</details>


### [51] [Exact Regular Planar Black Holes solutions in Bumblebee gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03338)
*Daniil Stepanenko*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores exact regular planar black hole solutions in bumblebee gravity, considering temporal and radial bumblebee field components and quadratic/linear potentials with zero/nonzero cosmological constants; it derives parameter constraints, obtains analytic planar deformation functions, and confirms singularity-free behavior at r=0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct singularity-free planar black hole solutions in bumblebee gravity—a Lorentz-violating extension of general relativity—by leveraging nonminimal coupling and specific bumblebee field configurations.

Method: Solves the full field equations of bumblebee gravity for planar symmetry, assuming temporal and radial bumblebee vector components; analyzes quadratic and linear bumblebee potentials, with and without cosmological constant; determines consistency conditions for nonminimal coupling and parameter relations.

Result: Derives exact analytical solutions for regular planar black holes characterized by a closed-form planar deformation function; confirms absence of curvature singularities at r = 0.

Conclusion: Regular planar black holes exist in bumblebee gravity under specified potential and coupling choices; the solutions are fully analytic and nonsingular, supporting the viability of Lorentz-violating gravity as a framework for resolving spacetime singularities.

Abstract: We discuss possibilities of finding exact regular planar black hole solutions in bumblebee gravity with temporal and radial components of the bumblebee field. We consider quadratic and linear potentials, with vanishing and nonzero cosmological constants, respectively. The field equations determine the nonminimal coupling and the relations between the model parameters. Found solution produce regular planar black hole with planar deformation function. Analytical expressions for this functions has been found. This solutions free of singularities at the $r=0$.

</details>


### [52] [Exact static two-body gravitational potential at first self-force order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03511)
*Ricardo Monteiro*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives a closed-form expression for the static first-self-force correction to the gravitational interaction potential between two non-spinning point masses, extending post-Newtonian calculations beyond previous limits using effective field theory and Schwarzschild-based linearised gravity in harmonic gauge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond the probe (zeroth-self-force) limit and compute the first finite-mass correction—quadratic in the light mass—to the static two-body interaction potential in general relativity, which is essential for high-precision gravitational waveform modeling and post-Newtonian theory.

Method: Constructs the Green function for linearised Einstein equations on a Schwarzschild background in harmonic gauge; fixes homogeneous modes and regularised coincident limit via dimensional continuation and matching to point-source effective field theory; computes the first-self-force correction exactly and expands it in the weak-field (post-Newtonian) limit.

Result: A closed-form expression for the static first-self-force correction quadratic in the light mass; its all-orders weak-field expansion; full determination of all static post-Newtonian coefficients in the linear-in-heavy-mass, quadratic-in-light-mass sector; consistency with known lower-order PN coefficients.

Conclusion: The first-self-force correction to the static two-body potential is analytically tractable and fully determined; this provides a foundational result for incorporating finite-size and self-force effects into high-accuracy post-Newtonian and effective-one-body models.

Abstract: The static interaction potential of two non-spinning point masses in general relativity was previously computed through seventh post-Newtonian order using methods of effective field theory. Its probe limit, or zeroth-self-force contribution, is determined exactly by the Schwarzschild geometry. We derive a closed-form expression for the static first-self-force correction, quadratic in the light mass, and establish its weak-field expansion to all orders. Working in harmonic gauge, we construct the required Green function for the coupled linearised gravitational equations about the Schwarzschild background. We fix its homogeneous contributions and regularised coincident limit by matching to the dimensionally continued point-source theory. The result reproduces known perturbative coefficients and determines all higher static post-Newtonian coefficients in this mass sector.

</details>


### [53] [The structured pseudospectrum of Schwarzschild black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00853)
*Liang-Bi Wu,Yu-Sen Zhou,Ming-Fei Ji,Xia-Yuan Liu,Libo Xie,Li-Ming Cao*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces the structured pseudospectrum—incorporating physically admissible perturbations—to analyze instability of black hole quasinormal modes, revealing anisotropic (elliptical) contours in contrast to isotropic circular conventional pseudospectra.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional pseudospectrum analysis allows unphysical, arbitrary operator perturbations; the authors aim to restrict perturbations to those consistent with effective field theory and the Rezzolla–Zhidenko parametrization to assess realistic spectral instability of black hole QNMs.

Method: The authors define two structured perturbation spaces (EFT-based and Rezzolla–Zhidenko–based), compute their structured pseudospectra using two distinct norms, and perform numerical analysis of quasinormal mode instability under these physically constrained perturbations.

Result: Physical constraints significantly suppress spectrum instability—especially for higher overtones—and induce anisotropic, approximately elliptical pseudospectral contours around isolated QNMs at small ε, with orientation depending on both mode index and perturbation space.

Conclusion: Structured pseudospectra provide a more physically meaningful measure of QNM instability than conventional pseudospectra, exposing previously hidden anisotropy and reducing predicted instability under realistic perturbations.

Abstract: The non-Hermitian nature of black hole quasinormal modes (QNMs) can give rise to remarkable spectrum instability. Such instability is naturally characterized by the pseudospectrum for the operator. However, conventional pseudospectrum analyses permit arbitrary operator perturbations that may not correspond to physically admissible modifications. In this work, we introduce the structured pseudospectrum into black hole perturbation theory. We construct two physically motivated perturbation spaces based on effective field theory and the Rezzolla--Zhidenko parametrization, and get their structured pseudospectra using two distinct norms. Our numerical results show that imposing physical constraints substantially reduces the accessible spectrum instability, especially for higher overtones. More importantly, at sufficiently small perturbation scales $\varepsilon$, the structured pseudospectrum around isolated QNMs exhibits approximately elliptical contours with preferred directions that depend on both the mode and the perturbation space, revealing an anisotropic structure beyond the familiar circular behavior of the conventional pseudospectrum.

</details>


### [54] [$C^0$-inextendibility of a class of Vaidya spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02133)
*Karim Mosani,Clemens Sämann,Kharanshu N. Solanki*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves that certain ingoing Vaidya spacetimes with positive mass and satisfying the weak energy condition are future $C^0$-inextendible, extending Sbierski's techniques from Schwarzschild.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish generic $C^0$-inextendibility of black hole interiors beyond Schwarzschild, particularly for dynamical black holes modeled by Vaidya spacetimes.

Method: Adapts and generalizes Sbierski's geometric and causal analysis techniques—particularly curve-length comparison and construction of inextendible timelike curves—to the ingoing Vaidya class under the weak energy condition.

Result: A broad subclass of ingoing Vaidya spacetimes with positive, non-decreasing mass function satisfying the weak energy condition is shown to be future $C^0$-inextendible.

Conclusion: Future $C^0$-inextendibility—a strong form of singularity robustness—holds generically for dynamical black hole interiors described by ingoing Vaidya metrics, supporting the expectation that strong cosmic censorship holds in low regularity.

Abstract: We prove the future $C^0$-inextendibility of a broad subclass of ingoing Vaidya spacetimes with positive mass function satisfying the weak energy condition, building on methods introduced and developed by Sbierski for the Schwarzschild spacetime.

</details>


### [55] [Remark About Action for Rastall Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00136)
*J. Kluson*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This short note reformulates Rastall gravity (as proposed by Golovnev) into a reparametrization-invariant action and derives the associated equations of motion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a reparametrization-invariant formulation of Rastall gravity, enabling a more geometrically consistent and covariant treatment of the theory.

Method: Reformulation of Golovnev's Rastall gravity action to achieve reparametrization invariance; derivation of the corresponding Euler–Lagrange equations of motion.

Result: A reparametrization-invariant action for Rastall gravity is obtained, and its explicit equations of motion are determined.

Conclusion: The reparametrization-invariant formulation provides a robust foundation for further theoretical analysis and potential physical interpretation of Rastall gravity.

Abstract: In this short note we study Rastall gravity action as was proposed by Golovnev in [7]. We construct its reparametrization invariant form and determine corresponding equations of motion.

</details>


### [56] [Strong-coupling of Minkowski spacetime in $f(R)$ gravity: a Hamiltonian perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00413)
*Aldair Pantoja*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper performs a Hamiltonian analysis of f(R) gravity in the perturbative regime, identifying constraint structures and counting physical degrees of freedom around Minkowski spacetime, confirming prior results on its spectrum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously determine the number and nature of physical degrees of freedom in f(R) gravity via canonical (Hamiltonian) analysis, especially around Minkowski background, resolving ambiguities in higher-order theories.

Method: Perturbative expansion of the f(R) action around a constant-curvature background; restriction to maximally symmetric spaces; specialization to Minkowski (R₀ = 0); systematic Dirac-Bergmann Hamiltonian formalism for higher-derivative systems; classification and analysis of primary and secondary constraints.

Result: Complete constraint structure is identified for various cases depending on f(R) and its derivatives at R₀; consistent degree-of-freedom count (three: two massless tensor + one massive scalar) is obtained around Minkowski; spectrum matches previous linearized analyses.

Conclusion: The Hamiltonian analysis confirms that f(R) gravity propagates three degrees of freedom around Minkowski spacetime—two from the massless graviton and one from a massive scalar mode—validating its well-posedness as a consistent extension of GR in the weak-field limit.

Abstract: A Hamiltonian analysis of $f(R)$ gravity in the perturbation field approximation is performed. The action principle is presented as a series expansion around a generic fixed background with constant scalar curvature $R_{0}$, then we restrict the theory to maximally symmetric spaces, later we recast the action by selecting $R_{0}=0$, i.e., by choosing Minkowski as the background. The Hamiltonian formulation is constructed by following the appropriate methods to approach higher-order systems. The constraint structure is identified for several cases that arise depending on imposed conditions on the values of the function $f(R)$ and its derivatives, classification of constraints allows to make a proper count of degrees of freedom for each case, at the end we confirm the spectrum behaviour of $f(R)$ gravity around Minkowski spacetime that has been reported by another means in the recent literature.

</details>


### [57] [Charged Gravastars with Casimir Energy in Quadratic f(T) Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00706)
*S. R. Harshitha,C. S. Varsha,V. Venkatesha,Phalaksha Murthy B*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a charged gravastar model in modified teleparallel gravity (f(T) = T + αT²), using Casimir vacuum energy for the interior, an ultra-relativistic shell (p = ρ), and an exterior Reissner–Nordström spacetime; junction conditions yield finite, smooth shell properties, and charge/torsion/vacuum interplay is systematically analyzed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quantum vacuum effects (Casimir energy), electric charge, and torsional gravity modifications (via f(T) gravity) jointly influence stable, horizonless compact objects—specifically gravastars—as alternatives to black holes.

Method: Solves the static, spherically symmetric f(T) field equations with electromagnetic contribution; constructs a three-layer gravastar (Casimir interior, ultra-relativistic shell, Reissner–Nordström exterior); applies Israel–Darmois junction conditions to determine shell surface energy density and pressure; analyzes proper length, shell energy, and entropy as functions of shell thickness and charge.

Result: Finite and smooth profiles for surface energy density and pressure across the shell; regular monotonic behavior of proper length, total shell energy, and entropy with increasing shell thickness; explicit dependence of all physical quantities on both charge Q and torsion coupling α.

Conclusion: The model demonstrates self-consistent, physically viable charged gravastars in f(T) gravity, highlighting nontrivial synergy among quantum vacuum, electromagnetism, and torsion-based gravitational modifications—supporting gravastars as robust alternatives to singular black hole solutions in extended gravity theories.

Abstract: We investigate a charged gravastar configuration in the framework of modified teleparallel gravity, considering the quadratic form $f(T)=T+\alpha T^{2}$ and incorporating Casimir vacuum energy as a source for the interior region. The gravastar is constructed as a three-region configuration consisting of a Casimir-energy dominated vacuum interior, an intermediate thin shell of ultra-relativistic matter satisfying the equation of state $p=\rho$, and an exterior Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime. The modified field equations are derived for a static, spherically symmetric geometry, and the interior and shell solutions are obtained by incorporating the contributions of Casimir energy and the electromagnetic field. The matching between the interior and exterior geometries is performed using the Israel-Darmois junction conditions, from which the surface energy density and pressure of the shell are determined. We examine the physical properties of the configuration through the proper length, shell energy, and entropy. The influence of the charge parameter on these quantities is also investigated. The resulting profiles remain finite and smooth across the shell, while the proper length, energy, and entropy exhibit regular behaviour with increasing shell thickness. The model provides a framework for studying the interplay between quantum vacuum effects, electric charge, and torsional modifications of gravity in compact-object configurations.

</details>


### [58] [Absorption and scattering of massless scalar waves by black holes with scalar hair](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00709)
*Lina Zhang,Yun Soo Myung,Yu-Xuan Wang,Xufen Zhang,Ming Zhang,De-Cheng Zou*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates scalar wave absorption and scattering by normal and phantom Bakopoulos--Nakas black holes using partial-wave analysis, revealing distinct effects of scalar charge $q$ and coupling parameter $\xi$ on horizon area, capture cross section, low- and high-frequency spectral behavior, and interference patterns—differing notably from Reissner–Nordström black holes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how scalar charge and non-minimal coupling ($\xi = 5$ vs. $\xi = -2$) affect wave dynamics—particularly absorption and scattering—around exotic black hole solutions beyond general relativity, and to compare them with classical charged black holes (e.g., Reissner–Nordström).

Method: Partial-wave analysis of massless scalar field propagation; computation of absorption cross sections (low-frequency limit → horizon area; photon-sphere scaling); scattering cross sections including glory and geometric capture regimes; comparison across scalar charge $q/M$, branches ($\xi=5$: normal; $\xi=-2$: phantom), and benchmark against Reissner–Nordström black holes.

Result: In the normal branch ($\xi=5$), increasing $q/M$ reduces horizon area and geometric capture cross section; in the phantom branch ($\xi=-2$), both increase. Low-frequency absorption approaches horizon area asymptotically; photon-sphere scaling unifies high-frequency absorption spectra. Scattering exhibits branch- and charge-dependent interference fringes that narrow at higher frequencies. Finite-frequency discrepancies persist even when matching capture/glory scales with Reissner–Nordström black holes.

Conclusion: Scalar charge and coupling sign fundamentally alter wave–black-hole interaction: phantom black holes behave oppositely to normal ones in geometric and absorption properties, and neither reproduces Reissner–Nordström scattering fully—even under scale-matching—highlighting intrinsic differences due to non-minimal scalar coupling.

Abstract: We use partial waves to study massless scalar absorption and scattering by the normal ($\xi=5$) and phantom ($\xi=-2$) Bakopoulos--Nakas black holes, described by mass $M$ and scalar charge $q$. At fixed mass $M=1$, increasing $q/M$ decreases the horizon area and geometric capture cross section in the normal branch, but it increases both in the phantom branch. The low-frequency absorption tends to the horizon area, while photon-sphere scaling brings the absorption spectra close together. In scattering picture, charge and branch change the interference pattern, and higher frequencies make the fringes narrow. However, matching the capture or glory scale of the normal branch with Reissner--Nordström black holes indicates finite-frequency differences.

</details>


### [59] [Near-extremal critical dynamics driven by a phantom field in Einstein-Maxwell-scalar theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00792)
*Zhen-Kun Jia,Lang Zhao,Lin Chen,Peng-Cheng Li,Cheng-Yong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper uses a phantom (negative-energy) field to dynamically access near-extremal Einstein-Maxwell-scalar black hole solutions, revealing a critical threshold $p_*$ separating evolution toward near-extremal Reissner–Nordström or nonextremal hairy black holes, with Type I critical behavior (logarithmic scaling of residence time) and a stable, nonsingular scalarized intermediate state.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore the low-mass, near-extremal region of Einstein-Maxwell-scalar (EMS) black hole solutions—a regime inaccessible via standard positive-energy perturbations—by enabling controlled negative-energy transport.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations of spherically symmetric EMS black holes with an added phantom scalar field; initial amplitude $p$ of the phantom field is tuned across a critical value $p_*$ while other initial data parameters are fixed; quasi-local mass and horizon quantities (surface gravity, curvature) are tracked during evolution.

Result: A critical threshold $p_*$ is identified where sub- and super-critical evolutions both pass through a long-lived, scalarized intermediate state characterized by small surface gravity and finite horizon curvature (no singularity); residence time near this state obeys Type I critical scaling $\tau \sim -\kappa \ln|p - p_*|$; departure growth rate matches inverse lifetime coefficient; final states bifurcate into either near-extremal 'bald' (RN) or nonextremal 'hairy' black holes.

Conclusion: Phantom-assisted negative-energy transport provides a viable dynamical pathway into the near-extremal EMS solution space and uncovers a novel threshold-mediated phase transition between bald and hairy black-hole end states, governed by Type I critical phenomena without horizon singularities.

Abstract: We investigate the fully nonlinear dynamics of Einstein-Maxwell-scalar black holes in the low-mass, near-extremal region of the solution space, which is difficult to access with conventional positive-energy perturbations. An independent phantom field is introduced as a controllable negative-energy channel, and its initial amplitude is varied while the remaining parameters of the initial data are held fixed. The phantom contribution is concentrated during the early-time infall and reduces the quasi-local mass in the black-hole region at fixed electric charge, thereby allowing the evolution to approach near-extremal configurations. Near a critical amplitude $p_\ast$, evolutions on both sides of the threshold enter the same long-lived scalarized intermediate stage before separating toward either a near-extremal Reissner-Nordstrom black hole or a nonextremal hairy black hole. The residence time near this intermediate stage obeys the logarithmic scaling law of Type I critical behavior, and the growth rate extracted from the departure is consistent with the inverse lifetime coefficient. The intermediate stage is characterized by a small horizon surface gravity and finite horizon curvature, with no curvature blow-up resolved at the horizon during the simulated interval. These results demonstrate that phantom-assisted negative-energy transport provides a dynamical route into the near-extremal sector of the EMS solution space and reveals a threshold-mediated transition between bald and hairy black-hole end states.

</details>


### [60] [Dimensional dependence of critical phenomena in black hole interiors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00829)
*Li-Jie Xin,Xiangdong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically studies how scalar field perturbations affect the inner horizon of D-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black holes, finding a universal power-law scaling of the inner horizon radius near a critical perturbation amplitude, with exponent γ ≈ 1/(D−2).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the stability and structure of the inner (Cauchy) horizon in higher-dimensional charged black holes under nonlinear scalar perturbations, and to probe universality of critical phenomena near the threshold of inner horizon destruction.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations of spherically symmetric, asymptotically flat D-dimensional Reissner-Nordström black holes perturbed by a real massless scalar field; systematic variation of initial scalar amplitude A and charge Q to locate critical behavior and extract scaling exponents.

Result: A critical amplitude A* exists such that for A < A*, the inner horizon persists with radius r_s ∝ |A − A*|^γ; γ ≈ 1/2 in D = 4 and γ ≈ 1/(D−2) for 4 ≤ D ≤ 9; scaling implies |A − A*| ∝ area of inner horizon cross-section.

Conclusion: The inner horizon exhibits a universal critical phenomenon governed by dimensional scaling, suggesting geometric origin of the critical exponent and reinforcing the role of horizon area as the relevant physical scale near criticality.

Abstract: We numerically investigate the interior dynamics of $D$ dimensional Reissner-Nordström (RN) black holes perturbed by a real scalar field. Strong perturbations destroy the null inner horizon and produce a spacelike singularity, whereas weaker perturbations preserve it with an areal radius that decreases as the perturbation amplitude increases. As the initial scalar field amplitude $A$ approaches its critical value $A^{*}$, the inner horizon radius decreases continuously to zero. Near criticality, this radius obeys the power law $r_{s}\propto|A-A^{*}|^\gamma$. In four dimensions, different initial scalar field profiles and initial RN black hole charges yield nearly identical critical exponents, $\gamma\simeq1/2$, supporting the universality of the scaling law. For $4\leq D\leq9$, the numerical results are well described by $\gamma\simeq1/(D-2)$. The corresponding scaling, $|A-A^{*}|\propto r_{s}^{D-2}$, indicates that the deviation of the initial parameter from its critical value is proportional to the area of the spherical cross section of the inner horizon.

</details>


### [61] [Probing Hidden Dimensions with Multimessenger Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00980)
*Hanlin Song,Minghui Du,Peng Xu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates how gravitational waves (GWs) from binary coalescences can probe compact extra dimensions in a Kaluza–Klein framework, derives Newtonian dynamics and leading-order extra-dimensional corrections to GW energy flux and waveforms within the ppE formalism, incorporates 1PN amplitude corrections from internal volume perturbations, constructs modified waveform templates, and uses multimessenger Bayesian analysis of GW170817 + EM data to constrain the phenomenological dimensional parameter n < 0.11 (95% C.L.), with Taiji simulations suggesting n < 0.00178.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally test whether 4D spacetime is embedded in higher dimensions using strong-field GW dynamics — a regime where extra-dimensional signatures may be amplified and distinguishable from GR predictions.

Method: Derives conservative Newtonian binary dynamics and leading-order extra-dimensional correction to GW energy flux in a compactified Kaluza–Klein model; formulates waveform modifications in the parameterized post-Einsteinian (ppE) framework; combines with known 1PN amplitude corrections from internal volume perturbations; constructs detector-specific (ground- and space-based) modified waveform templates; performs multimessenger Bayesian inference using GW170817 strain data and independent electromagnetic constraints; simulates Taiji observations of massive black hole binaries.

Result: Multimessenger analysis of GW170817 yields 95% credible upper bound n < 0.11 on the ppE dimensional parameter; Taiji simulations for massive BH binaries improve this bound to n < 0.00178 — nearly two orders of magnitude tighter.

Conclusion: Multimessenger GW observations provide a powerful probe of extra-dimensional gravity in the strong-field, dynamical regime; current and future detectors, especially space-based ones like Taiji, can significantly tighten constraints on compactified extra dimensions beyond current limits.

Abstract: The dimensionality of spacetime is a fundamental property of nature, yet whether our observed four-dimensional spacetime is embedded in a higher-dimensional geometry remains experimentally unresolved. Gravitational waves (GWs) offer a direct probe of compact extra dimensions through the strong-field dynamics of binary coalescences. In this work, we investigate extra-dimensional modifications to GW generation within a compactified Kaluza--Klein framework. We derive the Newtonian conservative binary dynamics and the leading-order extra-dimensional correction to the GW energy flux and formulate the corresponding waveform modifications within the parameterized post-Einsteinian (ppE) framework.
