Combining these results with the previously derived 1PN amplitude correction arising from perturbations of the internal volume, we construct modified GW waveform templates for ground- and space-based detectors. To alleviate parameter degeneracies in GW parameter estimation, we combine the observed strain data of GW170817 with independent electromagnetic observations in a multimessenger Bayesian analysis, yielding a 95\% credible upper bound of $n<0.11$ on the phenomenological dimensional parameter. We further investigate injected multimessenger massive black hole binary observations with Taiji, finding that the upper bound could be improved by nearly two orders of magnitude to $n<0.00178$. These results highlight the potential of multimessenger GW observations to probe extra-dimensional effects and test fundamental gravity in the strong-field and dynamical regime.

</details>


### [62] [Strong gravity physics of dynamical horizons in black hole mergers and its imprint in their gravitational radiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01275)
*Vaishak Prasad*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This thesis establishes a direct geometric correspondence between black hole horizon dynamics and gravitational waveforms in binary mergers, revealing horizon shear mimics the inspiral chirp, non-vanishing dynamical horizon Love numbers with universal behavior, strong correlation between horizon multipole moments and radiation at null infinity, and quasi-normal ringing signatures in the common horizon’s absorption of gravitational radiation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between the observed gravitational waveform and the underlying black hole horizon geometry across inspiral, merger, and ringdown phases—particularly lacking a detailed understanding of how horizon structure maps to waveform features.

Method: Analysis of numerical relativity simulations of binary black hole mergers; computation of horizon shear, dynamical horizon source multipole moments, and horizon Love numbers (both axisymmetric and non-axisymmetric); comparison with gravitational waveforms at null infinity and final Kerr geometry; tracking of outer common dynamical horizon evolution.

Result: 1) Horizon shear replicates the inspiral chirp waveform in frequency/amplitude evolution with consistent parameter estimation; 2) Dynamical horizon Love numbers are non-zero, exhibit universality for modest mass ratios, and extend meaningfully to non-axisymmetric modes; 3) Horizon multipole moments strongly correlate with radiation at ℐ⁺; 4) The outer common dynamical horizon absorbs gravitational radiation with quasi-normal ringing signatures and converges toward the final Kerr state, though anomalies and inconsistencies in its field structure persist.

Conclusion: Black hole horizons are not passive boundaries but active, information-rich geometric entities whose dynamics directly encode and mirror gravitational radiation. The horizon’s shear, multipole structure, and response to radiation provide a coherent geometric interpretation of the full coalescence waveform—challenging the notion that black hole 'tidal deformability' vanishes and opening avenues for horizon-based waveform modeling and tests of general relativity.

Abstract: In a binary black hole merger, the inspiral portion of the gravitational waveform corresponds to two distinct horizons orbiting each other, and the merger and ringdown signals correspond to the final horizon being formed and settling down to equilibrium. However, we still lack a detailed understanding of the relation between the horizon geometry in these three regimes and the observed waveform. In this thesis, we show that the well-known inspiral chirp waveform has a clear counterpart on black hole horizons, namely the shear of the outgoing null rays at the horizon. The shear behaves very much like a compact binary coalescence waveform with increasing frequency and amplitude, and the parameters of the system estimated from the horizon agree with those estimated from the waveform. We then track the source multipole moments of the dynamical horizons in simulations of several binary black hole mergers and calculate the corresponding Love numbers for axisymmetric modes. Unlike the (field) tidal Love numbers of non-spinning black holes, these do not vanish. We present evidence that, at least for modest mass ratios, their behaviour is universal. We extend this to the non-axisymmetric moments and show that the dynamical horizons encode detailed information about the dynamics of the binary, and that their source multipole moments are strongly correlated with the gravitational field at null infinity. Finally, we study the outer common dynamical horizon formed during the merger and its relationship with the outgoing gravitational waves, and show how the deformed common horizon approaches the final Kerr state by absorbing gravitational radiation with signatures of quasi-normal ringing. Its gravitational field shares a similar multipolar structure as that at future null infinity, and we point out some limitations, inconsistencies, and anomalies that need to be addressed.

</details>


### [63] [Gravitational dynamics and backward ray-traced shadows of bumblebee AdS black holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01413)
*Muhammad Yasir,Senyue Lou,Zelin Zhang,Abdul Jawad,Faren Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies photon frequency shifts, ISCO properties, and black hole shadows in bumblebee AdS black hole spacetimes, linking bumblebee-induced Lorentz violation to observable astrophysical signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observational consequences of Lorentz symmetry breaking induced by the bumblebee field in anti-de Sitter black hole spacetimes, particularly its impact on photon redshift/blueshift, orbital stability, and black hole shadow morphology.

Method: Analytical derivation of geodesic equations and effective potential for circular orbits; computation of total gravitational redshift/blueshift for photons from accretion disk; numerical backward ray tracing using Arcmancer to simulate black hole shadows; analysis of cosmological anisotropy generation via bumblebee vacuum expectation value.

Result: Bumblebee parameter modifies ISCO radius and total frequency shift relative to standard AdS BHs; anisotropic background selects preferred direction affecting photon propagation; shadow size and deformation depend quantitatively on bumblebee field strength and orientation.

Conclusion: The bumblebee AdS black hole yields distinct, potentially observable deviations in spectral shifts and shadow features, offering a pathway to constrain Lorentz-violating parameters using future high-resolution VLBI or X-ray timing observations.

Abstract: We investigate the total observed frequency shift of photons emitted by massive geodesic particles in circular orbits near the event horizon of bumblebee AdS black holes. The cosmological effects of the bumblebee field are investigated through dynamical equations governing the evolution and characteristic features of the Universe. In addition, the bumblebee field generates cosmological anisotropies by acting as a source of anisotropy and selecting a preferred spatial direction. We formulate the characteristic equations for the innermost stable circular orbit using the effective potential approach. Further analysis is devoted to establishing a functional relationship between the BH mass and the observed redshift and blueshift of photons emitted from the accretion disk. Finally, we study the shadow cast by the bumblebee AdS BH using the backward ray tracing process implemented in the Arcmancer code, where photon geodesics are initialized at the observers location and integrated backward in time to generate BH images, typically illuminated by electromagnetic radiation from nearby accretion flows.

</details>


### [64] [Effects of radiation on the Laser Interferometer Lunar Antenna (LILA)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01499)
*James M. Trippe,Brett N. Shapiro,Chloe A. Champagne,Brian D. Sierawski,Michael D. Hu,Volker Quetschke,Teviet Creighton,Philippe Lognonné,Mark P. Panning,John W. Conklin,Robert A. Reed,Karan Jani*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper evaluates radiation-induced noise sources (test-mass charging, momentum transfer, thermoelastic deformation) for the Laser Interferometer Lunar Antenna (LILA) on the Moon, using Geant4, electrostatic modeling, and thermoelastic kernels; it finds that with charge-aware design—including balanced electrostatic actuation and continuous current compensation—radiation noise remains subdominant in LILA’s sensitivity budget, even under extreme solar flare conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether radiation-induced disturbances (e.g., from cosmic rays, solar particles, lunar albedo neutrons) impose fundamental limits on the sensitivity of LILA—a proposed lunar-based midband gravitational-wave detector—and to inform robust test-mass and actuation design.

Method: Quantitative noise modeling combining: (1) Geant4 Monte Carlo simulations of particle transport to estimate energy deposition and charging rates; (2) finite-element electrostatic force mapping for charge-to-force coupling under two voltage configurations (uniform vs. balanced drive); (3) spatial thermoelastic response kernel to compute displacement from localized heating; (4) comparison across shielding thicknesses (50–1000 mil Al-equivalent) and radiation environments (solar-minimum GCR + albedo neutrons vs. 1989 solar flare SEP spectrum).

Result: Under charge-aware design—specifically balanced electrode actuation (B) and ideal continuous compensation of mean charging current—coherent charge accumulation is suppressed; discharge shot noise is negligible; charge-related force noise dominates below ~10 mHz, while thermoelastic noise dominates above ~100 mHz; radiation-induced noise remains subdominant to other LILA noise sources even during worst-case solar flares.

Conclusion: Radiation noise is not a limiting factor for LILA’s midband sensitivity if test-mass electrostatic actuation and shielding are properly designed—validating the feasibility of high-precision GW metrology on the lunar surface.

Abstract: The Moon provides a promising environment for gravitational-wave (GW) observations in the midband. The Laser Interferometer Lunar Antenna (LILA) combines a lunar deformation channel (LDC) and a geodesic deformation channel (GDC) to cover the midband. The LDC uses the Moon's resonant response to GW for the lower portion of the midband while the GDC measures displacement of suspended test masses to measure the latter. Radiation on the lunar surface can produce spurious signals through test-mass charging, momentum transfer, and thermoelastic deformation. We estimate these contributions for a notional 100 kg fused-silica optic using Geant4 transport, a finite-element electrostatic force map, and a spatial thermoelastic response kernel. Solar-minimum galactic cosmic rays and lunar albedo neutrons define a background case; a worst-day solar energetic particle spectrum (1989 solar flare conditions) defines an extreme, but rare, case. Aluminum-equivalent shielding from 50 to 1000 mils (1.27 to 25.4 mm) and two electrostatic actuator voltage configurations are compared. In the uniform-charge approximation, balanced drive with electrode configuration (B) substantially reduces linear charge-to-force coupling. Ideal continuous compensation of the mean charging current suppresses coherent charge accumulation while introducing negligible discharge shot noise. With compensation, charge-related noise dominates the low-frequency response, whereas thermoelastic deformation dominates at higher frequencies. Accumulated-dose and photodetector transient estimates identify additional component-qualification considerations. Estimates indicate that even under solar flare conditions, radiation noise is not a significant contributor to LILA's overall noise budget as long as charge-aware designs are followed.

</details>


### [65] [Choosing a generator for streaming Gaussian noise in gravitational-wave mock data: accuracy, cost and reproducibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01624)
*Isaac C. F. Wong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper evaluates noise generation methods for gravitational-wave mock data challenges, finding that crossfading long (≥256 s) independently drawn windows achieves the best trade-off between spectral accuracy (covariance error <10⁻³), throughput (~4.7 M samples/s), and low memory footprint (~hundreds of bytes), especially when the target power spectral density is smoothly continued below the analysis band; other methods (overlap-save FIR, AR/ARMA) underperform in accuracy-throughput Pareto fronts.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Gravitational-wave mock data challenges require long stretches of colored Gaussian noise matching prescribed detector spectra; existing generators vary in accuracy, speed, memory, and reproducibility, necessitating a systematic comparison using a rigorous metric—the covariance error against the target spectrum over the analysis band.

Method: The authors analytically derive and numerically evaluate the covariance error of five noise generation methods—crossfade of independent windows, overlap-save FIR filtering, autoregressive (AR), autoregressive moving-average (ARMA), and PyCBC’s reproducible filter—against target spectra for eleven detector curves (e.g., aLIGO O3/O4, Virgo, KAGRA design); they also benchmark throughput, state size, and bit-exact regeneration cost, distinguishing cases where the target spectrum is truncated vs. smoothly continued below the analysis band.

Result: Crossfade with ≥256 s windows achieves covariance error <10⁻³ across all eleven detector curves at ~4.7 M samples/s with minimal state; overlap-save FIR is faster but requires tens of thousands of taps for only ~few-percent accuracy; no AR/ARMA configuration lies on the accuracy–throughput Pareto front; truncating the target at the band edge degrades error to >0.5 for all methods on aLIGO O4 and five others; cyclostationarity of crossfade contributes significantly to error only at short windows (8 s) on select curves.

Conclusion: The crossfade method with sufficiently long windows (as tabulated per detector curve) and smooth continuation of the target spectrum below the analysis band is recommended as the optimal choice for high-fidelity, efficient, and reproducible gravitational-wave noise generation in mock data challenges.

Abstract: Mock data challenges need long stretches of colored Gaussian noise with a prescribed spectrum. The candidates are the crossfade of independently drawn windows that the standard gravitational-wave libraries implement, overlap-save finite-impulse-response filtering, autoregressive and autoregressive moving-average (ARMA) models, and the reproducible filter of PyCBC. One measure ranks them all: the covariance error against the target over the analysis band, evaluated from each generator's algebra; generated noise only checks the implementations. We also measure throughput, retained state and the cost of bit-exact regeneration. With the target continued smoothly below the band, the crossfade with windows of 256 s or more reaches an error below $10^{-3}$ on all eleven detector curves as tabulated, at about 4.7 million samples per second from a state of a few hundred bytes. An overlap-save filter is faster but needs tens of thousands of taps for an error of a few percent, and no tested autoregressive or ARMA configuration lies on the accuracy--throughput fronts. An error of $10^{-3}$ needs a window of 8 to 235 s, longest on the design curves with narrow lines, where it also depends on the tabulation. With the target cut at the band edge, as current generators do by default, every method has an error above one half on the projected Advanced LIGO (aLIGO) O4 curve, and the crossfade on five other curves. The crossfade is cyclostationary. With the continued target, its cyclic part is the larger part of the error only at 8 s, on the aLIGO O4 and the Virgo and KAGRA design curves, and stays within a factor of two of the stationary part on the aLIGO O3 and the Virgo and KAGRA design curves. We recommend the crossfade with a window from the table of detector curves, and a smooth continuation of the target below the band.

</details>


### [66] [Spherically-symmetric solutions with internal Ricci-flat spaces in Brans-Dicke theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01691)
*K.K. Ernazarov*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs new exact spherically symmetric solutions in D-dimensional Brans-Dicke theory with n Ricci-flat internal spaces, derives horizon and regularity conditions, and analyzes photon spheres and ISCOs—showing that dynamical extra dimensions typically prevent regular horizons except in Schwarzschild or frozen-internal-space limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize known Brans-type and multidimensional black-hole solutions to D-dimensional Brans-Dicke gravity with Ricci-flat internal spaces, and to clarify how nonminimal scalar coupling affects horizon structure and geodesic motion in such geometries.

Method: Uses conformal transformation to Einstein frame, leverages known exact solution for Einstein gravity with minimally coupled scalar field, then transforms back to Jordan frame; performs causal analysis (horizon condition), Kretschmann-scalar curvature analysis for regularity, and computes photon-sphere and ISCO radii from geodesic equations in the 4D section.

Result: Explicit metric and Brans-Dicke scalar field expressions in Jordan frame; generalized algebraic exponent constraint; horizon condition nu >= 1 for R = L surface; generic absence of regular horizons when internal spaces are dynamical; analytic expressions for photon-sphere and ISCO radii in 4D section with Schwarzschild limits recovered.

Conclusion: Nonminimal Brans-Dicke coupling combined with dynamical Ricci-flat extra dimensions generically spoils horizon regularity—unlike the Schwarzschild or frozen-internal-space cases—highlighting a key qualitative departure from pure Einstein gravity and constraining viable multidimensional black-hole models.

Abstract: We construct a new family of exact spherically symmetric solutions in $D$-dimensional Brans-Dicke theory with $n$ internal Ricci-flat spaces. The spacetime is assumed to be a product of a four-dimensional spherical sector and $n$ Ricci-flat internal manifolds. By performing a conformal transformation to the Einstein frame, the problem is reduced to Einstein gravity with a minimally coupled scalar field, for which the exact solution is already known. Applying the inverse transformation to the Jordan frame yields explicit expressions for the metric and the Brans-Dicke scalar field, together with a generalized algebraic constraint on the exponents. The obtained solutions generalize the four-dimensional Brans solutions and the multidimensional solutions with Ricci-flat internal spaces. We analyze the causal structure of the four-dimensional section and derive the necessary condition for the surface $R=L$ to be an event horizon, $\nu \equiv \left|a+b+\frac12\sum_i a_iN_i\right| \ge 1$. A Kretschmann-scalar analysis shows that, apart from the Schwarzschild limit and the case of frozen internal spaces, a regular horizon is generically absent when the extra dimensions are dynamical. For the four-dimensional section we also obtain the photon-sphere and ISCO radii and discuss their Schwarzschild limits. The results clarify the role of nonminimal Brans-Dicke coupling in multidimensional black-hole geometries with Ricci-flat internal factors.

</details>


### [67] [Ricci-Non-metric Holographic Dark Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01766)
*Sukanta Bhunia,Prabir Rudra,Abhijit Lahiri*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a holographic dark energy model using a combination of Ricci scalar R and non-metricity scalar Q as the infrared cutoff, enabling a purely local, invariant-based description of cosmic evolution; it successfully reproduces all cosmological epochs (radiation → matter → dark energy) and exhibits flexible equation-of-state behavior (quintessence, phantom-divide crossing, or phantom).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a more fundamental and causally local holographic dark energy model by replacing conventional infrared cutoffs (e.g., Hubble or particle horizon) with geometric invariants—specifically the Ricci scalar R and non-metricity scalar Q—ensuring evolution depends only on current spacetime properties and enhancing theoretical robustness via diffeomorphism-invariant quantities.

Method: Formulated a holographic dark energy density $\rho_{\text{HDE}} \propto (\alpha R + \beta Q)^{-1}$, where $\alpha, \beta$ are dimensionless parameters; derived Friedmann equations in a symmetric teleparallel (STEGR-based) gravity framework incorporating non-metricity; solved background cosmology analytically/numerically to obtain evolution of $\Omega_{\text{HDE}}$, $w_{\text{HDE}}$, and $q$; constrained parameter space using observational consistency with standard cosmological phases.

Result: The model reproduces sequential radiation-dominated ($\Omega_r \sim 1$), matter-dominated ($\Omega_m \sim 1$), and dark-energy-dominated ($\Omega_{\text{HDE}} \sim 1$) eras; $w_{\text{HDE}}$ evolves from $\sim 1/3$ (radiation) to $0$ (matter) to values crossing $-1$ (phantom divide) or asymptoting to $<-1$, depending on $\alpha/\beta$; deceleration parameter $q$ transitions from $1/2$ (radiation) to $1/2$ (matter) to $-1$ (accelerated expansion); all parameters remain well-behaved without singularities.

Conclusion: The $R$–$Q$-based holographic dark energy model is a viable, geometrically grounded alternative that unifies infrared physics with fundamental spacetime invariants; it naturally accommodates cosmic phase transitions and diverse dark energy dynamics—including phantom behavior—without fine-tuning, supporting its use in extended gravity frameworks beyond general relativity.

Abstract: In this work we propose a holographic dark energy model inspired by the Ricci scalar $R$ and non-metricity scalar $Q$. The infrared cutoff of the holographic dark energy is described by a combination of the Ricci scalar and the non-metricity scalar. Such a structure is very helpful because the evolution is entirely dependent on the universe's current characteristics rather than its previous or future characteristics. Additionally, the theory becomes more fundamental due to the application of invariants. We have built the model and examined its cosmological characteristics. The behavior of several cosmological parameters, including the density parameter, equation of state parameter, and deceleration parameter, is examined. It is observed that the holographic dark energy model may depict every cosmological epoch, beginning with radiation in the early universe, moving on to matter, and concluding with the dark energy-dominated period in later times. Depending on the parameter spaces selected, the equation of state parameter demonstrates that the model may have quintessence nature, phantom-divide crossing, or even phantom nature in future times.

</details>


### [68] [Optical Characteristics and Frequency Shifts of Quartic Wormholes in $f(T,τ)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01811)
*Sourav Chaudhary,S. K. Maurya,Jitendra Kumar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs traversable wormhole solutions in $f(T,\tau)$ gravity using a quartic inverse-power density profile ($\rho \propto 1/r^4$), derives the exact shape function, confirms standard wormhole geometry (throat, flaring-out, asymptotics), identifies exotic matter via NEC violation and negative radial pressure at the throat, computes gravitational lensing via Gauss-Bonnet formalism showing sensitivity to parameters $\gamma$ and $\rho_0$, and analyzes perturbative stability (scalar/electromagnetic) and redshift behavior with a novel electromagnetic potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct and comprehensively characterize traversable wormhole solutions in $f(T,\tau)$ teleparallel gravity using a physically motivated quartic inverse-power energy density profile ($\rho \propto r^{-4}$), addressing geometry, exotic matter requirements, gravitational lensing, and stability.

Method: $f(T,\tau)$ gravity framework; exact integration of field equations with $\rho(r) = \rho_0/r^4$ to obtain shape function $b(r)$; geometric analysis (throat condition $b(r_0)=r_0$, flaring-out $b'(r_0)<1$, asymptotics); energy conditions (NEC) evaluation; Gauss-Bonnet theorem for deflection angle $\alpha$; linear perturbation analysis for scalar and electromagnetic fields; derivation of redshift function under a novel electromagnetic potential.

Result: Exact wormhole solution with $b(r)$ satisfying all geometric criteria; NEC violation and negative radial pressure localized at throat confirming exotic matter; analytical expression for deflection angle $\alpha$ dependent on $\gamma$ and $\rho_0$; stable perturbative behavior for scalar and EM fields; modified redshift profiles due to the introduced electromagnetic potential.

Conclusion: The quartic inverse-power density profile yields a consistent, stable, and observationally distinguishable traversable wormhole solution in $f(T,\tau)$ gravity, unifying geometric, matter, lensing, and perturbative properties—providing a concrete model for testing teleparallel gravity against exotic compact objects.

Abstract: This work explores traversable wormhole solutions characterized by a quartic inverse-power density profile within $f(T,{\tau})$ gravity. A specific inverse-power density profile, characterized by proportional to $1/r^4$ is introduced to establish the corresponding exact form of the shape function. The derived configuration exhibits all the necessary geometrical features of a traversable wormhole, such as throat formation, flaring-out behavior, and acceptable asymptotic characteristics. To gain further insight into the matter sector, we analyze the corresponding radial profiles of the energy density and pressure components. The throat region is characterised by negative radial pressure and null energy condition violation, both of which are relevant to the exotic matter content of the wormhole solution. To examine the lensing characteristics of the obtained spacetime, we employ the Gauss-Bonnet formalism to evaluate the gravitational deflection angle. Our findings indicate that the parameters $\gamma$ and $\rho_0$ exert a considerable influence on the resulting light trajectories. The perturbative behavior of the obtained configuration is investigated within the scalar and electromagnetic field sectors. Subsequently, a novel electromagnetic potential is introduced to examine its influence on the radial variation of the red-blue shifts characteristics. Our findings establish a unified picture of the geometrical, perturbative, and optical characteristics of quartic density-supported wormholes.

</details>


### [69] [Vanishing Island: Spectral Criticality in Boson Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01826)
*Bo-Xuan Ge*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies a cusp-type critical endpoint in the parameter space of solitonic boson stars, where radial stability is lost due to coalescence of two zero-mode boundaries and vanishing quadratic/cubic stiffness—leading to a square-root soft-mode scaling and divergent linear timescale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the onset and nature of radial instability in solitonic boson stars, particularly the structural transition at stability boundaries.

Method: Analysis of radial perturbations near stability thresholds; examination of constrained mode frequencies, stiffness coefficients (quadratic, cubic, quartic), and scaling behavior of the soft mode near a cusp singularity.

Result: A cusp-type endpoint is identified where two radial zero-mode boundaries merge; quadratic and cubic stiffness vanish, quartic coefficient remains finite and non-zero; soft mode frequency scales as Ω_soft ∝ (σ_cusp − σ)^1/2, implying divergent linear timescale.

Conclusion: The cusp endpoint governs a universal critical behavior in radially stable boson star families, robust under deformations of scalar self-interaction, indicating structural universality in nonlinear solitonic systems.

Abstract: We identify a cusp-type endpoint at which a finite radially stable region of solitonic boson stars disappears. Two fundamental radial zero-mode boundaries coalesce there, while the constrained quadratic stiffness and cubic coefficient vanish and the quartic coefficient remains finite and non-zero. The soft radial mode obeys \(\Omega_{\mathrm{soft}}\propto (\sigma_{\mathrm{cusp}}-\sigma)^{1/2}\), revealing a divergent linear timescale at the endpoint. The same critical structure persists under the tested deformation of the scalar self-interaction.

</details>


### [70] [Light Ring Stability Across Spacetime Dimensions and Theories of Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01917)
*Kajol Paithankar,Sanved Kolekar*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the light ring (LR) theorem beyond 4D General Relativity to modified gravity (f(R), scalar-tensor, Horndeski) and higher dimensions (d ≥ 4), showing LR existence remains topologically guaranteed in pairs but stability is no longer assured—depending on theory-specific couplings and dimensionality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess viability of horizonless ultracompact objects (UCOs) as black hole mimickers across gravitational theories and dimensions, given that stable light rings may trigger nonlinear instabilities undermining their longevity.

Method: Topological analysis of null geodesic effective potentials in stationary, axisymmetric, horizonless spacetimes; extension of light ring existence and stability criteria to f(R), scalar-tensor, and restricted Horndeski gravity in d ≥ 4 dimensions; stability determined via second-derivative sign of the potential and NEC generalization.

Result: In 4D modified gravity, LR stability depends on coupling parameters—not just NEC—so stable LRs can be absent in some regimes; in d > 4, LRs still appear in pairs topologically, but now include four types (two saddle points), and pairing constraints prevent guaranteed coexistence of stable and unstable LRs.

Conclusion: Light ring stability is not generic across theories or dimensions; thus, using UCOs for GR tests requires explicit accounting of both gravitational model and spacetime dimension—undermining universal stability-based exclusion arguments.

Abstract: The light ring theorem states that four-dimensional stationary, axisymmetric, horizonless ultracompact objects (UCOs) possess light rings (LRs) in pairs, one of which is stable if the Null Energy Condition (NEC) holds in General Relativity (GR). The existence of a stable LR may trigger nonlinear spacetime instabilities, thereby raising questions about the viability of UCOs as long-lived black hole mimickers. We extend the LR existence and stability theorem beyond four-dimensional GR to modified gravity theories, in particular, $f(R)$ gravity, scalar-tensor theories, and restricted Horndeski gravity, in ($d \geq 4$) dimensional spacetimes. Our findings show that in four-dimensional modified gravities, the stability of LRs is governed by theory-specific coupling parameters in addition to the NEC. Consequently, a stable LR is not guaranteed to occur in certain parameter regimes. In higher dimensions ($d>4$), we confirm using topological arguments that LRs still emerge in pairs; however, the topological charges are now associated with four distinct types of LRs, including two types of saddle points. The stability analysis further constrains the allowed LR pairings, implying that, unlike in four-dimensional GR, the emergent pair does not necessarily include a stable LR. These findings directly impact tests of GR using UCOs, demonstrating that LR stability is highly sensitive to both the underlying gravitational theory and spacetime dimensionality.

</details>


### [71] [Spin-dependent topological structure of black-hole thermodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02035)
*Erdem Sucu,İzzet Sakallı,Yusuf Sucu,Emmanuel N. Saridakis*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper connects quantum-corrected Hawking radiation (via spin-dependent tunneling and GUP) with thermodynamic topology in massive-gravity AdS black holes, showing that particle spin reshapes local defect structure without changing global topological charge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether quantum features of Hawking radiation—specifically particle spin and generalized uncertainty principle (GUP) corrections—are linked to the off-shell thermodynamic topology of black holes, a connection previously unexplored.

Method: Combines arbitrary-spin zitterbewegung tunneling and GUP-corrected quantum emission with thermodynamic geometry: derives spin-dependent Hawking temperature, constructs channel-dependent effective entropy via first law, and formulates off-shell free energy and thermodynamic vector field; analyzes Bekenstein-Hawking and exponential entropy corrections for comparison.

Result: Reveals spin- and GUP-dependent repositioning of topological defects in the thermodynamic vector field; global winding number W is branch-dependent (W=+1 for γ=−1, W=0 for γ=+1), but spin shifts defect locations and controls the fold bifurcation separating two-defect and defect-free regimes.

Conclusion: Quantum properties of emitted particles do not alter the global thermodynamic topological class but encode information in the local defect and bifurcation structure—establishing a direct link between quantum-corrected radiation channels and effective off-shell thermodynamic topology.

Abstract: Thermodynamic topology classifies black-hole equilibrium states through topological defects of an off-shell thermodynamic field, while quantum-corrected Hawking radiation can retain information about the quantum numbers of the emitted particle. Whether these two structures are connected has remained largely unexplored. In this work we investigate this question for a massive-gravity AdS black hole by combining arbitrary-spin zitterbewegung tunneling with generalized-uncertainty-principle corrections. We derive the particle-spin-dependent Hawking temperature, construct the corresponding channel-dependent effective entropy through the first law, and formulate the associated off-shell free energy and thermodynamic vector field. As reference sectors, we also analyze the Bekenstein-Hawking and exponentially corrected entropies. We find a clear separation between local and global thermodynamic topology. For the $\gamma=-1$ branch, a single positively wound defect persists, yielding $W=+1$, whereas for $\gamma=+1$ the defects occur in oppositely wound pairs and the global charge remains $W=0$. The particle spin nevertheless shifts the defect positions and the critical configurations at which the $\gamma=+1$ pair merges and annihilates. The fold separating the two-defect and defect-free regions is controlled jointly by the particle spin, the GUP parameter, and the cosmological constant. Thus, the quantum properties of the emitted particle do not alter the global topological class, but they reorganize the local defect and bifurcation structure within it. Our results establish a direct connection between quantum-corrected Hawking radiation and the corresponding effective off-shell thermodynamic topology, showing that its local defect structure can retain information about the quantum channel through which the black hole radiates.

</details>


### [72] [Torsion, Frames and Quantum Bounces: Inflation and Ekpyrotic Contraction in Quantum Trajectory Cosmology with a Non-Minimally Coupled Scalar](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02036)
*Rikpratik Sengupta,Paulo Vargas Moniz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates de Broglie-Bohm quantum cosmology for a scalar field non-minimally coupled to Riemann-Cartan geometry with torsion, showing torsion does not cause bounces—interference does—and impacts inflationary/ekpyrotic observables (e.g., spectral tilt, tensor-to-scalar ratio) only weakly or via frame-dependent rescalings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether spacetime torsion in Riemann-Cartan geometry can induce cosmological bounces or meaningfully alter inflationary and ekpyrotic dynamics within a well-defined quantum cosmological framework (de Broglie-Bohm interpretation).

Method: Analyzes the minisuperspace Wheeler-DeWitt equation in de Broglie-Bohm pilot-wave theory for a scalar field coupled to metric-compatible Riemann-Cartan geometry; eliminates auxiliary torsion trace algebraically pre-quantisation; exploits conformal flatness of minisuperspace metric to establish frame covariance; solves WdW equation exactly for massless and exponential/quadratic potentials; computes Bohmian trajectories, quantum potential, and observational predictions (ns, r) in both Jordan and Einstein frames.

Result: Torsion does not generate bounces—bounces arise from wavefunction interference; Bohmian trajectories bounce in Einstein frame for massless scalar but only for ξ < ξc in Jordan frame; torsion modifies late-time expansion exponent and induces small (<1%) shift in tensor-to-scalar ratio r for quadratic inflation; single-field ekpyrosis yields universally blue spectra (ns > 3) independent of torsion; entropic two-field version introduces tilt corrections ∝ √ξ (non-minimal coupling) and ∝ ξ^(3/2) (torsion); tensor modes remain blue and largely unaffected by the Bohmian bounce.

Conclusion: Torsion in this Riemann-Cartan scalar-tensor model is observationally subdominant: it alters dynamics only through frame-rescaled kinetic terms and induces negligible corrections to CMB observables; the bounce is quantum-interference-driven, not torsion-driven; thus, torsion cannot rescue classical singularities or qualitatively change ekpyrotic/inflationary phenomenology in this setup.

Abstract: We study the de Broglie-Bohm quantum cosmology of a scalar field non-minimally coupled to a metric-compatible Riemann-Cartan geometry, comparing inflation with ekpyrotic contraction. The torsion trace is auxiliary and is eliminated algebraically before quantisation. The minisuperspace metric depends on the coupling $F(\phi)$ and a constant $\kappa$ for an independent quadratic torsion term ($\kappa=0$ Einstein-Cartan, $\kappa\to\infty$ torsion-free). For every $F$, $\kappa$ and potential this metric is conformally flat, so torsion acts only through the Einstein-frame kinetic function $K(\phi)$. Its curvature is therefore not a diagnostic of torsion, and the Laplace-Beltrami Wheeler-DeWitt equation, quantum potential and Bohmian trajectories are frame covariant. For a massless scalar the equation is solved exactly; in a two-packet state all 41 flux-weighted Bohmian trajectories bounce in the Einstein frame. In the Jordan frame the bounce at the symmetric event is independent of torsion and persists only for $\xi<\xi_c$ (closed form); torsion changes the late-time exponent from $1-\sqrt{6\xi/(1+6\xi)}$ to $1-\sqrt{6\xi}$. The bounce comes from interference in the state, not from torsion. For exponential potentials, exact Wheeler-DeWitt solutions show that in the sector containing the ekpyrotic attractor no Bohmian trajectory bounces, and single-momentum states bounce only where the potential is negligible and end in the crunch. For quadratic inflation the torsion-specific shift of $r$ is below one per cent. A single ekpyrotic field gives blue spectra, $n_s=3+2/(\epsilon-1)$ and $r=16\epsilon$, whatever the torsion, also for running $\epsilon$. In an entropic two-field version the tilt shifts at order $\sqrt\xi$ (non-minimal coupling) and $\xi^{3/2}$ (torsion). Tensor modes cross the massless Bohmian bounce almost unchanged and stay blue.

</details>


### [73] [A Singularity Theorem for Quasi-Topological Gravities](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02190)
*Francesco Di Filippo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends Penrose's singularity theorem to quasi-topological gravity theories with non-singular black hole solutions, proving that spherical collapse under the null energy condition still leads to future null geodesic incompleteness—implying persistent predictability breakdown (Cauchy horizons) even dynamically, not just in vacuum.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether quasi-topological gravity theories genuinely resolve the black hole singularity and predictability problems, given their non-singular static black hole solutions but presence of Cauchy horizons.

Method: Mathematical generalization of Penrose's singularity theorem to arbitrary quasi-topological gravity theories admitting non-singular spherically symmetric black hole solutions, assuming spherical symmetry, null energy condition, and formation of a trapped sphere.

Result: All such quasi-topological theories exhibit future null geodesic incompleteness upon trapped surface formation—i.e., they inevitably develop Cauchy horizons under dynamical collapse, just like general relativity.

Conclusion: The Cauchy horizon problem is intrinsic and not limited to vacuum solutions; thus, these theories do not resolve the fundamental predictability issue in black hole interiors, remaining as problematic as general relativity for interior dynamics.

Abstract: Quasi-topological theories of gravity have recently attracted substantial attention as several theories in this class have a non-singular black hole as a vacuum and spherically symmetric solution. This motivates the expectation that these theories can provide a resolution to the singularity problem and a description of black holes cores. The known solutions, however, possess a Cauchy horizon which denotes the end of predictability of the theory, as the initial data do not determine a unique continuation past this type of horizon. In this paper, we show that Penrose singularity theorem can be extended to all the known classes of quasi-topological theories that have non-singular black holes as solutions. We prove that spherical collapse satisfying the null energy condition necessarily leads to future null geodesic incompleteness once a trapped sphere forms. This implies that the lack of predictability of the theories is not an artefact of vacuum solutions and these theories are as problematic as general relativity for the dynamical studies of black hole interiors.

</details>


### [74] [Pilot search for continuous gravitational waves from Hercules X-1 in O3 LIGO data: enhancements to a hidden Markov model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02307)
*Nicholas Kah Yean Low,Andrew Melatos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper presents a semi-coherent search for continuous gravitational waves from stellar oscillations in Hercules X-1 using LIGO O3 data and an enhanced hidden Markov model algorithm; it introduces and validates several algorithmic improvements that boost sensitivity depth by ~14%, and reports four surviving candidates in the 60–200 Hz band, with a 95% confidence upper limit on strain of 9.3×10⁻²⁶ at 174 Hz.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To implement and test algorithmic enhancements—such as second-order projected semi-major axis correction, orbital eccentricity correction, subband width adjustment, and propagation of orbital parameters with nonzero orbital period derivative—for future continuous gravitational-wave searches targeting low-mass X-ray binaries like Hercules X-1.

Method: Semi-coherent search using an established hidden Markov model (HMM) to track stochastic frequency wandering; validation via synthetic signal injections into Gaussian noise; application to LIGO Observing Run 3 data over 60–200 Hz; five-level veto procedure applied to candidate selection.

Result: Enhancements improve sensitivity depth by ≈14% for a Hercules X-1-like target; four candidates survive vetoes at terminating frequencies 76.41 Hz, 109.47 Hz, 144.40 Hz, and 189.24 Hz; frequentist 95% upper limit on wave strain is 9.3×10⁻²⁶ at 174 Hz (uniform polarization prior).

Conclusion: The proposed algorithmic enhancements are empirically validated and yield measurable sensitivity gains; while no detection is claimed, the pilot search demonstrates feasibility and sets improved strain limits, paving the way for more sensitive future searches targeting neutron star oscillations in low-mass X-ray binaries.

Abstract: A semi-coherent search is conducted for continuous gravitational waves from stellar oscillations in Hercules X-1 with data from Observing Run 3 of the Laser Interferometer Gravitational-wave Observatory, using an established hidden Markov model algorithm to track stochastic wandering of the signal frequency. The primary goal is to implement and test several algorithmic enhancements relevant to future searches, including a second-order projected semi-major axis correction, an orbital eccentricity correction, an adjustment to the width of a search subband, and propagation of the electromagnetically measured orbital parameters with a nonzero orbital period derivative. The enhancements are validated by injecting synthetic signals into Gaussian noise and improve the sensitivity depth by $\approx$ 14% for a Hercules X-1-like target. In the 60--200 Hz band, the pilot search yields four candidates that survive a five-level veto procedure, whose terminating frequencies are 76.41 Hz, 109.47 Hz, 144.40 Hz, and 189.24 Hz. The frequentist upper limit on the maximum detectable wave strain at 95% confidence is arbitrarily set at 174 Hz and found to be $9.3 \times 10^{-26}$, assuming a uniform prior on polarization.

</details>


### [75] [First law of thermodynamics for interacting charged black holes and applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02663)
*Qing Han,Marcus Khuri,Gilbert Weinstein,Jingang Xiong*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: Derives a mass variation identity for black hole thermodynamics in Einstein-Maxwell theory with multiple charged horizons and conical singularities; establishes monotonicity of angular momentum Hessian along charge-fixed paths with increasing Weyl gaps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the first law of black hole thermodynamics to multi-horizon, asymptotically flat, stationary axisymmetric Einstein-Maxwell solutions—including those with conical axis singularities—and to understand how geometric and thermodynamic quantities vary across parameter families.

Method: Uses variational methods on the Einstein-Maxwell action under asymptotic flatness and axisymmetry; derives a first-law-type mass variation identity valid for independent parameter variations within fixed horizon-type families; combines it with a sign theorem for conical deficit parameters to infer monotonicity properties of the angular momentum Hessian.

Result: Obtains a precise first-law identity for multi-horizon charged black holes with conical singularities; defines a corrected ADM mass whose angular momentum derivatives yield horizon angular velocities on the all-degenerate family; proves positive definiteness of the angular momentum Hessian at Majumdar-Papapetrou; shows the Hessian is nondecreasing (in positive-semidefinite order) along admissible paths of increasing Weyl gaps at fixed charges.

Conclusion: The derived mass variation identity provides a rigorous thermodynamic framework for multi-horizon electrovacuum black holes with conical singularities, and the monotonicity of the angular momentum Hessian reflects structural rigidity tied to Weyl gap growth and charge conservation.

Abstract: We derive a mass variation identity expressing the first law of black hole thermodynamics for asymptotically flat, stationary, axisymmetric Einstein-Maxwell solutions with multiple electrically charged horizons and finite conical axis singularities. Under the stated asymptotic and parameter regularity hypotheses, the identity holds for independent variations of all parameters within families of fixed horizon types. On the all-degenerate family, a correction to the ADM mass defines a scalar function whose derivatives with respect to angular momenta are the horizon angular velocities. At the Majumdar-Papapetrou family, its angular momentum Hessian is positive definite. Combining the first variation identity with a separate theorem on the sign of the conical parameters shows that this Hessian is nondecreasing in the positive-semidefinite order along admissible paths of increasing Weyl gaps at fixed positive charges.

</details>


### [76] [Charge-Activated Birefringence and Shadow Mimicry around Reissner-Nordström Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02727)
*Mao-Yuan Wan,Chen Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that electric charge can induce birefringence in a neutral vector field coupled to the Einstein tensor near a Reissner-Nordström black hole; the coupling—combined with charge—produces parity-dependent shadow edges and a helicity-reversed backward-scattered signal, which cannot be mimicked by charge alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To distinguish between effects caused solely by black hole electric charge versus those arising from a fundamental coupling between a neutral vector field and gravity (via the Einstein tensor), particularly in black hole shadow observations.

Method: Analytical and semi-analytical study of wave propagation for a neutral vector field nonminimally coupled to the Einstein tensor in a Reissner-Nordström spacetime; comparison of scattering amplitudes, effective potentials, absorption cross sections, and shadow characteristics for both parities (left/right circular polarizations).

Result: Charge activates birefringence only when coupling is present; coupling splits shadow edges between parities and generates a nonzero, fully helicity-reversed backward-scattered signal; weak-coupling corrections to the effective potential are identical for both parities but sign-dependent (positive/negative coupling oppositely affect low-frequency absorption and average shadow radius); uncoupled case yields identical shadows and zero backward intensity at all charges.

Conclusion: The observed split shadow edge and helicity-reversed backward signal jointly serve as smoking-gun signatures of the Einstein-tensor coupling—unattainable by tuning charge alone—providing a novel observational discriminant, pending detection mechanisms for the field.

Abstract: We discover that electric charge can activates birefringence for a neutral vector field coupled to the Einstein tensor around a Reissner-Nordström black hole. Changing the electric charge of an uncoupled black hole can mimic one parity's shadow radius of a coupled black hole. The coupling makes the two parity looks differently. It separates their shadow edges of two parities and produces a backward-scattered signal with reversed helicity. However changing the charge alone cannot reproduce both effects. They require both nonzero charge and coupling. At weak coupling, the leading correction is the same for both potential. Positive coupling raises the potential, reduces low-frequency absorption and decrease the average shadow radius. Negative coupling reverses these trends. The backward signal is nonzero, this is due to the difference between the complex scattering amplitudes of the two parities. It is entirely helicity reversed. For the uncoupled field, the shadows is the same for both parities and the backward intensity vanishes exactly at every charge. We can use the split edge and backward signal to distinguish the coupling from a change of charge alone. A comparison with observations still requires a mechanism that produces and detects this field.

</details>


### [77] [Integrable mass of Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson black hole](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03123)
*Li Hu,Rong-Gen Cai,Shan-Wen Jiang,Shao-Jiang Wang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper solves the long-standing problem of determining the conserved mass of rotating black holes in the Bertotti-Robinson universe using integrability methods and rigorous analytic techniques, yielding a closed-form Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson mass without thermodynamic assumptions—enabling the first rigorous derivation of the first law and Smarr formula for this family.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The nontrivial asymptotic structure of rotating black holes in the Bertotti-Robinson universe prevents direct determination of conserved mass; prior work relied on thermodynamic input, leaving the mass definition unjustified from first principles.

Method: Integrability methods are applied; local analytic existence and uniqueness are established via the Cauchy-Kovalevskaja theorem; a rescaled recurrence relation is derived and shown to terminate at all orders, yielding a closed-form mass expression.

Result: A closed-form expression for the Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson black hole mass is obtained, matching previous results derived via the canonical first law, and correctly reducing to the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson subcases; the first law and Smarr formula are rigorously proven without thermodynamic assumptions.

Conclusion: The conserved mass of Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black holes is now fully determined from geometric and analytic principles alone—providing a foundational basis for thermodynamics and enabling future applications in exact solutions and holography.

Abstract: The nontrivial asymptotic structure of rotating black holes in the Bertotti-Robinson universe complicates a direct determination of their conserved mass, leading previous studies to rely on a priori thermodynamic input. We address this long-standing problem using integrability methods, establish local analytic existence and uniqueness via the Cauchy-Kovalevskaja theorem, and obtain a closed expression via a rescaled recurrence whose termination is proved at all orders. The resulting Kerr-Newman-Bertotti-Robinson mass coincides with the expression previously derived using the canonical first law identity and reproduces the masses of both Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson subcases. With the mass thus fixed without invoking thermodynamic relations for the first time, we rigorously establish the first law of black-hole thermodynamics and the Smarr formula for a whole family of Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black holes suitable for future applications.

</details>


### [78] [Surrogate model of gravitational waveforms for quasi-circular, spin-aligned, asymmetric-mass black hole binaries based on the hybridization of WaSABI-C inspirals with numerical relativity waveforms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03564)
*H. L. Iglesias,D. M. Shoemaker*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: WaSABINRHybSur2dq15 is the first gravitational waveform surrogate model unifying self-force (WaSABI-C), post-Newtonian, and numerical relativity (SXS Catalog 3) inputs; it covers mass ratios q ∈ [1.25, 15], primary spin χ₁z ∈ [−0.5, 0.5], five dominant spherical harmonic modes, and is accurate (mismatch < 10⁻³) for total masses 80–300 M⊙ within LVK band.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the accuracy gap across regimes—extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (self-force), intermediate-mass-ratio (PN), and comparable-mass binaries (NR)—by constructing a unified, high-fidelity surrogate model for aligned-spin, quasi-circular black hole binaries relevant to LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA observations.

Method: Trained a reduced-order surrogate model on 65 hybrid waveforms: WaSABI-C self-force inspirals matched to SXS Catalog 3 NR simulations; uses parameterized interpolation over q ∈ [1.25, 15], χ₁z ∈ [−0.5, 0.5]; includes (ℓ,m) = (2,2), (2,1), (3,3), (4,4), (5,5) spin-weighted spherical harmonic modes; calibrated to LVK frequency band (f_low = 20 Hz).

Result: WaSABINRHybSur2dq15 achieves mismatches < 10⁻³ against its training hybrid waveforms for total masses 80–300 M⊙; covers full LVK band for M ≳ 111.5 M⊙; enables rapid, accurate evaluation of multi-mode waveforms across broad asymmetric-spin parameter space.

Conclusion: This work demonstrates successful integration of self-force, PN, and NR information into a single surrogate model, enabling high-accuracy, computationally efficient gravitational waveforms for parameter estimation and detection of spinning, asymmetric-mass black hole binaries in current and future ground-based detectors.

Abstract: With the incorporation of aligned-spin dynamics into self-force waveform models, such as the WaSABI-C inspiral model, and with the release of the SXS collaboration's third public catalog of NR simulations, the gravitational waveform modeling community has made significant advances in the modeling of spinning, asymmetric-mass black hole binaries. In light of these developments, we present WaSABINRHybSur2dq15, the first surrogate model to incorporate information from self-force theory, post-Newtonian theory, and numerical relativity. The model is trained on 65 hybrid waveforms for quasi-circular, spin-aligned, asymmetric-mass black hole binaries with mass ratios spanning 1:1.25 to 1:20 and aligned-spins for the primary black hole ranging -0.5 to 0.5. The hybrid waveforms are generated from a set of WaSABI-C inspirals and NR waveforms from the third SXS public catalog. The model covers the parameter domain $1.25 \leq q \leq 15$ and $|\chi_{1z}| \leq 0.5$, and includes the $(2,2),\, (2,1),\, (3,3),\, (4,4),$ and $(5,5)$ spin-weighted spherical harmonic modes. The model also spans the entire LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA (LVK) frequency band (with $f_{\text{low}}=20$ Hz) for total masses $M \gtrsim 111.5\,M_{\odot}$. We find that the WaSABINRHybSur2dq15 model faithfully reproduces the hybrid waveforms with mismatches less than $10^{-3}$ for total masses in the range $80\,M_{\odot} \leq M \leq 300\,M_{\odot}$.

</details>


### [79] [Tracing source imprints on the gravitational direct wave](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03632)
*Andrew Laeuger,Colin Weller,Ling Sun,Aleyna Koro,Patrick P. Chen,Sizheng Ma,Yanbei Chen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper advances the understanding of black hole ringdown waveforms by introducing a frequency-dependent filter and complex-coordinate analytic continuation of Teukolsky source variables, enabling a highly accurate, semi-analytic description of the direct wave component shaped by plunging perturbers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To clarify the incomplete understanding of how the trajectory of a plunging perturber imprints its dynamics onto the direct wave component of black hole ringdown—beyond the standard quasinormal-mode picture.

Method: Introduces a frequency-dependent black hole response filter for improved kernel localization; analytically continues Teukolsky source variables to complex coordinates; applies a generalized steepest-descent method to model coherent emission and elucidate source-dynamics imprint beyond light-cone constraints.

Result: Achieves a semi-analytic direct wave model that closely matches full numerical integration across the entire plunge; demonstrates superior localization via frequency-dependent filtering; reveals how coherent emission governs dynamical imprint outside the light-cone paradigm.

Conclusion: The direct wave carries rich, non-quasinormal-mode information about plunging trajectories, accessible through frequency-dependent filtering and complex-source formalism—enabling more precise waveform modeling and gravitational-wave inference for extreme-mass-ratio inspirals.

Abstract: The direct wave component of black hole (BH) ringdown, revealed through quasinormal-mode filters, carries a non-quasinormal-mode imprint of the plunging perturber's dynamics. However, the connection between the source trajectory and the observed direct wave remains incompletely understood. We first show that the full waveform filtered by the frequency-dependent black hole response gives a substantially more localized kernel than the standard rational filter approach. We then show that analytically continuing the Teukolsky source variables to complex coordinates allows for a semianalytic direct wave which closely reproduces numerical integration throughout the entire plunge. Our generalized steepest-descent method clarifies how coherent emission shapes the imprint of source dynamics on the observed direct wave beyond the light-cone picture.

</details>

### [80] [Quantum Simulation of Hadronization: From String Breaking to Event Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00214)
*Christian W. Bauer,Anthony N. Ciavarella,Jad C. Halimeh,Yannick Meurice,Stephen Mrenna,Martin J. Savage,Giovanni Cataldi,Henry Froland,Dorota M. Grabowska,Sebastian Grieninger,Kenneth Heitritter,Michael Hite,Marc Illa,Saurabh V. Kadam,Henry Lamm,Zhiyao Li,Zane Ozzello,Vincent R. Pascuzzi,Blake Senseman,Kaidi Xu,Xiaojun Yao,Nikita A. Zemlevskiy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This white paper outlines a roadmap for using quantum computers to simulate QCD hadronization—currently modeled phenomenologically—to improve LHC precision; it proposes staged, toy-model-based quantum simulations bridging current string-breaking demonstrations to quantitative event generator input.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Uncertainties in phenomenological hadronization models used in event generators limit precision of LHC measurements; first-principles QCD simulation is intractable classically but potentially feasible on quantum computers.

Method: Develops a staged roadmap: (1) sequential toy models connecting existing quantum simulations of string-breaking in gauge theories to full QCD; (2) identification of key observables and technical challenges; (3) phased integration—first qualitative, then quantitative—into event generators.

Result: A concrete, stepwise program is proposed, specifying target observables (e.g., hadron multiplicity, momentum spectra, flavor correlations), current limitations (qubit count, coherence, lattice discretization, real-time evolution fidelity), and milestones toward quantum-informed hadronization models.

Conclusion: Quantum simulation of hadronization is a long-term but viable path toward reducing model dependence in collider physics; success requires co-design across quantum computing, lattice gauge theory, and high-energy phenomenology, with near-term focus on qualitative validation and mid-term on quantitative calibration.

Abstract: High-energy collisions produce large numbers of quarks and gluons that are bound into hadrons by the confining interactions of quantum chromodynamics (QCD). This hadronization process is intrinsically real-time and non-perturbative. Event generators describe hadronization using phenomenological models with parameters fitted to data rather than calculated from QCD. Uncertainties in these models limit the precision of certain measurements at the Large Hadron Collider. Quantum computers could enable first-principles simulations of these dynamics. Existing quantum simulations of gauge theories have demonstrated string-breaking dynamics. This white paper summarizes the conclusions of the InQubator for Quantum Simulation workshop \textit{From String Dynamics to Event Generation with Quantum Simulation}. We develop a roadmap for extending these simulations to inform phenomenological models of hadronization. A sequence of toy models is proposed for connecting current quantum simulations to QCD. The observables that should be extracted are listed, and the challenges that separate present simulations from quantitative predictions are identified. We propose a staged program in which quantum simulations first provide qualitative modifications to event generators and later provide quantitative input.

</details>


### [81] [Heavy-meson molecules with dynamical pions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01768)
*Yan-Ke Chen,Liang-Zhen Wen,Lu Meng,Shi-Lin Zhu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a coupled Fock-space Hamiltonian framework treating pions as dynamical degrees of freedom to study heavy-meson molecules, enabling direct pole extraction via a linear generalized eigenvalue problem and yielding explicit wave functions; it benchmarks successfully on $T_{cc}^+$ and $X(3872)$, then predicts new isoscalar bound states and resonances in $BB\pi$, $B\bar{B}\pi$, $DB\pi$, and $D\bar{B}\pi$ systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond energy-dependent two-body approximations by consistently incorporating three-body dynamics (e.g., pion emission/absorption) in heavy-meson molecular systems, enabling rigorous treatment of particle-number-changing processes and direct access to poles and wave-function composition.

Method: Constructs an energy-independent Hamiltonian coupling two-body ($H_1 H_2^*$) and three-body ($H_1 H_2 \pi$) Fock sectors; derives $H^*$ self-energy, pion-exchange interaction, and three-body propagation self-consistently; solves the coupled Schrödinger equation using Gaussian expansion and complex scaling methods to obtain poles and wave functions via a single linear generalized eigenvalue problem.

Result: Benchmarked predictions for $T_{cc}^+$ and $X(3872)$ agree with experimental pole positions; predicts one isoscalar bound state each in $BB\pi$, $B\bar{B}\pi$, $DB\pi$, $D\bar{B}\pi$; $DB\pi$ hosts a resonance below its three-body threshold, $D\bar{B}\pi$ one above; proposes $DB^* \to DB\gamma$ and $D\bar{B}\pi$ final states for experimental searches.

Conclusion: The coupled Fock-space formalism provides a unified, non-perturbative, and numerically stable approach to heavy-meson molecules with dynamical pions, yielding both spectral information and structural insight; it is generalizable to other few-body systems with unstable or decaying constituents.

Abstract: We investigate heavy-meson molecules by treating the pion as a dynamical degree of freedom. We construct an energy-independent Hamiltonian that couples the two- and three-body Fock spaces. The two-body sector contains a pseudoscalar meson and a vector meson $H_1H_2^{*}$, while the three-body sector consists of two pseudoscalar mesons and a pion $H_1H_2 \pi$. The $H^*$ self-energy, the $H_1H_2^{*}$ pion-exchange interaction, and the $H_1H_2\pi$ three-body propagation are consistently generated through the coupling between the two Fock sectors. We use the Gaussian expansion and complex scaling methods to solve the coupled Schrödinger equation. Previous approaches usually reduce three-body effects to energy-dependent two-body kernels and solve the equations iteratively. Our approach obtains the poles by solving a single linear generalized eigenvalue problem. The eigenvectors also provide explicit wave functions in both Fock sectors, allowing a qualitative analysis of channel weights and spatial structure. We benchmark the framework in $T_{cc}^{+}$ and $X(3872)$. The benchmark results are consistent with the current experimental pole determinations. We then apply this formalism to the $BB\pi$, $B\bar{B}\pi$, $DB\pi$ and $D\bar{B}\pi$ systems. We predict one isoscalar bound state in each of these systems. The $DB\pi$ and $D\bar{B}\pi$ systems also contain resonances lying below and above their three-body threshold, respectively. We propose complementary searches for these resonances through $DB^*\to DB\gamma$ and $D\bar B\pi$. This coupled Fock-space framework can be readily extended to other few-body systems in which unstable constituents can decay and change the particle number.

</details>


### [82] [A General Manifestly Covariant Construction of Partial-Wave Amplitudes in the Rarita-Schwinger Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01824)
*Hao-Jie Jing,Shu-Ming Wu,Jia-Jun Wu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Relative Spin Wave Functions (RSWFs) via covariant pure boosts to a common rest frame, enabling manifestly covariant, on-shell partial-wave amplitudes for arbitrary-spin two-body decays while cleanly separating spin transport from orbital-spin coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a manifestly Lorentz-covariant framework that disentangles spin transport from orbital-spin coupling in multi-spin hadronic processes, supporting rigorous partial-wave analysis and operator construction in QCD-based theories.

Method: Constructs Relative Spin Wave Functions (RSWFs) using covariant pure boosts to a common rest frame within the Rarita-Schwinger formalism; derives explicit, covariant, on-shell partial-wave amplitudes for two-body decays of massive particles with arbitrary spin, labeled by definite orbital angular momentum and total final-state spin.

Result: Derives fully covariant, on-shell partial-wave amplitudes for arbitrary-spin two-body decays; establishes RSWFs as a foundational Lorentz basis for LS-organized operators in Lattice QCD, QCD sum rules, and hadronic reaction analyses.

Conclusion: RSWFs provide a theoretically robust, manifestly covariant generalization of partial-wave decomposition that preserves conventional angular structure while enabling consistent spin-orbit separation—critical for first-principles QCD studies of high-spin resonances and reactions.

Abstract: We propose a new set of functions, named Relative Spin Wave Functions (RSWFs), defined via covariant pure boosts to a common rest frame. RSWFs separate spin transport from orbital-spin coupling while preserving the conventional partial-wave angular structure in a manifestly covariant form. Within the Rarita-Schwinger formalism, this approach generates explicit, covariant on-shell partial-wave amplitudes for two-body decays of massive particles of arbitrary spin, with definite orbital angular momentum and total final-state spin. This general framework provides a foundational Lorentz basis for hadronic reaction studies and $LS$-organized operators in theoretical investigations, such as Lattice QCD and QCD sum rules.

</details>


### [83] [Unitarity and Positivity Constraints on C- or P-odd Anomalous Quartic Couplings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00133)
*Oscar J. P. Éboli,Álvaro Lozano-Onrubia,Samuel Rosende Herrero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives new unitarity and positivity bounds for dimension-eight anomalous quartic gauge couplings in SMEFT, completing partial-wave analysis for all 34 operators—including CP-odd ones—and showing CP-odd coefficients necessitate CP-even contributions; combined constraints tighten bounds on 23 Wilson coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To fully constrain the dimension-eight genuine aQGC sector of SMEFT by extending partial-wave unitarity and forward-limit positivity analyses to include all C- or P-odd (i.e., CP-odd) operators—previously omitted—thereby ensuring theoretical consistency (unitarity and causality) across the complete operator basis.

Method: Systematic partial-wave decomposition of scattering amplitudes involving quartic gauge bosons; analytical derivation of forward-limit positivity bounds using dispersion relations and crossing symmetry; incorporation of CP-odd operators into both unitarity and positivity frameworks; joint application of unitarity and positivity constraints to bound Wilson coefficients.

Result: First complete partial-wave unitarity analysis covering all 34 dimension-eight genuine aQGC operators; first analytical forward-limit positivity bounds including all CP-odd Wilson coefficients; demonstration that non-zero CP-odd coefficients imply mandatory non-zero CP-even contributions; strengthened numerical bounds on 23 out of 34 Wilson coefficients via combined constraints.

Conclusion: Theoretical consistency requirements—unitarity and positivity—impose interdependent, non-trivial constraints across CP-even and CP-odd aQGCs; ignoring CP-odd operators leads to incomplete and potentially misleading bounds; the full set of constraints significantly narrows the viable parameter space for dimension-eight SMEFT aQGCs.

Abstract: In this work, we provide a comprehensive study of partial-wave unitarity and positivity constraints in the dimension-eight genuine anomalous quartic gauge coupling (aQGC) sector of the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT). We extend previous partial-wave unitarity analyses to include all C- or P-odd operators, thereby completing the coverage of the full basis of 34 operators. We also derive an analytical set of necessary forward-limit positivity bounds that include all CP-odd Wilson coefficients. We demonstrate that non-vanishing CP-odd Wilson coefficients require non-vanishing contributions from the CP-even sector to satisfy the derived positivity conditions. Combining the positivity conditions with partial-wave unitarity, we obtain stronger bounds on twenty-three of the thirty-four Wilson coefficients in this sector of the SMEFT.

</details>


### [84] [The Standard Model with Non-Anticommuting $γ_5$ in the BMHV Scheme](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00141)
*Paul Kühler,Dominik Stöckinger,Matthias Weißwange*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper completes the 1-loop renormalisation of the full Standard Model in the BMHV scheme for γ₅ in dimensional regularisation, including all symmetry-restoring counterterms in general Rξ-gauge, and clarifies the role of evanescent interactions—showing QCD unavoidably contributes to gauge/BRST symmetry breaking, thus requiring a unified treatment and recommending 4D electroweak currents.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve multi-loop electroweak precision calculations with a fully consistent treatment of γ₅ in dimensional regularisation, while resolving long-standing issues of gauge and BRST invariance violation in the BMHV scheme.

Method: Systematic 1-loop renormalisation of the full Standard Model in the BMHV scheme within general Rξ-gauge; derivation of all singular and finite counterterms required to restore gauge and BRST invariance; analysis of evanescent gauge interactions and their impact on photon/gluon current dimensionality.

Result: Complete set of 1-loop symmetry-restoring counterterms for the SM in BMHV scheme; demonstration that QCD contributions inevitably break gauge/BRST symmetry at the regularised level; identification of optimal treatment: 4-dimensional electroweak currents, D-dimensional QCD currents.

Conclusion: A fully consistent multi-loop electroweak calculation requires treating the SM as an inseparable whole in the BMHV scheme; evanescent interactions necessitate careful dimensional assignment, and electroweak gauge currents should be strictly 4-dimensional to avoid spurious anomalies and ensure robustness.

Abstract: The Breitenlohner-Maison/'t Hooft-Veltman (BMHV) scheme provides a treatment of $\gamma_5$ in dimensional regularisation (DReg) that is guaranteed to be self-consistent at all orders, but it breaks gauge and BRST invariance at the regularised level, which must be restored by suitable counterterms. We present the complete 1-loop renormalisation of the full Standard Model in the BMHV scheme in general $R_\xi$-gauge, including all singular and all finite symmetry-restoring counterterms. We further analyse the impact of evanescent gauge interactions, which allow the photon and gluon currents to be treated either 4- or $D$-dimensionally. We show that QCD always contributes to the symmetry breaking, so that the Standard Model must be treated as a whole, and we recommend a purely 4-dimensional treatment of electroweak gauge interactions. Our results provide a necessary step towards multi-loop electroweak precision calculations with a fully consistent treatment of $\gamma_5$.

</details>


### [85] [Heavy quarkonium dynamics beyond the binding energy over temperature expansion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00194)
*Nora Brambilla,Arthur Yi-Ting Lin,Tom Magorsch,Antonio Vairo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the pNRQCD open-quantum-system master equation for bottomonium in quark-gluon plasma in a spherical basis, avoids the binding-energy-over-temperature expansion, and introduces a positive-sector Lindblad approximation via optimal operator rotations for efficient QTraj simulations; it quantifies positivity-breaking effects as systematic uncertainty and provides first large-scale quantum-trajectory results for Υ(1S,2S,3S) survival probabilities across the full heavy-ion collision temperature range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional perturbative expansions (e.g., binding-energy-over-temperature) and positivity-violating structure in the pNRQCD master equation for bottomonium in QGP, enabling robust, numerically tractable, and physically faithful simulation of in-medium evolution across the full temperature range of heavy-ion collisions.

Method: Derives the full spherical-basis pNRQCD master equation without binding-energy-over-temperature expansion; identifies non-Lindblad structure due to signed jump operators; applies optimal unitary rotations to minimize the negative-decay-channel sector; constructs a positive-sector Lindblad approximation for QTraj simulation; treats the discarded negative sector as systematic uncertainty; uses a chromoelectric spectral model constrained by IR/UV limits to compute in-medium widths and Bjorken-evolved survival probabilities.

Result: Demonstrates convergence of the binding-energy-over-temperature expansion at high T; obtains first large-scale quantum-trajectory simulations of bottomonium (Υ1S,2S,3S) in the low-T regime; computes temperature-dependent in-medium widths and full-range survival probabilities consistent with heavy-ion collision dynamics; quantifies positivity breaking as controllable systematic error.

Conclusion: The positive-sector Lindblad approximation enables efficient, scalable, and physically interpretable open-quantum-system simulations of bottomonium in QGP; the framework bridges high- and low-temperature regimes, supports direct comparison with experimental suppression data, and establishes a robust methodology for assessing theoretical uncertainties from approximations (Born, Markov) inherent in open-quantum-system descriptions of quarkonium.

Abstract: We revisit the pNRQCD open-quantum-system description of bottomonium in a quark-gluon plasma, writing the full master equation in a spherical basis and establishing a framework for its numerical treatment. The equation does not rely on an expansion in the ratio of the binding energy over the temperature, while retaining the Born, Markov, and dilute approximations. It depends on suitable convolutions of Coulombic Green's functions with real-time chromoelectric correlators, rather than a finite set of transport coefficients. It contains jump-operator contributions that enter with both positive and negative signs, so it is not of a Lindblad type. We utilize optimal operator rotations to minimize the negative sector and define a positive-sector approximation that retains only the optimized positive terms, obtaining in this way a Lindblad equation that can be simulated efficiently with the open-source code QTraj for arbitrary chromoelectric spectral functions. We treat the discarded sector as a systematic uncertainty associated with positivity-breaking effects of the pseudo-Lindblad generator. Positivity breaking effects can be attributed to the approximations made in the derivation of the quarkonium master equation, such as the Born--Markov approximations.
