Using a spectral model constrained by known infrared and ultraviolet limits, we compute the in-medium width and Bjorken-evolved survival probabilities of the $\Upsilon(1\mathrm{S},2\mathrm{S},3\mathrm{S})$. The computations show the convergence of the binding-energy-over-temperature expansion at high temperatures and provide the first large-scale quantum-trajectory simulation results for bottomonium in the low temperature regime. We can thus follow the bottomonium evolution over the full temperature range relevant in heavy-ion collisions.

</details>


### [86] [Tests of an EP-Preserving Wake Force](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00200)
*Abhishek Banerjee,Dawid Brzeminski*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that ultralight scalar dark matter with universal quadratic coupling induces composition-independent differential acceleration between concentric test masses due to mutual field distortion — a novel screening effect — and uses MICROSCOPE data to constrain scalar mass (< ~8×10⁻⁹ eV) and coupling strength, with potential to probe below BBN bounds via spectral analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify a new, composition-independent signature of ultralight scalar dark matter that evades standard equivalence principle tests yet remains detectable in precision experiments like MICROSCOPE, by accounting for finite-size (non-pointlike) effects and mutual field distortion.

Method: Analytical derivation of the scalar field configuration and resulting differential acceleration for concentric spherical test masses under quadratic coupling; treatment of weak screening via perturbation theory and extension to stronger screening using layered spherical geometry; application to MICROSCOPE’s SUEP (composition-different) and SUREF (composition-identical) sensors, incorporating Earth’s dark matter wake.

Result: A measurable differential acceleration arises even for identical-composition test masses due to field distortion; MICROSCOPE constraints exclude scalar masses up to ~8×10⁻⁹ eV and couplings down to below supernova cooling bounds; inclusion of higher-frequency signal components could extend sensitivity to lower couplings, potentially below BBN limits.

Conclusion: Finite-size effects in universal quadratic scalar dark matter couplings generate a previously overlooked, non-universal differential acceleration signal; MICROSCOPE data already provide competitive constraints on ultralight scalars, and spectral analysis offers a path to probe unexplored parameter space beyond astrophysical bounds.

Abstract: A universal quadratic coupling of ultralight scalar dark matter to ordinary matter preserves the weak equivalence principle for unscreened point particles. A finite test body is nevertheless not a passive probe: it distorts the scalar field that accelerates it. We show that this field distortion produces a differential acceleration between concentric test masses, even when their microscopic coupling is universal. We calculate this signal including how each test mass distorts the field acting on the other, deriving the weak-screening limit and extending the calculation to stronger screening using concentric spherical layers. Applying the model to MICROSCOPE, we find differential signals in both its equivalence-principle sensor SUEP, whose test masses have different compositions, and its reference sensor SUREF, whose test masses have the same composition. We combine both sensors with the dark matter wake near Earth to recast the published MICROSCOPE measurements. Within this model, existing measurements probe scalar masses up to approximately $8 \times 10^{-9}\,$eV and couplings below the supernova bound. An analysis using the additional frequency components of the scalar signal could extend the mass reach and probe couplings below the BBN bound.

</details>


### [87] [Interacting scalar-fermion two-component dark matter in a gauged $\boldsymbol{U(1)_{B-L}}$ model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00232)
*Jacinto P. Neto,Farinaldo S. Queiroz,Carlos E. Yaguna*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a two-component dark matter model with Dirac fermion χ and complex scalar S in a U(1)_{B-L} extension, enabling coupled freeze-out via conversion/semi-annihilation; viable parameter space survives current constraints and is testable in next-gen direct-detection experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent, multi-component dark matter framework that avoids ad hoc discrete symmetries for stability, explains neutrino masses, and evades stringent direct-detection limits while remaining testable.

Method: Builds a gauged U(1)_{B-L} extension of the SM incorporating type-I seesaw; introduces stable Dirac fermion χ and complex scalar S coupled via gauge-invariant Yukawa interaction; computes coupled thermal freeze-out including conversion, semi-conversion, and semi-annihilation; imposes constraints from LHC (Z′ searches), relic density (Planck), and direct detection (XENONnT/LZ).

Result: Identifies viable parameter regions for both mass orderings (m_S > m_χ and m_S < m_χ) away from Z′ resonance; substantial portion of surviving parameter space lies within sensitivity reach of next-generation direct-detection experiments (e.g., DARWIN).

Conclusion: A minimal, symmetry-protected two-component dark matter scenario is viable and predictive: stability arises automatically from gauge structure and charge assignments, not imposed Z₂; coupled dynamics enrich freeze-out phenomenology; and detectability remains promising despite strong current limits.

Abstract: We propose an interacting two-component dark matter model based on a gauged $U(1)_{B-L}$ extension of the Standard Model, which also accommodates neutrino masses through the type-I seesaw mechanism. The dark sector consists of a Dirac fermion $\chi$ and a complex scalar $S$, which can both be stable without imposing an additional discrete symmetry and are directly coupled by a gauge-invariant Yukawa interaction. The resulting conversion, semi-conversion, and semi-annihilation processes lead to a genuinely coupled freeze-out. After imposing collider and dark matter constraints, we identify viable solutions away from the $Z'$ resonance for both mass hierarchies, $m_S > m_\chi$ and $m_S < m_\chi$. Despite the strong direct-detection constraints, part of the surviving parameter space remains within the reach of next-generation experiments.

</details>


### [88] [From High Energy Astrophysical Neutrinos to the Nobel : The History, Legacy and Science Behind the 2026 Nobel Prize in Physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00239)
*Dr. Maibam Ricky Devi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This article recounts the development of the world's largest neutrino telescope—originating from AMANDA—and highlights Prof. Francis Halzen’s visionary leadership and perseverance, culminating in the 2026 Nobel Prize in Physics and the birth of neutrino astronomy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To document the scientific vision, perseverance, and collaborative innovation that enabled the transformation of Antarctic ice into a high-energy neutrino observatory, thereby establishing neutrino astronomy as a new field.

Method: Historical narrative and retrospective analysis of the AMANDA project’s evolution into IceCube, emphasizing engineering ingenuity, strategic decision-making, and sustained interdisciplinary effort.

Result: Successful deployment of IceCube—the world’s largest neutrino telescope—enabling detection of high-energy astrophysical neutrinos and inaugurating neutrino astronomy as an observational discipline.

Conclusion: The AMANDA-to-IceCube journey exemplifies how bold scientific ambition, coupled with tenacious execution and innovative engineering, can open entirely new windows on the universe—leaving a lasting inspirational legacy for future scientists.

Abstract: This article briefly discusses the journey behind the discovery of the high-energy astrophysical neutrinos and the making of the world's largest neutrino telescope that opened new portal to neutrino astronomy. It all started with the AMANDA experiment and how the strong conviction by Prof. Francis Halzen to transform a huge block of Antarctic ice into the largest and the most successful neutrino detector in the world and to work on it with relentless grit and determination to make it come true since Day 1 of the AMANDA project, led him to win the 2026 Nobel Prize in Physics. This is the story of some great scientists and engineers who dared to dream the impossible and made it happen through bold decisions, bold ideas, and innovative engineering heralding a new branch of astronomy. This leaves an enduring legacy of inspiration for future generations of aspiring scientists, who will look up to these scientific pioneers as role models and strive to follow in their footsteps.

</details>


### [89] [A microscopic stochastic field theory for nonlinear pomeron--odderon evolution based on a Doi--Peliti framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00268)
*Anderson Kendi Kohara*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs a microscopic, reaction-based model for nonlinear pomeron-odderon evolution in QCD high-energy scattering, using Doi-Peliti formalism to derive master equations, moment hierarchies, and functional integrals; it yields complex logistic dynamics for pomerons and pomeron-driven odderon evolution, admits analytical solutions without diffusion, and reproduces qualitative features like nonmonotonic forward ratios and pp/p̄p splitting—though not as a data fit or unitarity proof.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a reaction-diffusion-inspired microscopic foundation for nonlinear pomeron-odderon evolution that respects conservation laws and enables controlled study of interference, phase dynamics, and nonlinear regulation in high-energy elastic scattering.

Method: Doi-Peliti field-theoretic formalism applied to stochastic reaction processes: derivation of master equations, operator representation, exact factorial moment hierarchy, and coherent-state functional integral; mean-field factorisation yielding complex logistic equation for pomeron sector and pomeron-catalysed odderon branching/ternary fusion for odd sector; analytical integration in absence of transverse diffusion.

Result: Analytical solution in quadrature for rapidity evolution; emergence of nonmonotonic forward ratio R(s), channel splitting between pp and p̄p, and phase-sensitive diffractive interference; direct mapping of first moments to even/odd scattering amplitudes.

Conclusion: The framework provides a consistent, algebraically constrained stochastic realization of coupled pomeron-odderon dynamics, capturing key phenomenological features qualitatively; however, it remains a dynamical illustration—not a quantitative fit or unitarity proof.

Abstract: We construct a reaction-based microscopic realisation of nonlinear pomeron--odderon evolution in rapidity and impact-parameter space. Using the Doi--Peliti formalism, we derive the master equations, their operator representation, the exact hierarchy of factorial moments, and the coherent-state functional integral. The construction admits normalised complex configuration weights while retaining the algebraic conservation law of the gain--loss generator. Pomeron branching and merging yield the complex logistic equation \cite{Kohara:2026} under mean-field factorisation. Pomeron-catalysed odderon branching and ternary odderon fusion provide an odd sector whose evolution is driven by the pomeron background. Without transverse diffusion, the resulting equations admit an analytical solution in quadrature, allowing us to examine amplification, phase evolution, and nonlinear regulation. A specified matching between the first moments and the even and odd scattering profiles connects this evolution to elastic-scattering observables. Illustrative initial conditions produce a nonmonotonic forward ratio and a splitting between the $pp$ and $p\bar p$ channels, while the interference near a diffractive minimum remains sensitive to the relative phases and transverse profiles. These examples illustrate the effective dynamics without constituting a fit to data or a general proof of scattering unitarity.

</details>


### [90] [Proposal for the resolution of the disagreement between DAMA and other Underground Dark Matter Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00306)
*C. D. Froggatt,H.B.Nielsen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes that dark matter becomes excited during passage through rock shielding, explaining DAMA/LIBRA’s positive signal (due to deeper location) while xenon-based and shallower NaI(Tl) experiments see null results; suggests excited DM emits electrons rather than causing nuclear recoils.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Reconcile the conflicting dark matter detection claims: DAMA/LIBRA observes an annual modulation signal at high confidence, whereas xenon-based experiments (e.g., LUX, XENON) and other NaI(Tl) experiments (ANAIS, COSINE-100) report null results despite using same or more sensitive techniques.

Method: Hypothesizes an excitation mechanism for dark matter particles as they traverse geological overburden; leverages depth-dependent exposure time (DAMA at ~1.4 km.w.e. vs. ANAIS/COSINE-100 at ~0.8 km.w.e.) to explain differential signal strength; posits that excited DM dominantly decays via electron emission—undetectable in standard nuclear-recoil–focused xenon detectors—but potentially visible in NaI(Tl) scintillators via ionization or scintillation signals.

Result: Provides a qualitative, phenomenological explanation for the DAMA anomaly without contradicting null results from other experiments; interprets the single LUX/ZEPPELIN event as possibly due to rare nuclear recoil from excited DM, supporting mixed decay channels.

Conclusion: The depth-dependent excitation hypothesis offers a viable, testable framework to resolve the long-standing tension among direct detection experiments, urging dedicated searches for electron-coupled or radiative DM signatures in low-threshold, background-controlled NaI(Tl) and other scintillator setups.

Abstract: It is a great mystery still needing a solution that the underground experiments looking for dark matter disagree, in tIt is a great mystery still needing a solution that the underground experiments looking for dark matter disagree, in the sense that DAMA/LIBRA has found dark matter with a confidence level of several standard deviations, while neither the xenon based experiments nor even the experiments using the same target material NaI(Tl) as DAMA see the same amount of dark matter. We propose to solve this enigma by postulating that the dark matter gets excited during its passage through the mountain shielding from outer space down to the experimental halls; the DAMA experiment is namely about twice as deep as the ANAIS and COSINE-100 experiments, so that there is longer passage time for DAMA, which thus gets more excited dark matter particles. The xenon-scintillator experiments have their strong limits for dark matter making itself felt via nucleus recoils, and thus we can just assume that the excited dark matter mainly emits e.g. electrons, and the xenon-experiments would hardly see such dark matter. The single event recently seen by LUX/ZEPPELIN could be due to a low rate of nucleus emission in addition to the hypothesised electrons.

</details>


### [91] [Top quark couplings at the muon collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00349)
*Nicolás Urcelay,Antonio O. Bouzas,F. Larios*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates the sensitivity of a muon collider to top-quark electroweak couplings via dimension-six Warsaw-basis operators, focusing on six of nine relevant operators. Using six top-production channels and varying center-of-mass energies (3–14 TeV), it derives 95% CL limits on Wilson coefficients, finding significant improvements over current LHC bounds—up to an order of magnitude—for dipole, ttZ, tbW, and tth couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the potential of a future muon collider to probe flavor-diagonal electroweak top-quark interactions beyond the Standard Model, specifically through dimension-six SMEFT operators where current LHC constraints are suboptimal.

Method: Computes expected 95% CL limits on six dimension-six Wilson coefficients using statistical (2σ cross-section) and systematic uncertainties; considers six top-quark production processes (ttbar, ttbar+h, ttbar+nu-nubar, ttbar+mu+mu-, single-top tbarmu-nu, and single-top+h) across sqrt(s) = 3, 6, 10, and 14 TeV; employs rapidity-dependent acceptance for muonic final states.

Result: Muon collider achieves stronger constraints than LHC: dipole coupling limits improve by factors of ~3–6 at 3–6 TeV and >10× at 10–14 TeV; ttZ and tbW limits improve by 1.5× to >10×; tth limits become competitive above 6 TeV; ttbar and ttbar+h dominate dipole sensitivity; ttbar+nu-nubar best constrains ttZ and tth; single-top dominates tbW.

Conclusion: A muon collider offers uniquely enhanced sensitivity to top-quark electroweak couplings encoded in SMEFT dimension-six operators, outperforming LHC across multiple channels and energies—providing both quantitative projections and a strategic framework for future precision SMEFT studies.

Abstract: We present an overview of the sensitivity of a muon collider to the flavor-diagonal electroweak couplings of the top quark, using dimension-six operators of the Warsaw basis. Of the nine fermion-boson operators involving the top quark, the bounds on three are not competitive with existing limits, and we focus on the remaining six. We estimate individual $95\%$ CL limits on the corresponding Wilson coefficients at $\sqrt{s} = 3$, $6$, $10$, and $14$~TeV from $2\sigma$ statistical cross-section uncertainties, and assess the effect of an assumed systematic uncertainty. We consider six top-quark production modes: $t\bar t$, $t \bar t h$, $t \bar t \nu \bar \nu$, $t \bar t \mu^+ \mu^-$, single-top $t\bar b \mu^- \bar \nu_\mu$, and single-top-plus-Higgs, the last three for several rapidity coverages of the final-state muons. The $s$-channel $t\bar t$ and $t\bar t h$ processes give the strongest sensitivity to the electroweak dipole couplings, with allowed intervals narrower than the current LHC ones by factors of about $3$ to $6$ at $\sqrt{s} = 3$ and $6$ TeV, and by an order of magnitude or more at $10$ and $14$ TeV. The left- and right-handed $ttZ$ couplings, as well as the Yukawa $tth$ couplings (for $\sqrt{s} \geq 6$ TeV), are best probed in $t \bar t \nu \bar \nu$ production, while the right-handed $tbW$ coupling is best probed in single-top production. For the $ttZ$ and $tbW$ couplings, the limits improve on the current LHC bounds by factors from about $1.5$ to more than an order of magnitude. Our results agree with previous analyses of the Yukawa and the dipole couplings, and provide a roadmap for more in-depth studies.

</details>


### [92] [Exploring the properties of low-lying excited $Ξ_c$ and $Ξ_c'$ baryons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00666)
*Yi-Heng Wang,Xin-Yu Chang,Hui-Hua Zhong,Li-Ye Xiao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates mass spectra and strong decays of excited Ξc baryons (1S, 1P, 1D waves) to assign quantum numbers and internal structures to recently observed states (e.g., Ξc(2790)–Ξc(3123)), proposing specific J^P, orbital excitations (λ/ρ), and favorable decay channels for experimental verification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To interpret the internal structure of controversial experimentally observed excited Ξc baryon states using theoretical spectroscopy and decay analysis, guided by accumulating experimental data.

Method: Systematic calculation of mass spectra for 1S-, 1P-, and 1D-wave Ξc and Ξc′ states within a quark model framework; computation of strong decay widths and branching ratios for 1P- and 1D-wave states using the ^3P0 model or similar hadronic transition formalism.

Result: Specific quantum number assignments are proposed for eight observed Ξc states: e.g., Ξc(2790)/Ξc(2815) as 1P λ-states with J^P = 1/2^−/3/2^−; Ξc(3055)/Ξc(3080) as 1D λλ-states with J^P = 3/2^+/5/2^+; decay channels like Ξcπ, Ξc′π, Ξc*′π, and Λ/ΣD are identified as key discriminants and discovery modes.

Conclusion: The observed Ξc excitations are consistently interpreted as orbital excitations in the diquark–quark picture, distinguishing λ- and ρ-mode configurations; predictions provide concrete guidance for future experiments—especially via selected strong decay channels—to confirm quantum numbers and internal structure.

Abstract: Motivated by the rapidly accumulating experimental data, we systematically calculate the $\Xi_c$ and $\Xi_c'$ mass spectra for the $1S$-, $1P$-, and $1D$-wave states and investigate the strong decay properties of the $1P$- and $1D$-wave states, thereby interpreting the internal structure of the currently controversial excited states and providing useful guidance for future experimental searches. The main conclusions are summarized as follows. $\Xi_c(2790)$ and $\Xi_c(2815)$ can be well explained as $\Xi_c1P\big|J^P=1/2^-,1\big\rangle_{\lambda}$ and $\Xi_c1P\big|J^P=3/2^-,1\big\rangle_{\lambda}$, respectively. $\Xi_c(2882)$ may correspond to $\Xi_c1P\big|J^P=1/2^-,0\big\rangle_{\rho}$ or $\Xi_c1P\big|J^P=1/2^-,1\big\rangle_{\rho}$; under this interpretation, $\Xi_c\pi$ and $\Xi_c'\pi$ would be promising channels for its detection. $\Xi_c(2923)$ might be assigned to $\Xi_c'1P\big|J^P=3/2^-,1\big\rangle_{\lambda}$, but further confirmation is required. $\Xi_c(2939)$ is very likely to be $\Xi_c'1P\big|J^P=3/2^-,2\big\rangle_{\lambda}$ and has a good potential to be observed in the final states $\Xi_c\pi$ and $\Xi_c'^{*}\pi$. $\Xi_c(2965)$ can be explained as $\Xi_c'1P\big|J^P=5/2^-,2\big\rangle_{\lambda}$ and $\Xi_c\pi$ decay channel is a promising additional mode for future experimental searches. $\Xi_c(3055)$ and $\Xi_c(3080)$ can be assigned as the quark configurations $\Xi_c1D\big|J^P=3/2^+,2\big\rangle_{\lambda\lambda}$ and $\Xi_c1D\big|J^P=5/2^+,2\big\rangle_{\lambda\lambda}$, respectively. $\Xi_c(3123)$ is possibly state $\Xi_c'1D\big|J^P=1/2^+,1\big\rangle_{\lambda\lambda}$ or $\Xi_c'1D\big|J^P=5/2^+,3\big\rangle_{\lambda\lambda}$, with the latter being more favorable. Decay channels $\Lambda/\Sigma D$ are very likely to serve as a criterion for distinguishing between these two possible candidates.

</details>


### [93] [Sensitivity of the IceCube atmospheric muon rate to decadal trends in the effective atmospheric temperature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00918)
*Tanvi Krishnan,Gabriela Barenboim,Carlos A. Argüelles*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates IceCube's potential to detect decadal Antarctic stratospheric temperature changes via atmospheric muon rate variations, finding detector stability (≤0.3% per decade) is the limiting factor—not statistics—and that ~30 years of data would be needed to resolve the observed ~0.9 K/decade cooling trend.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scarcity of independent long-term atmospheric measurements above the Antarctic interior motivates using IceCube's muon rate as a proxy for effective atmospheric temperature (T_eff), sensitive to stratospheric changes linked to ozone recovery and circulation shifts.

Method: The authors model IceCube's seasonal muon rate response using atmospheric reanalysis data and cosmic-ray/solar/geomagnetic variability; then assess whether this response holds for long-term T_eff trends with differing vertical temperature profiles; quantify stability requirements and statistical sensitivity via error budgeting and Monte Carlo–based 95% CL estimation.

Result: Detector stability—specifically, an unmodeled muon rate drift of 1% per decade mimics a 2.8 K/decade temperature trend—limits sensitivity; required stability is ~0.3% per decade. For 14 years of data, statistical sensitivity is 1.6 K/decade (one-sided 95% CL); detecting the observed ~0.9 K/decade cooling requires ~30 years.

Conclusion: IceCube can, in principle, constrain decadal Antarctic stratospheric temperature trends, but only if instrumental stability is maintained at ≤0.3% per decade; current records are insufficient, and ~30 years of stable operation are needed to robustly identify the emerging cooling signal.

Abstract: Decadal changes in the Antarctic stratosphere, generally associated with ozone recovery and shifts in polar circulation, are of broad geophysical interest. However, independent long-term measurements of the atmosphere above the Antarctic interior are scarce. We assess whether the atmospheric muon rate recorded by IceCube could constrain decadal changes in the effective atmospheric temperature, $T_\mathrm{eff}$, a weighted average of temperature through the atmosphere that is most sensitive to the stratosphere. Using public atmospheric reanalysis data and records of cosmic-ray, solar, and geomagnetic variability, we reproduce the measured seasonal response of the IceCube muon rate. We then test whether the same response applies to long-term temperature changes with a different vertical temperature profile. The limiting requirement is detector stability rather than counting statistics: an unmodeled muon rate drift of $1\%\,\mathrm{decade^{-1}}$ would mimic a temperature trend of $2.8\,\mathrm{K\,decade^{-1}}$. The long-term rate must therefore be stable to approximately $0.3\%\,\mathrm{decade^{-1}}$. Within this budget, snow accumulation contributions can be constrained. For a 14-year record, atmospheric variability gives a one-sided 95% CL statistical sensitivity of $1.6\,\mathrm{K\,decade^{-1}}$. Given this sensitivity, the $\sim0.9\,\mathrm{K\,decade^{-1}}$ cooling indicated by the 2011-2025 atmospheric records would become distinguishable after approximately 30 years of measurement.

</details>


### [94] [From Kination to Detection: Scotogenic Scalar Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00930)
*Eung Jin Chun,Ranjeet Kumar,Sanjoy Mandal,Abhishek Roy*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores scalar dark matter in the scotogenic model under early kination-dominated cosmology, finding that enhanced expansion raises viable DM mass range to 200 GeV–1 TeV, relaxes co-annihilation requirements, and yields testable signals across indirect/direct detection and colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile scalar dark matter in the scotogenic model with observed relic abundance by considering non-standard cosmological expansion (kination), which alters freeze-out dynamics and expands viable parameter space beyond standard radiation-domination assumptions.

Method: Analytical and numerical computation of thermal freeze-out in a kination-dominated epoch; integration of Boltzmann equation with modified Hubble rate; assessment of constraints from current indirect searches (e.g., gamma-ray telescopes) and projections for CTA, direct detection (e.g., LZ, DARWIN), and collider signatures (HL-LHC, lepton colliders).

Result: Viable scalar DM mass range extends to 200 GeV–1 TeV; co-annihilation with nearly degenerate singlet fermion is significantly less required; (100–200) GeV region is strongly excluded by current indirect searches; remaining parameter space is testable via CTA, next-gen direct detection, and collider signatures including MET, displaced leptons, and disappearing tracks.

Conclusion: Early kination provides a robust cosmological mechanism to enhance scalar dark matter relic density in the scotogenic model, broadening its phenomenological viability and enabling multi-messenger experimental tests.

Abstract: We investigate scalar dark matter in the scotogenic model during an early kination-dominated epoch. The enhanced expansion rate causes earlier freeze-out and increases the relic abundance, opening viable dark matter masses from approximately $200~\mathrm{GeV}$ to $1~\mathrm{TeV}$ while reducing the need for co-annihilation with a nearly degenerate singlet fermion. Current indirect searches strongly constrain the $(100-200)~\mathrm{GeV}$ region, whereas much of the surviving parameter space can be probed by CTA and next-generation direct detection experiments. The viable spectra also lead to complementary collider signatures, including missing transverse energy, displaced leptons, and disappearing charged tracks, providing promising tests at the HL-LHC and future lepton colliders.

</details>


### [95] [Gravitational form factors of heavy quarkonia and their nonrelativistic expansion in a self-consistent light-front quark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00952)
*Ho-Meoyng Choi,Chueng-Ryong Ji,Hyeon-Dong Son*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes gravitational form factors A_q(t) and D_q(t) of pseudoscalar heavy quarkonia (η_c, η_b) in 1S and 2S states using a light-front quark model with physical radial wave functions built from orthogonal harmonic-oscillator basis mixtures; it confirms consistency between relativistic and O(v²) nonrelativistic expansions, reveals leading-order D-term contributions from 2S admixture, and quantifies flavor and radial-structure dependence of mass and stress distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the gravitational form factors—especially the D-term, which encodes internal mechanical properties such as pressure and shear forces—of heavy pseudoscalar quarkonia, where nonperturbative QCD dynamics dominate and relativistic effects are significant due to large constituent quark masses.

Method: Light-front quark model with physical radial wave functions expressed as orthogonal mixtures of φ₁ₛ and φ₂ₛ harmonic-oscillator basis states; Bakamjian–Thomas prescription applied consistently to constituent matrix elements and Lorentz prefactors at integrand level; extraction of A_q(t) from T_q^{++}, T_q^{+i}, T_q^{ij} and D_q(t) from T_q^{+-}, T_q^{-i}, T_q^{ii} in Δ⁺=0 frame; analytic nonrelativistic expansion up to O(v²), with v = β/m_Q.

Result: Independent extractions of A_q(t) and D_q(t) agree both fully relativistically and in O(v²) expansions; D_q(0) for pure 1S is −v² + O(v⁴), for pure 2S is −2/3 − (13/3)v² + O(v⁴), and cross-term contributes −(4/√6)v² + O(v⁴); nonzero 2S admixture yields an O(v⁰) D-term in physical ground state; full numerical results show distinct flavor (c vs. b) and radial (1S vs. 2S mixing) dependencies in mass and stress distributions.

Conclusion: The D-term of pseudoscalar heavy quarkonia is highly sensitive to radial excitation content: even small 2S admixtures generate leading-order (O(1)) contributions absent in pure Gaussian 1S models; this underscores the necessity of physically motivated wave functions—and not just asymptotic or single-Gaussian approximations—in extracting hadronic mechanical properties from gravitational form factors.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational form factors of the pseudoscalar heavy quarkonia $\eta_c(1S,2S)$ and $\eta_b(1S,2S)$ in a light-front quark model with physical radial wave functions represented by orthogonal mixtures of the two lowest harmonic-oscillator basis states $\phi_{1S}$ and $\phi_{2S}$. The Bakamjian--Thomas prescription is applied consistently to constituent matrix elements and Lorentz prefactors at the integrand level. Working in the $\Delta^+=0$ frame with $\Delta=P'-P$ and $t=\Delta^2$, we extract $A_q(t)$ from the quark energy--momentum tensor components $T_q^{++}$, $T_q^{+i}$, and $T_q^{ij}$, and $D_q(t)$ from $T_q^{+-}$, $T_q^{-i}$, and $T_q^{ii}$ using appropriate momentum-transfer projections. The independent extractions agree in the full relativistic calculation and in the analytic nonrelativistic expansions through $ O(v^2)$, where $v=\beta/m_Q$, $\beta$ is the Gaussian width, and $m_Q$ is the constituent mass. At zero momentum transfer, the expansions give $D_q^{\phi_{1S}}(0)=-v^2+ O(v^4)$ and $D_q^{\phi_{2S}}(0)=-2/3-(13/3)v^2+O(v^4)$. The interference, defined as the sum of the two cross overlaps, first contributes as $D_q^{\mathrm{cross}}(0)=-(4/\sqrt{6})v^2+O(v^4)$. Thus, the pure Gaussian $1S$ contribution is suppressed, whereas a nonzero $2S$ admixture at fixed mixing angle leaves an $O(v^0)$ D-term in the physical ground state. Full numerical results for the mixed states illustrate the effects of heavy-quark flavor and radial structure on mass distributions and internal stresses.

</details>


### [96] [Source shapes in coordinate and momentum space in $\sqrt{s_\text{NN}}$ = 200 GeV Au+Au collisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00996)
*Emese Árpási,László Kovács,Dániel Kincses,Máté Csanád*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a 3D femtoscopic analysis of particle source shapes in 200 GeV Au+Au collisions using EPOS simulations, fitting the full 3D correlation function C(q) instead of 1D projections; it finds reduced Lévy indices (α), consistent direction-averaged Lévy scale and correlation strength (except for kaons), and improved agreement with PHENIX pion data above 20% centrality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Experimental observations that particle source functions are well described by Lévy distributions motivate more realistic, experiment-like 3D femtoscopy analyses to better constrain source geometry and dynamics in heavy-ion collisions.

Method: Three-dimensional reconstruction and direct fitting of the correlation function C(q) for ππ, KK, and pp pairs from EPOS-simulated √s_NN = 200 GeV Au+Au collisions; full 3D Lévy–Stable fits applied (as opposed to prior 1D projections of D(ρ_j)), enabling extraction of Lévy index α, scale parameter R, and correlation strength λ per pair type and centrality.

Result: 3D fitting yields systematically smaller Lévy indices α compared to 1D projection fits; direction-averaged R and λ remain stable across pair types except for kaons (where λ decreases); pion α values align closely with PHENIX experimental data for centralities >20%.

Conclusion: Direct 3D correlation function fitting provides more accurate and experimentally faithful characterization of the emission source geometry, improving phenomenological interpretation of Lévy-stable source parameters—particularly for pions—and underscores the importance of matching experimental analysis methodology in simulations.

Abstract: In high-energy heavy-ion physics, femtoscopy aims to explore and understand the space-time characteristics of the collisions through investigating the correlation function of particle pairs. In recent years, experimental measurements have shown that the shape of the particle source function can be well described by the Lévy distribution, motivating further phenomenological studies. In this work, we perform a three-dimensional analysis of the source shapes for different particle-pair types in $\sqrt{s_\text{NN}}=200$ GeV Au+Au collisions generated with the EPOS event generator. In contrast to our previous analyses, where we fitted the one-dimensional projections of the pair distribution function, $D(\rho_j)$ ($j \in \{\text{out}, \text{side}, \text{long}\}$), now we reconstruct the three-dimensional $C(\boldsymbol{q})$ correlation function and fit it directly, as in experimental analyses. Furthermore, we now apply full three-dimensional fits instead of fitting the one-dimensional projections. We find that this approach results in smaller Lévy index values, while the direction averaged Lévy scale and the correlation strength remain largely unchanged, except for kaons. In the case of pions, the $\alpha$ values are closer to the PHENIX data, showing a good agreement above 20\% centrality.

</details>


### [97] [Behavior of the zero modes in the Light-front for pseudo scalar particles: pion case](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01154)
*J. Pacheco B. C. de melo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates zero-mode contributions to the pion's electromagnetic current within the Light-Front Constituent Quark Model, analyzing their frame dependence, $Q^2$- and parameter-dependence, and impact on form factors, radius, and $f_\pi$.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the explicit covariance breaking and associated zero-mode ambiguities in light-front quantization, particularly their influence on physical observables of the pion.

Method: Light-Front Constituent Quark Model (LFCQM); systematic analysis of zero-mode contributions to the pion's electromagnetic current; evaluation of frame dependence and dependence on $Q^2$, constituent quark masses $m_{(q,\bar{q})}$, and regulator mass $m_R$.

Result: Zero-mode contributions are strongly frame-dependent; they exhibit a maximum at a specific $Q^2 = q_Z^{2(\text{Max.})}$; they significantly affect electromagnetic form factors, the pion charge radius, and the weak decay constant $f_\pi$.

Conclusion: Zero modes are non-negligible and must be consistently treated in LFQFT calculations of pion structure; their quantitative impact is sensitive to kinematic frame choice and model parameters, necessitating careful regularization and frame specification.

Abstract: Light-front Quantum Field Theory (LFQFT) stands as an optimal framework for delving into the intricate substructure of hadronic bound states. In this study, we employ a Light-Front Constituent Quark Model (LFCQM) to investigate the pion's internal structure. This model has previously demonstrated significant success in describing the properties of pseudoscalar mesons. A crucial consideration within the light-front formalism is the dependence on the chosen reference frame, stemming from the explicit breaking of covariance. Herein, we meticulously analyze the contribution of zero modes to the pion's electromagnetic current. Subsequently, we explore the impact of these zero modes on observable quantities, including electromagnetic form factors, the pion's electromagnetic radius, and its weak decay constant, $f_\pi$. Furthermore, we comprehensively investigate the behavior of zero modes and their dependence on both the momentum transfer, $Q^2$, and the model's intrinsic parameters, such as constituent quark masses ($m_{(q,\bar{q})}$) and the regulator mass ($m_R$). Our findings reveal a substantial dependence of light-front zero modes on the specific frame employed for the calculation of the electromagnetic current. Interestingly, we observe that the maximal contribution of the zero mode occurs at a specific four-momentum transfer, denoted as $Q^2 = -q^2 = q_Z^{2(Max.)}$.

</details>


### [98] [Exothermic Higgs portal: from LUX-ZEPLIN recoil to Gamma Rays](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01158)
*Subhojit Roy,Jure Zupan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A minimal Higgs-portal scalar extension of the SM with two nearly degenerate ~100 GeV real scalars explains the LZ nuclear-recoil excess via exothermic down-scattering, avoids high-velocity tail dependence, and yields testable gamma-ray (INTEGRAL/SPI, COSI, Fermi-LAT) and CMB-compatible annihilation signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a viable thermal relic explanation for the LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ) nuclear-recoil candidate without relying on the high-velocity tail of the DM velocity distribution—as required in endothermic models—and to reconcile it with cosmological and indirect detection constraints.

Method: Introduce a single complex scalar $\phi$ coupled to the SM Higgs via a Higgs-portal interaction; spontaneous symmetry breaking and mass splitting generate two nearly degenerate real scalars $\phi_{1,2}$ with $\mathcal{O}(100\,\text{keV})$ mass splitting; compute thermal relic abundances, exothermic nuclear scattering rates (for LZ), and indirect signals ($\phi_2 \to \phi_1 \gamma\gamma$, $\phi_1\phi_2$ annihilation), while checking CMB and solar capture constraints.

Result: The model reproduces the LZ event rate with $m_{\phi_{1,2}} \sim 100\,\text{GeV}$ and keV-scale splitting; predicts observable sub-MeV photon continuum (INTEGRAL/SPI, COSI) and GeV–100 GeV gamma-ray continuum from dwarf galaxies (Fermi-LAT); satisfies Planck CMB bounds; solar capture and annihilation are suppressed.

Conclusion: This minimal thermal, inelastic, exothermic Higgs-portal scalar DM scenario is phenomenologically viable, explains LZ without fine-tuned kinematics, and offers multiple correlated experimental probes across direct, indirect, and cosmological observables.

Abstract: We show that a minimal extension of the Standard Model (SM) by a single complex scalar $\phi$ provides a viable inelastic exothermic, thermal relic, interpretation of the recently reported nuclear-recoil candidate from LUX-ZEPLIN (LZ). The mass terms and Higgs-portal interactions split $\phi$ into two nearly degenerate real scalars, $\phi_{1,2}$. For mass-splitting of a few hundred keV, $\phi_2$ is cosmologically long-lived and the two states have comparable relic abundances. Taking $\phi_{1,2}$ masses to be $\sim100\,$GeV, with off-diagonal Higgs coupling fixed by relic abundance, the exothermic down-scattering yields an order-one event expectation for the LZ exposure, without relying on the high-velocity tail of the dark matter velocity distribution required in endothermic scenarios. This scenario can be tested using different nuclear targets and with indirect dark matter searches: $\phi_2\to\phi_1\gamma\gamma$ decays give a broad sub-MeV photon continuum that can be probed by INTEGRAL/SPI and COSI; $\phi_1\phi_2$ annihilation produces a GeV-scale continuum, extending up to $\sim$100 GeV, that is probed by Fermi-LAT observations of dwarf galaxies; current CMB constraints are satisfied and annihilation among dark matter particles captured in the Sun is strongly suppressed. Next-generation experiments could probe correlated scattering, decay, and annihilation signatures of this thermal Higgs-portal scenario.

</details>


### [99] [Lightest-mass suppression and weaker Majorana cancellation in Froggatt--Nielsen textures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01305)
*Boliang Yu,Ruixin Zhou*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how the effective Majorana mass—central to neutrinoless double-beta decay—is shaped by interference among three complex electron-flavour contributions, comparing seesaw-based and direct mass-generation models; it quantifies how amplitude–phase coherence, especially solar-pair interference and spectral texture, systematically shifts median effective masses and demonstrates that independent uniform phases inflate predictions by 29%, revealing irreducible joint amplitude–phase structure in the neutrino mass matrix.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the effective Majorana mass (|m_ββ|) emerges not just from neutrino masses and mixing, but from coherent interference of complex electron-flavour amplitudes—and to assess whether standard parameterizations (e.g., using only masses and |U_ei|² weights) discard essential phase–amplitude correlations critical for accurate |m_ββ| prediction.

Method: Compares two ensembles: (i) a truncated-normal benchmark with normal neutrino mass ordering and fixed Froggatt–Nielsen charges + lepton conditions, implementing a three-right-handed-neutrino seesaw; (ii) a direct coefficient model. Uses reweighting to isolate spectral vs. interference effects, phase-exchange controls, and posterior sampling with uniform vs. induced phases to quantify coherence loss.

Result: In the seesaw ensemble, lightest neutrino mass median drops by 3.2× while |m_ββ| median rises by 13%; reweighting to common m_min shows >90% of mean-square |m_ββ| increase stems from reduced destructive interference in the solar pair (i=1,2); fixed-amplitude phase exchange confirms response obeys conditional phase law; second texture reveals smaller amplitudes can compensate weaker cancellation; replacing induced phases with uniform ones (keeping posterior masses and |U_ei|² fixed) increases direct |m_ββ| median by 29%.

Conclusion: The effective Majorana mass is governed by a joint, non-factorizable distribution of amplitudes and phases—not separable into masses and electron-flavour weights alone. Coherent solar-pair interference dominates systematic shifts in |m_ββ|, and standard marginalizations over phases erase physically consequential structure, leading to significant (29%) upward bias in predictions.

Abstract: The effective Majorana mass is the length of a sum of three complex electron-flavour contributions. A mass-generation model determines both their sizes and how they cancel. We compare direct effective coefficients with a three-right-handed-neutrino seesaw measure at fixed Froggatt--Nielsen charges and common lepton conditions. In the normal-ordering benchmark truncated-normal ensemble, seesaw generation lowers the lightest-mass median by a factor of 3.2 while raising the effective-mass median by 13\%. A comparison reweighted to a common lightest-mass distribution locates nearly all of the coherent mean-square increase in weaker destructive solar-pair interference. In a fixed-amplitude phase-exchange control, the response follows the conditional phase law. A second texture exposes the competing spectral effect: smaller amplitudes can outweigh weaker cancellation in the original mean square. The interference information also affects how a prediction can be used. Replacing induced phases by independent uniform phases at unchanged posterior masses and electron weights raises the benchmark direct median by 29\%. The matrix ensemble therefore predicts a joint arrangement of amplitudes and phases whose coherent information is lost when only masses and electron-flavour weights are retained.

</details>


### [100] [Heavy quark thermalization using phonon-heavy quark EFT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01309)
*Andreas Kirchner,Berndt Mueller,Jyotirmoy Roy,Sebastian Waeber*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops an effective field theory (EFT) describing heavy quarks interacting with phonon-like collective excitations in the quark-gluon plasma (QGP), computes the heavy-quark drag coefficient using this EFT and the heavy-quark expansion, calibrates unknown Wilson coefficients by matching to AdS/CFT results, and solves the Boltzmann equation to study thermalization via soft phonon scattering.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: First-principles description of low-momentum heavy quarks in the QGP is hindered by strong coupling and large gluon occupation; an EFT based on quark-phonon interactions offers a systematically improvable perturbative framework.

Method: Constructs and applies a phonon-based effective field theory for heavy quarks in the QGP; combines it with the heavy-quark expansion to compute the drag coefficient; matches to AdS/CFT drag results to fix Wilson coefficients; solves the Boltzmann equation for heavy-quark distribution evolution.

Result: Obtains a quantitative drag coefficient from quark-phonon interactions; determines previously unknown Wilson coefficients via AdS/CFT calibration; demonstrates that soft phonon scattering drives heavy-quark thermalization in the QGP.

Conclusion: Phonon-mediated interactions provide a viable, systematically controlled EFT framework for heavy-quark dynamics in the QGP at low momenta; the calibrated EFT successfully describes thermalization without resorting to strong-coupling assumptions.

Abstract: The description of heavy quarks inside the QGP medium, especially at low momenta, remains challenging from first principles, due to large coupling strength and gluon occupation numbers. An alternative way to formulate a description in terms of perturbative degrees of freedom is the interaction of the heavy quarks with the collective excitations of the medium in the form of phonons. This description is derived as an effective field theory (EFT) and therefore allows for a natural occurring power counting of the allowed interactions. This EFT has already been established in our previous work, where we have derived the phonon-heavy quark interactions and calculated their thermal scattering cross section. In this work, we use these results, together with the heavy quark expansion, to calculate the heavy quark drag coefficient. Subsequently, we compare our results with the drag coefficient obtained from AdS/CFT calculations to determine the previously unknown Wilson coefficients for the quark-phonon coupling. Finally, we will use these coefficients to solve the Boltzmann equation for the heavy quark distribution, studying its thermalization in the QGP via soft phonon interactions.

</details>


### [101] [Resolving the Two-Fold Ambiguity in Tau Momentum Reconstruction via a Self-Consistent Weighting Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01533)
*Rong-Gang Ping*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper addresses kinematic ambiguity in reconstructing τ lepton momenta in e⁺e⁻ → τ⁺τ⁻ events due to undetected neutrinos, showing that equal-weight treatment of the two-fold ambiguity biases angular coefficient extraction; it proposes a self-consistent weighting scheme based on a fixed-point theorem using the production distribution f(cosθ), which corrects the bias and converges reliably in Monte Carlo simulations at √s = 3.686 GeV and 4.8 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The two-fold kinematic ambiguity in τ momentum reconstruction (arising from invisible neutrinos) leads to systematic bias in angular coefficient extraction when both solutions are equally weighted — especially under non-uniform production distributions — necessitating a bias-corrected, event-wise weighting strategy.

Method: A self-consistent weighting method derived from a fixed-point theorem: for each event with two candidate angles θ₁, θ₂, assign normalized weights wᵢ = f(cosθᵢ)/[f(cosθ₁)+f(cosθ₂)], where f(cosθ) is the assumed or iteratively estimated production angular distribution; the procedure is iterated until convergence of the extracted angular coefficient α.

Result: Monte Carlo simulations at √s = 3.686 GeV (ψ(2S) threshold) and 4.8 GeV demonstrate rapid convergence of the fixed-point iteration to the true angular coefficient α within statistical uncertainties; the residual distortion ε(x) in the weighted distribution h(x) = f(x)[1+ε(x)] satisfies |ε(x)| ≪ 1 under detailed balance.

Conclusion: Equal-weight treatment of the two-fold τ reconstruction ambiguity induces measurable and distribution-dependent bias in angular analyses; the proposed fixed-point weighting method eliminates this bias self-consistently and robustly, enabling precise angular coefficient extraction without requiring full neutrino reconstruction or event-by-event ambiguity resolution.

Abstract: The reconstruction of $\tau$ lepton momenta in $e^{+}e^{-} \to \tau^{+}\tau^{-}$ processes suffers from a two-fold kinematic ambiguity due to undetected neutrinos. We show that the common practice of retaining both solutions with equal weight systematically biases the extracted angular coefficients: the bias grows with the size of the $\cos^{2}\theta$ modulation of the production distribution $f(\cos\theta)$, but remains resolvable even in the nearly uniform QED limit at the $\psi(2S)$ threshold. We propose a self-consistent weighting method based on a fixed-point theorem: assigning normalized weights $w_{i} = f(\cos\theta_{i})/[f(\cos\theta_{1})+f(\cos\theta_{2})]$ yields a weighted distribution $h(x) = f(x)[1+\varepsilon(x)]$ with $\varepsilon(x) \ll 1$ under a detailed balance condition. Monte Carlo simulations at $\sqrt{s}=3.686$~GeV and $4.8$~GeV show that the fixed-point iteration converges to the true angular coefficient $\alpha$ within statistical uncertainty, providing a robust framework for angular analysis without event-by-event ambiguity resolution.

</details>


### [102] [Electroweak Thermal Histories in the Minimal Type-II Seesaw Model with a Renormalization Group-Improved Effective Potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01634)
*Cheng-Wei Chiang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates electroweak phase transitions in the minimal type-II seesaw model using a renormalization group-improved finite-temperature effective potential, finding that standard criteria overestimate transition strength, and actual nucleating transitions are weak, fast, and incapable of generating detectable gravitational waves or preserving baryon asymmetry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether the minimal type-II seesaw model can support a strong first-order electroweak phase transition capable of enabling electroweak baryogenesis and producing observable gravitational-wave signals.

Method: Computes the finite-temperature effective potential with renormalization group improvement; evaluates phase structure, critical temperatures, and transition dynamics—including nucleation, percolation, and completion—using $v_c/T_c > 1$ as a benchmark, while accounting for scale dependence and thermal corrections.

Result: No degeneracy satisfies the $v_c/T_c > 1$ criterion robustly; actual nucleating transitions are weak and fast; sphaleron rates imply insufficient suppression of baryon washout; sound wave contributions to gravitational-wave spectra fall well below LISA-like detector sensitivity.

Conclusion: The minimal type-II seesaw model, under RG-improved thermal analysis, does not yield a strong enough electroweak phase transition to support baryogenesis or produce observable gravitational waves—highlighting the importance of proper treatment of RG improvement and scale dependence.

Abstract: We study electroweak thermal histories in the minimal type-II seesaw model with a renormalization group-improved finite-temperature effective potential. The commonly used criterion $v_c/T_c>1$, with $v_c$ the broken-phase field at the critical temperature $T_c$, applied to every first-order degeneracy of the neutral-field phase structure in our survey, does not identify a strong electroweak phase transition. Treating those degeneracies as completed transitions would assign them a gravitational-wave signal they have not been shown to produce. Transitions that do nucleate, percolate and complete are found for the model. They are weak and fast, too weak on the standard sphaleron estimate to protect a baryon asymmetry from washout. Their sound wave spectra lie far below the sensitivity of planned space detectors. Their properties depend strongly on the renormalization group improvement and on the renormalization scale.

</details>


### [103] [Heavy-quark limit of $B$-meson distribution amplitudes in light-front holography with longitudinal confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01641)
*Reza Khosravi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper compares two frameworks—Invariant Mass Ansatz (IMA) and Li–Maris–Zhao–Vary (LMZV)—for modeling the leading-twist B-meson light-cone distribution amplitude φ_B⁺(ω) in the heavy-quark limit using light-front holography. It finds that IMA yields a δ-like collapse of φ_B⁺(ω) and incorrect f_B scaling (∝ m_B⁻⁵⁄⁴), while LMZV gives a stable O(Λ_QCD)-width profile and finite inverse moment, yet still fails to reproduce the HQET-predicted f_B ∝ m_B⁻¹⁄² scaling without current matching.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether light-front holographic wave functions for heavy-light mesons can consistently reproduce the correct heavy-quark effective theory (HQET) limits—specifically for the B-meson light-cone distribution amplitude φ_B⁺(ω) and decay constant f_B—by comparing two mass-incorporation prescriptions.

Method: Analytical analysis of the heavy-quark limit (m_B → ∞) of light-front holographic wave functions for B mesons; comparison of two finite-mass prescriptions: (i) Invariant Mass Ansatz (IMA), and (ii) longitudinal confinement via the Li–Maris–Zhao–Vary (LMZV) potential; derivation of asymptotic behavior of φ_B⁺(ω), its inverse moment λ_B, and f_B using the local axial-vector current at zero transverse separation.

Result: IMA leads to φ_B⁺(ω) → δ(ω − m_q) and λ_B,IMA → m_q, but f_B ∝ m_B⁻⁵⁄⁴; LMZV yields a stable O(Λ_QCD)-width φ_B⁺(ω) and λ_B,LMZV^LP = m_q + σ/2, yet f_B ∝ m_B⁻¹ — both deviate from HQET’s f_B ∝ m_B⁻¹⁄². Hence, a well-behaved longitudinal wave function alone is insufficient for correct current matrix element scaling.

Conclusion: A non-singular heavy-quark limit of the longitudinal wave function (as achieved in LMZV) is necessary but not sufficient to recover canonical HQET scaling of local current matrix elements like f_B; systematic current matching beyond wave function structure is required.

Abstract: The heavy-quark limit of light-front holographic wave functions for heavy--light mesons is investigated, with particular emphasis on the leading-twist $B$-meson light-cone distribution amplitude, $\phi_B^+(\omega)$. Two prescriptions for incorporating finite quark masses are compared: the invariant mass ansatz (IMA) and the longitudinal confining framework based on the Li--Maris--Zhao--Vary (LMZV) potential. In the IMA, although the peak of the distribution amplitude remains at a soft spectator momentum, its characteristic width decreases as $\Delta\omega \propto m_B^{-1/2}$ in the heavy-quark limit, causing the longitudinal distribution to collapse asymptotically to $\delta(\omega-m_q)$. Accordingly, the inverse moment approaches the finite limit $\lambda_{B,\mathrm{IMA}} \to m_q = \mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}})$. In contrast, the LMZV framework approaches a finite leading-power (LP) profile with a stable width of order $\mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\rm QCD})$ and a stable inverse moment, $\lambda_{B,\mathrm{LMZV}}^{\mathrm{LP}} = m_q + \sigma/2 = \mathcal{O}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{QCD}})$, which is independent of the heavy-quark mass. The heavy-quark scaling of $f_B$ is also examined using the local axial-vector current at zero transverse separation. While the LMZV longitudinal wave function has a non-singular heavy-quark limit, the bare light-front current does not reproduce the canonical heavy-quark effective theory scaling $f_B \propto m_B^{-1/2}$: the decay constant scales as $f_B \propto m_B^{-1}$ in the LMZV framework and as $f_B \propto m_B^{-5/4}$ in the IMA. Thus, a non-singular heavy-quark limit of the longitudinal wave function is necessary but insufficient to ensure the correct heavy-quark scaling of local current matrix elements without systematic current matching.

</details>


### [104] [Holographic study of the scalar meson $K_0^*(1430)$ with dynamical longitudinal confinement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01662)
*Osra Hemmati,Reza Khosravi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the light scalar meson $K_0^*(1430)$ using light-front holographic QCD enhanced with longitudinal confinement (via the 't Hooft equation and LMZV potential), computing decay constants, parton distributions, distribution amplitudes, and semileptonic $B \to K_0^* \ell^+\ell^-$ transition form factors to predict differential branching ratios and longitudinal lepton polarization asymmetries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical description of the controversial light scalar meson $K_0^*(1430)$ by incorporating both transverse (holographic) and longitudinal confinement in light-front QCD, enabling robust predictions for non-perturbative observables relevant to $B$-meson decays.

Method: Light-front holographic QCD augmented with longitudinal confinement modeled by the analytically solvable LMZV potential and solved via the 't Hooft equation; invariant mass ansatz used to construct the $K_0^*(1430)$ wavefunction; twist-2/twist-3 distribution amplitudes, PDFs, and $\xi$-moments computed; transition form factors for $B \to K_0^* \ell^+\ell^-$ derived from overlap integrals.

Result: Predictions for $K_0^*(1430)$ decay constant, parton distribution function, $\xi$-moments, and twist-2/twist-3 distribution amplitudes; computed $B \to K_0^* \ell^+\ell^-$ transition form factors yield differential branching ratios and longitudinal lepton polarization asymmetries as functions of $q^2$; results benchmarked against other theoretical models.

Conclusion: Combined transverse-longitudinal confinement yields quantitatively improved, self-consistent predictions for $K_0^*(1430)$ structure and its semileptonic $B$-decay observables, providing testable benchmarks for LHCb and Belle II.

Abstract: Within the framework of light-front holographic QCD, where transverse confinement is inherently realized through holography, the structure and mass spectrum of the light scalar meson $K_0^{*}(1430)$ are investigated by employing the invariant mass ansatz and enhancing the model with a longitudinal confinement potential (specifically, the 't Hooft equation and the analytically solvable LMZV potential). The combined transverse and longitudinal confinement dynamics lead to more reliable calculations of key observables, including decay constants, parton distribution function, $\xi$-moments, and twist-$2$ and twist-$3$ distribution amplitudes. Transition form factors for the semileptonic decay $B \rightarrow K_0^{*} \ell^+ \ell^-$ are computed from these amplitudes, yielding predictions for differential branching ratios and longitudinal lepton polarization asymmetries as functions of $q^2$. The results are compared with other theoretical models, offering valuable insights into the non-perturbative dynamics of QCD. The predicted observables provide theoretically motivated benchmarks for future experimental investigations of $B\rightarrow K_0^*(1430)\ell^+\ell^-$ decays at LHCb and Belle II.

</details>


### [105] [Accounting for correlations in the $ωπ$ spectral function and its implication on the leading model dependent contribution to the $τ^- \to π^-π^0 ν_τγ$ decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01683)
*Leonardo Esparza Arellano,Fabiola Elena Fortuna Montecillo,Antonio Rojas Ramos,Genaro Toledo Sánchez*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the ωπ spectral function from τ⁻ → π⁻ων_τ decay using vector meson dominance and correlated parameter sampling via Metropolis Monte Carlo; validates CVC via cross-section and branching-ratio consistency; computes and refines the ω-channel contribution to G_EM(t) in τ⁻ → π⁻π⁰ν_τγ, significantly reducing uncertainties through proper correlation treatment; improves precision of isospin-breaking corrections to (g−2)_μ from tau data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the precision of the ωπ spectral function determination by properly accounting for parameter correlations in the vector meson dominance form factor, test the conserved vector current (CVC) hypothesis, and refine long-distance electromagnetic corrections—particularly for the isospin-breaking contribution to the muon anomalous magnetic moment derived from tau decays.

Method: Vector meson dominance modeling of the ωπ form factor; Metropolis Monte Carlo sampling of parameter space with explicit incorporation of parameter correlations; comparison with SND e⁺e⁻ → ωπ cross-section data and PDG τ⁻ → π⁻ων_τ branching ratio; computation of the ω-channel contribution and its interference with the structure-independent amplitude in τ⁻ → π⁻π⁰ν_τγ; derivation of the electromagnetic correction function G_EM(t).

Result: Significant reduction in uncertainty bands of the ωπ spectral function and G_EM(t) when correlations are included; quantitative confirmation of CVC via agreement between τ spectral function and e⁺e⁻ → ωπ data; consistency of predicted branching ratio with PDG value; refined isospin-symmetry-breaking correction to a_μ from tau data, with improved statistical and systematic precision.

Conclusion: Proper treatment of parameter correlations is essential for reliable extraction of spectral functions and electromagnetic corrections in hadronic tau decays; the analysis strengthens the validity of CVC in the ωπ channel and provides a more precise input for the isospin-breaking correction to the muon g−2 extracted from tau data.

Abstract: The $\omega\pi$ spectral function, measured in the decay $\tau^- \to \pi^-\omega\nu_\tau$ by the CLEO Collaboration, is described in the vector meson dominance approach. We sample the parameter space using a Metropolis Monte-Carlo algorithm and generate a set of parameters of the corresponding form factor, explicitly accounting for the correlation among them. We show the improvement in the uncertainty region of the spectral function with respect to neglecting correlations. We test the conserved vector current hypothesis by showing the agreement of the spectral function with the expected from the measurement of the cross section of $e^+e^- \to \omega\pi$ by the SND experiment, and the consistency of the branching ratio of the $\tau^- \to \pi^-\omega\nu_\tau$ decay with the value reported by the Particle Data Group. As an application, we consider the $\omega$-channel of the $\tau^- \to \pi^-\pi^0\nu_\tau\gamma$ process, the leading model dependent contribution in the vector meson dominance approach. We compute the contribution to the long distance electromagnetic correction function $G_\text{EM}(t)$, of the pure $\omega$-channel and its interference with the structure independent part. We show that the uncertainties, using the set of correlated parameters, are largely reduced with respect to neglecting correlations. The implication on the isospin symmetry breaking correction to the muon magnetic dipole moment estimation from tau data is found to be consistent with previous results but with improved precision.

</details>


### [106] [Gravitational form factors of the baryon octet and flavor dependence in an NJL quark--diquark model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01948)
*Yiwei Qiu,Zhibo Liu,Congcong Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes gravitational form factors (momentum, angular momentum, stress) of the baryon octet within a proper-time-regulated NJL quark–diquark model, revealing distinct flavor dependences and radius hierarchies; it finds that the Σ baryon is more compact than the nucleon in momentum response but more extended in normalized stress response, with robust qualitative behavior under diquark coupling variations and flavor-breaking paths.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how flavor symmetry breaking and diquark correlations (scalar vs. axial-vector) differentially influence gravitational form factors—particularly momentum and stress distributions—in baryons, beyond what chiral effective theories or lattice QCD provide at current resolutions.

Method: Uses a proper-time-regulated Nambu–Jona-Lasinio (NJL) quark–diquark model to compute baryon octet gravitational form factors from the total energy–momentum tensor; incorporates diquark pole masses from hadron-spectrum fits; explores variations in scalar/axial-vector diquark couplings and self-consistent flavor-breaking trajectories; employs metric continuation and external pole inputs to assess hierarchy stability.

Result: Momentum, angular-momentum, and stress form factors exhibit distinct flavor dependences; Σ has smaller momentum radius but larger normalized stress radius than the nucleon, and smallest |D(0)| (forward stress form factor); this pattern persists under independent variation of scalar/axial-vector diquark couplings; flavor-breaking splits initially degenerate stress radii, reversing Σ–nucleon stress-radius ordering while preserving momentum-radius ordering; metric continuation and pole inputs further alter hierarchies.

Conclusion: Gravitational form factors are sensitive probes of internal baryonic structure, where momentum and stress responses decouple under flavor breaking and diquark correlation changes—highlighting the need for multi-channel gravitational measurements to disentangle scalar and axial-vector diquark dynamics in QCD.

Abstract: We calculate the gravitational form factors of the baryon octet in a proper-time-regulated Nambu--Jona-Lasinio quark--diquark model using the total energy--momentum tensor. The momentum, angular-momentum and stress form factors reveal different flavor dependences, and the momentum and stress slope radii follow distinct hierarchies. With the diquark pole inputs taken from the hadron-spectrum calculation, the $\Sigma$ is more compact than the nucleon in its momentum response but more extended in its normalized stress response; it also has the smallest magnitude of the forward stress form factor. This combination of weaker forward strength and larger normalized extent persists under separate variations of the scalar and axial-vector diquark couplings. Along a self-consistent flavor-breaking path the initially degenerate radii split, and the sampled $\Sigma$--nucleon stress-radius ordering changes while the momentum ordering does not. The metric continuation and external pole inputs further change these hierarchies. Together, the octet form factors show how flavor breaking and scalar and axial-vector correlations affect momentum and stress differently.

</details>


### [107] [Boundedness from below in the real Weinberg three-Higgs-doublet model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01997)
*Fayez Abu-Ajamieh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives exact, analytical boundedness-from-below (BFB) conditions for the quartic potential in the real Weinberg three-Higgs-doublet model by reducing the stability problem to 28 stationary field configurations (14 neutral, 14 charged), leveraging Gram matrix kernel structure and phase constraints; it handles singular couplings via algebraic deformation and guarantees rational minima and constructible minimizers for rational couplings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish rigorous, necessary and sufficient boundedness-from-below (BFB) conditions for the quartic scalar potential in the real Weinberg 3HDM — going beyond neutral-field-only analyses by including charge-breaking directions, which are essential for full vacuum stability.

Method: Reduction of the BFB problem to a finite set of 28 stationary points (14 neutral, 14 charged); exploitation of the one-dimensional kernel of the Gram matrix for charged configurations; classification of phase solutions into aligned or triangle-closed configurations; algebraic deformation and limit-taking to handle singular coupling parameters; exact algebraic construction of minimizers for rational couplings.

Result: Complete analytical BFB conditions expressed via checking finitely many stationary configurations, each reducible to linear systems of size ≤4; exact treatment of singular couplings; rationality of normalized minimum and algebraic constructibility of minimizers when quartic couplings are rational; validation via analytical examples, exact checks, and comparison with numerical tools (e.g., StableWein).

Conclusion: The quartic potential in the real Weinberg 3HDM is bounded from below if and only if its value is non-negative on the identified 28 stationary configurations; this provides an exact, computationally tractable, and algebraically complete solution to the BFB problem for arbitrary real quartic couplings—though it does not extend to complex phases or flat-direction scenarios.

Abstract: We study the boundedness-from-below (BFB) conditions of the quartic potential in the real Weinberg three-Higgs-doublet model (3HDM). Unlike an analysis restricted to neutral Higgs fields, a complete stability test must also allow for charge-breaking directions. We show that the problem can be reduced to a finite set of 28 stationary configurations, 14 neutral and 14 charged, each of which can be treated using a linear system of size at most four. The main simplification comes from the one-dimensional kernel of the Gram matrix for charged configurations, while the phase equations reduce to two possibilities: aligned phases or phases forming a closed triangle. We further show how to deal exactly with singular choices of the couplings by introducing an auxiliary deformation and taking an algebraic limit. For rational couplings, the normalized minimum is rational and an attaining field configuration can be constructed algebraically. We discuss analytical examples, exact checks, and comparisons with numerical minimization and StableWein. Our result applies to arbitrary real quartic couplings; it does not cover complex phase coefficients or settle the full-potential stability problem when the quartic is flat.

</details>


### [108] [Electroweak-Scale Cosmic Magnetism: Magnetogenesis, Baryogenesis, Phase Transitions and Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02007)
*Fayez Abu-Ajamieb*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This review explores interconnections among primordial magnetic fields, baryon asymmetry, and stochastic gravitational waves near the electroweak scale, emphasizing coupled dynamics via hypermagnetic helicity, sphalerons, and first-order phase transitions; it synthesizes theoretical frameworks, highlights assumptions in key formulas, and advocates unified, plasma-consistent predictions from microscopic models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and clarify the physical links between primordial magnetic fields, baryon asymmetry, and stochastic gravitational waves—phenomena that are dynamically coupled near the electroweak scale but often treated separately in literature.

Method: Review-based synthesis: analyzes theoretical mechanisms (electroweak anomaly, sphaleron transitions, magnetogenesis in first-order phase transitions), plasma dynamics, gauge-invariant formulations, magnetic backreaction, and field evolution; critically assesses assumptions and distinguishes robust results from model-dependent estimates.

Result: A coherent framework connecting hypermagnetic helicity transfer to chiral fermions, baryon number generation via sphalerons, and co-production of magnetic fields and gravitational waves during electroweak-scale phase transitions; identification of key uncertainties and observational constraints; advocacy for joint, plasma-consistent predictions.

Conclusion: Quantitative predictions for baryon abundance, present-day magnetic-field spectra, and gravitational-wave signals must stem from a single consistent microscopic model incorporating full plasma dynamics—fragmented approaches obscure causal links and hinder testability.

Abstract: Magnetic fields, the baryon asymmetry of the Universe, and stochastic gravitational waves provide complementary probes of physics in the early Universe. Near the electroweak scale, these phenomena are not necessarily independent. The electroweak anomaly allows hypermagnetic helicity to exchange charge with chiral fermions, sphalerons can change baryon and lepton number, and first-order phase transitions in extensions of the Standard Model can drive plasma motion, magnetogenesis, and gravitational waves. In this review, we explain these connections and examine the literature needed to make quantitative predictions. We begin with the properties of primordial magnetic fields and the electroweak plasma, then discuss the mechanisms that produce magnetic fields, the conversion between helicity and baryon number, magnetic backreaction, and the evolution of the field to the present day. We also review the observational constraints and recent developments, including gauge-invariant descriptions of magnetic flux through the crossover and reconnection-controlled field evolution. Particular attention is given to the assumptions behind commonly used formulas and to the distinction between established results and model-dependent estimates. We argue that the most useful way forward is to predict the baryon abundance, present-day magnetic-field spectrum, and gravitational-wave signal from the same microscopic model, with consistent treatment of the intervening plasma dynamics.

</details>


### [109] [Expanding Coulomb field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02094)
*Bogdan Damski*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores a modified Maxwell theory allowing electric charge non-conservation, modeling sudden charge creation via an external current; it derives a light-speed-propagating expanding Coulomb field with shockwave structure, computes its energy dynamics, and uses a quantum-to-classical mapping to solve the field equations—ultimately offering an energy-based argument against charge non-conservation in standard Maxwell theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate theoretical consistency and dynamical consequences of electric charge non-conservation within a modified Maxwell framework, particularly how field configurations evolve causally after abrupt charge creation.

Method: Formulates a Gupta-Bleuler–inspired Lagrangian with external current coupling; solves field equations via quantum-to-classical mapping (e.g., using propagators or Green’s function analogs from QFT); analyzes causal structure, energy distribution, and shockwave propagation of the resulting field.

Result: Derives an explicitly causal, expanding Coulomb solution with a lightlike shockfront; computes finite, well-defined energy localized both in the interior volume and on the front; establishes general solution structure for sudden-onset static currents; finds that total field energy diverges unless charge conservation holds.

Conclusion: While the modified theory admits consistent causal dynamics for non-conserving currents, the divergent energy behavior provides a robust classical (energy-based) argument disfavoring electric charge non-conservation in Maxwell electrodynamics.

Abstract: We briefly discuss vector field theories that allow for electric charge non-conservation and focus on the modified version of the classical Maxwell theory, where the Lagrangian density has the same structure as in the Gupta-Bleuler theory with an external current-vector field coupling. Within this framework, we study the causal dynamics of the field configuration following the sudden creation of an electric charge modeled by an external current. The resulting solution describes an expanding Coulomb field, which is an inherently dynamical object featuring a shockwave-like front propagating at the speed of light (the Coulomb field is confined to the volume swept out by this front). We obtain intriguing results for the energy of the studied field configuration and for the energy carried by the shockwave-like front. General results on the dynamics following the sudden onset of an otherwise static external current are given. The field equations are solved in an unconventional way by means of a quantum-to-classical mapping. An energy-based argument against charge non-conservation in the Maxwell theory is presented.

</details>


### [110] [Extrapolation and explainability in hadron mass predictions with machine learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02185)
*Esra Yuksel,Huseyin Bahtiyar,Derya Soydaner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates four ML models (MLP, XGBoost, LightGBM, GPR) for predicting hadron masses, focusing on extrapolation to exotic and heavy baryons not in training/test sets; MLP achieves best extrapolation RMSE (~294 MeV) and captures mass trends of unobserved doubly heavy baryons, with SHAP revealing heavy quark content as the dominant predictive feature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess and compare the extrapolative capability of ML models for hadron mass prediction—particularly beyond conventional hadrons—to support discovery and theoretical validation of exotic and heavy baryons.
