Method: Trained MLP, XGBoost, LightGBM, and GPR on conventional hadron masses using valence quark content and quantum number features; evaluated on held-out test set and a dedicated extrapolation set of 39 states (including exotic candidates and heavy baryons); used SHAP for feature importance analysis.

Result: MLP achieved lowest extrapolation RMSE (294.2 ± 57.9 MeV) on 30 experimentally measured states—~3× better than other models; reproduced qualitative mass scale of 9 predicted doubly heavy baryons (underestimating bb states); SHAP identified heavy quark content as most influential feature, with context-dependent contributions from other features.

Conclusion: MLP demonstrates superior extrapolative performance for hadron mass prediction, especially for heavy and exotic states; feature-driven interpretability (via SHAP) confirms physical plausibility and highlights quark mass dominance—establishing MLP as a robust tool for guiding experimental searches and theoretical benchmarks.

Abstract: In this work, we study the predictive performance of four machine learning (ML) models for hadron masses, with particular attention to extrapolation beyond the conventional hadrons represented in the training data. Calculations are performed using a multilayer perceptron (MLP), eXtreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost), Light Gradient Boosting Machine (LightGBM), and Gaussian process regression (GPR). ML models are trained on conventional hadrons, using features that describe valence quark content and quantum numbers. Then, we evaluate their performance on test sets and on a separate extrapolation set of 39 states, including exotic candidates and heavy baryons, none of which appear in the training or test sets. We find that the MLP achieves the lowest extrapolation Root Mean Square Error (RMSE) on the $30$ states with measured masses at $294.2 \pm 57.9$~MeV, roughly one-third that of the other models. We also show that the MLP reproduces the overall mass scale of nine unobserved doubly heavy baryons predicted by theoretical calculations, although it underestimates the doubly bottom masses. We use SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) analysis and show that heavy quark content is the most significant factor in the MLP's mass predictions, with the remaining features contributing differently across hadron types. Our results quantify the extrapolation accuracy of ML models for hadron masses and identify the features that drive the MLP predictions.

</details>


### [111] [Higgs boson decays into gauge bosons: theoretical predictions in RNS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02196)
*Edilson A. Reyes R*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes loop-induced Higgs decays (h→Zγ, h→γγ, h→gg) in Radiatively Natural Supersymmetry (RNS), finding that h→Zγ can deviate significantly (up to ~7.5 keV) from SM predictions while h→γγ and h→gg remain SM-like; it assesses current LHC constraints and future sensitivity to distinguish RNS.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe supersymmetric contributions beyond the Standard Model through loop-induced Higgs decays, especially in the Radiatively Natural Supersymmetry framework, which addresses naturalness while accommodating mh ≈ 125 GeV.

Method: Computation of decay amplitudes and partial widths for h→Zγ, h→γγ, and h→gg within RNS, using parameter points consistent with mh ≈ 125 GeV; comparison with ATLAS/CMS data.

Result: Predicted h→Zγ width reaches up to ~7.5 keV—significantly enhanced over SM—whereas h→γγ and h→gg widths stay near SM values across the RNS parameter space; current data marginally constrain but do not exclude RNS; improved precision could distinguish RNS from SM.

Conclusion: h→Zγ is the most sensitive channel for testing RNS at the LHC; upcoming high-luminosity measurements will critically test this natural SUSY scenario.

Abstract: We discuss recent results on the loop-induced decays $h\to Z\gamma$, $h\to\gamma\gamma$, and $h\to gg$ in the Radiatively Natural Supersymmetry (RNS) framework. These processes are sensitive to contributions from particles beyond the Standard Model and provide complementary probes of supersymmetric effects. We evaluate the decay amplitudes and partial widths for parameter points consistent with a Higgs boson mass near the measured value of 125 GeV, and examine their dependence on the model parameters. Across the parameter space, the predicted $h\to Z\gamma$ width can reach about 7.5 keV, while the $h\to\gamma\gamma$ and $h\to gg$ widths remain close to their Standard Model predictions. We compare the $h\to Z\gamma$ predictions with the available ATLAS and CMS measurements and discuss the prospects for distinguishing RNS from the Standard Model with improved experimental precision.

</details>


### [112] [Fisher-Rao Geometry of Gluon Saturation Fronts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02459)
*Edoardo Spezzano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper applies Fisher-Rao information geometry to saturation fronts in the Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation and its Fisher-KPP diffusive limit, revealing a universal t^{-4} (or τ^{-4}) relaxation of transverse deformations; it identifies a geometric mechanism—initial deformation ≈ translation + effective time delay—that explains memory loss and quantifies sensitivity to initial conditions via the transverse metric g^⊥_{θθ}, with implications for geometric scaling and collider phenomenology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear gluon saturation erases memory of initial conditions in high-energy QCD—a key issue for universality and geometric scaling—and to develop an information-geometric framework that quantifies distinguishability of saturation front shapes beyond longitudinal evolution.

Method: Constructs Fisher-Rao geometry using the normalized front density p = ∂N/∂ln r²; isolates the transverse metric g^⊥_{θθ} by factoring out longitudinal flow; performs asymptotic analysis of the Fisher-KPP equation and full numerical BK evolution; identifies deformation-relaxation equivalence (translation + effective time delay); extracts geometric parameters from neighboring runs to predict metric ratios.

Result: Transverse deformations relax as t^{-4} (Fisher-KPP) and τ^{-4} (BK); g^⊥_{θθ} is predicted within 1% using only neighboring-run translation/delay extraction; Fisher-KPP asymptotics yield universal amplitude C_* matching numerics at sub-percent level; BK exhibits 3.5× larger translation information than Fisher-KPP due to Levin-Tuchin saturation region.

Conclusion: Nonlinear saturation induces universal, power-law memory loss governed by information geometry; the transverse metric g^⊥_{θθ} provides a quantitative, model-independent measure of residual sensitivity to initial shape, explaining persistent parameter dependence in HERA fits and motivating EIC sensitivity studies.

Abstract: How nonlinear gluon saturation erases the memory of initial conditions is a central question in high-energy QCD. We develop the Fisher-Rao information geometry of saturation fronts in the Balitsky-Kovchegov (BK) equation and its Fisher-KPP diffusive limit. Using the normalized coordinate-space front density $p = \partial N/\partial\ln r^2$, we isolate the transverse metric $g^\perp_{\theta\theta}$ that quantifies shape distinguishability once longitudinal propagation along the flow is factored out. Nonlinear saturation arrests diffusive broadening and freezes the Fisher speed. Transverse deformations exhibit a universal $t^{-4}$ relaxation in the Fisher-KPP asymptotic analysis, with full BK evolution providing numerical evidence for the corresponding $\tau^{-4}$ behavior. We trace this steep power law to a relaxation mechanism: deforming the initial profile is equivalent, to leading order, to a spatial translation combined with an effective time delay of the universal relaxation flow. Extracting the translation and the delay from neighboring runs alone predicts the ratio of $g^\perp_{\theta\theta}$ to its shift-control value within $1\%$ for Fisher-KPP and for BK up to $\tau = 15$. For Fisher-KPP, the asymptotic front expansion yields the $t^{-4}$ law conditionally on stated analytic hypotheses, with a universal amplitude $C_*$ that agrees with numerical extrapolations to sub-percent accuracy. For BK, the asymptotic translation information exceeds its diffusive approximation by a factor of 3.5, which we attribute to the non-diffusive Levin-Tuchin saturation region. These results provide an information-geometric characterization of the approach to geometric scaling, consistent with the persistent sensitivity of pre-asymptotic HERA fits to initial shape parameters, and motivate systematic studies of parameter sensitivity in EIC kinematics.

</details>


### [113] [Matching $ν$2HDM onto $ν$SMEFT up to one-loop accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02500)
*Wen-Feng Duan,Xuan-Zhen He,Xin-Qiang Li,Ya-Dong Yang,Xing-Bo Yuan*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a complete tree-level and one-loop matching of the neutrinophilic two-Higgs-doublet model (ν2HDM) onto the νSMEFT up to dimension-six operators, integrating out the heavy scalar doublet above the electroweak scale using functional methods and dimensional regularization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically connect the UV-complete ν2HDM to low-energy observables via the SM Effective Field Theory with right-handed neutrinos (νSMEFT), enabling global phenomenological analyses.

Method: Functional matching method combined with naive dimensional regularization and modified minimal subtraction; integration of the heavy scalar doublet; basis conversion from Green’s basis to the minimal Warsaw-extended basis; inclusion of both physical and evanescent operators.

Result: Full analytic expressions for Wilson coefficients of dimension-six νSMEFT operators in terms of pre-SSB ν2HDM parameters, provided as ancillary files on GitHub.

Conclusion: The derived matching provides a rigorous EFT framework for future global fits and precision tests of the ν2HDM using νSMEFT.

Abstract: The neutrinophilic two-Higgs-doublet model ($\nu$2HDM) represents one of the most economical extensions of the Standard Model (SM) while still giving rise to a rich phenomenology. In this work, we carry out a full tree-level and one-loop matching of this ultraviolet completion onto the SM Effective Field Theory extended with right-handed neutrinos ($\nu$SMEFT), including all operators up to mass dimension six. This is achieved by integrating out the heavy scalar doublet at a scale well above the electroweak scale. For the matching procedure, we employ the functional method in combination with the naive dimensional regularization and modified minimal subtraction schemes. After converting from the Green's basis to the minimal, non-redundant Warsaw-extended basis, the effects of high-scale new physics are encoded in the Wilson coefficients of both physical and evanescent operators in the $\nu$SMEFT. These coefficients are given in terms of the $\nu$2HDM parameters prior to spontaneous symmetry breaking. Our final expressions at the high-energy matching scale are provided as ancillary files on \href{this https URL}{GitHub}. This setup forms the foundation for forthcoming systematic and global analyses of the $\nu$2HDM parameter space using effective field theory techniques.

</details>


### [114] [On-shell constituent approach to the light-front partonic energy-momentum tensor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02607)
*Abi Jebarson A,Harleen Dahiya*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a complete matching between gravitational TMDs and canonical vector TMDs up to twist-4 within relativistic light-front constituent models, enabling access to nucleon mechanical properties like energy density, stress anisotropy, and Sivers distortion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the undetermined light-front minus-column of the energy-momentum tensor in momentum space—specifically the unintegrated parton energy k^−—which hinders a full microscopic description of hadronic mechanical structure via gravitational TMDs.

Method: Uses relativistic light-front constituent frameworks where active quarks are treated as effective on-shell quasiparticles; demonstrates algebraic factorization of the light-front energy k^−, enabling closed matching of all 32 gravitational TMDs to canonical vector TMDs up to twist-4, including polarization and T-odd effects.

Result: Derives full mapping of gravitational TMDs to vector TMDs; obtains light-front energy density and its transverse Sivers dipole distortion; proves local equivalence of convective energy and momentum transport velocities; determines longitudinal normal stress; defines a boost-invariant anisotropy ratio quantifying mechanical crossover from collinear confinement to transverse isotropy.

Conclusion: The algebraic factorization of k^− in constituent frameworks completes the gravitational TMD formalism up to twist-4, providing a self-consistent, three-dimensional mechanical picture of the nucleon—despite being model-based, it furnishes essential baseline insights for continuum QCD mechanics.

Abstract: The forward matrix elements of the energy-momentum tensor in momentum space, parameterized by gravitational transverse-momentum distributions (TMDs), provide a microscopic window into hadronic mechanical structure. While the longitudinal and transverse columns match to canonical vector TMDs up to twist-4, the light-front minus column involves the unintegrated parton energy $k^-$, leaving this sector undetermined in general quantum chromodynamics. Working within relativistic light-front constituent frameworks where active quarks are modeled as effective on-shell quasiparticles, we show that this energy factorizes algebraically. This establishes a closed matching relating all 32 gravitational TMDs to canonical vector TMDs up to twist-4, including target polarization and T-odd final-state interactions. This completion provides access to the light-front energy density and its transverse Sivers dipole distortion, demonstrates the local equivalence between convective energy and momentum transport velocities, and determines the longitudinal normal stress. Comparing longitudinal and transverse stresses yields a boost-invariant anisotropy ratio that charts the mechanical crossover from collinear confinement dominance to transverse isotropic balance. While formulated within the constituent approximation, these results offer baseline insights into the three-dimensional continuum mechanics of the nucleon.

</details>


### [115] [Strong vertex $N N ρ$ in dense medium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02770)
*M. Shekari Tousi,K. Azizi*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates in-medium $NN\rho$ strong coupling form factors using QCD sum rules, incorporating density-dependent condensate modifications in the OPE for the first time; results are reported at nuclear saturation density and extended to higher densities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain complementary theoretical insight into the in-medium $NN\rho$ interaction, crucial for understanding dense hadronic matter in phenomenology, experiments, and astrophysical observations.

Method: QCD sum rule framework with in-medium operator product expansion (OPE), including density-dependent modifications of quark and gluon condensates and relevant operators.

Result: Density- and momentum-transfer-dependent strong coupling form factors for the $NN\rho$ vertex; on-shell couplings evaluated at nuclear saturation density and extrapolated to higher densities.

Conclusion: The in-medium $NN\rho$ strong couplings exhibit significant density dependence, offering new constraints for modeling dense nuclear/hadronic matter.

Abstract: We investigate the strong coupling form factors associated with the $NN\rho$ interaction vertex in nuclear matter using the QCD sum rule framework. These form factors are calculated incorporating the in-medium modifications of the relevant condensates and operators into the operator product expansion (OPE) for the first time. We analyze the behavior of the strong form factors as functions of the momentum transfer squared and density of medium and evaluate the corresponding strong couplings at the on-shell point of the $\rho$ meson at nuclear saturation density. Then we extend the analyses to get information at higher densities. Our results provide complementary information on the in-medium $NN\rho$ interaction and may be useful for phenomenological, experimental and observational studies of dense hadronic matter.

</details>


### [116] [Axion Emission Controlled by Ion Motion in Relativistic Beam-Plasma Filamentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02824)
*Zhi-Bo Zhang,Pei-Lun He,Zheng Gong,Yue-Yue Chen*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows that ion dynamics in plasma significantly enhance axion emission from relativistic electron beams via current filamentation instability, enabling a new accelerator-based method to probe axion-electron coupling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore novel environments for producing weakly coupled particles (e.g., axions) using ultra-strong magnetic fields generated by relativistic electron beam–plasma interactions, and to assess the role of ion motion—often neglected—in axion emission.

Method: Particle-in-cell (PIC) simulations incorporating a self-consistent axion-emission model, applied to a 10-GeV electron beam propagating through dense plasma (~10^21 cm⁻³), with controlled suppression of ion motion to isolate its effect on filamentation and axion yield.

Result: Ion motion governs nonlinear filament expansion/coalescence, leading to phase-space recirculation of cold-core electrons into large-amplitude orbits; this repeatedly exposes them to strong magnetic vortex flanks, boosting axion emission by 14.5× compared to ion-suppressed cases.

Conclusion: Ion dynamics are essential for efficient axion generation in relativistic beam–plasma systems; this reveals a viable beam-driven pathway to experimentally constrain the axion-electron coupling at existing or near-future accelerators.

Abstract: The interaction between relativistic electron beams and plasma can generate magnetic fields with strengths of $\sim 10^4$ T far exceeding conventional magnets, which offers a promising environment for producing weakly coupled particles. Using particle-in-cell simulations incorporating an axion-emission model, we show that a 10-GeV electron beam traversing dense plasma emits a forward-collimated axion beam during the ion-mediated nonlinear stage of current filamentation instability. Although the electron-driven linear instability seeds the filaments, ion motion governs their subsequent expansion and coalescence. This evolution reinjects cold-core electrons into large-amplitude orbits in phase space, where they repeatedly cross the strong-field flanks of neighboring magnetic vortices, thereby enhancing axion emission. At a background-plasma density of $\sim 10^{21}\mathrm{cm}^{-3}$, suppressing ion motion reduces the axion yield by a factor of 14.5. Our results demonstrate that ion dynamics play a crucial role in characterizing the emission of weakly coupled particles induced by relativistic filamentation instabilities, suggesting a new beam-driven method for probing the axion-electron interaction at accelerators.

</details>


### [117] [Schwinger Pair Production in Superposed Electric-Field Pulses with Distinct Spatial Widths](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02899)
*Abduweli Abdukerim*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies electron-positron pair production in inhomogeneous electric fields using the DHW formalism in 1+1D, showing that spatial-width disparity between two Gaussian pulses serves as a new control parameter for tuning particle yield and momentum distribution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend beyond standard combined-pulse configurations (with identical spatial envelopes) and explore how independent spatial widths of constituent pulses affect Schwinger pair production dynamics.

Method: Dirac--Heisenberg--Wigner (DHW) formalism in 1+1 dimensions; analysis of a composite electric field composed of two Gaussian pulses with independently tunable spatial widths (λ₁, λ₂) and variable temporal separation.

Result: For temporally overlapping pulses: decreasing λ₂ (at fixed λ₁) enlarges the weak-field region and enhances low-momentum particle yield; oscillatory temporal profiles increase total yield but reduce net momentum transfer. For temporally separated pulses: the spatial width of the delayed pulse governs its ability to accelerate or reshape the momentum distribution of pairs created by the earlier pulse.

Conclusion: Spatial-width disparity between constituent pulses is an effective additional control parameter for engineering Schwinger pair production in multicomponent electric fields.

Abstract: Electron--positron pair production in spatially and temporally inhomogeneous electric fields is investigated in $1+1$ dimensions within the Dirac--Heisenberg--Wigner (DHW) formalism. We consider a composite field formed by two Gaussian pulses with independently adjustable spatial widths, $\lambda_1$ and $\lambda_2$, extending commonly used combined-pulse configurations in which the constituent pulses share the same spatial envelope. Both temporally overlapping and temporally separated configurations are analyzed. For overlapping pulses, decreasing $\lambda_2$ at fixed $\lambda_1$ enlarges the weak-field region and enhances the low-momentum part of the particle spectrum. Oscillatory temporal profiles further increase the particle yield while reducing the net momentum transferred to the particles. For temporally separated pulses, the spatial width of the delayed pulse determines the extent to which it accelerates particles created by the earlier pulse. A narrow delayed pulse affects only a limited fraction of these particles, whereas a broader pulse substantially reshapes the final momentum distribution. These results identify the spatial-width disparity between the constituent pulses as an additional control parameter for Schwinger pair production in multicomponent electric fields.

</details>


### [118] [Role of the GPD-$E$ in exclusive dijet electroproduction at the Electron-Ion Collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02911)
*Trambak Jyoti Chall,Agnieszka Łuszczak,Marta Łuszczak,Wolfgang Schäfer,Antoni Szczurek*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper calculates the contribution of E-type generalized parton distributions (GPDs) to exclusive dijet electroproduction (ep → e′jjp) within collinear QCD factorization, incorporating both helicity-flipping and helicity-conserving amplitudes via the double distribution model; it finds that the helicity-flip cross section—dominated by valence-quark exchange—exhibits distinct |t| and xₚ dependence and becomes comparable to the helicity-conserving contribution at large |t| and xₚ in EIC kinematics, demonstrating sensitivity to E-GPDs at the EIC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe E-type generalized parton distributions (GPDs), which encode transverse quark/gluon structure and spin-orbit correlations in the proton, via exclusive dijet electroproduction—a channel sensitive to proton helicity flip and accessible at modern facilities like the Electron-Ion Collider (EIC).

Method: Collinear QCD factorization framework with double distribution modeling of E-type GPDs; includes full amplitude decomposition (helicity-conserving and helicity-flipping), and accounts for light sea/valence-quark and gluon exchanges; numerical evaluation performed for HERA and EIC kinematics.

Result: Helicity-flipping cross section is dominated by valence-quark exchange and shows qualitatively different |t| and xₚ dependence compared to helicity-conserving contribution; at EIC kinematics, helicity-flip contribution becomes comparable to helicity-conserving one at large |t| and large xₚ; thus, exclusive dijet production provides measurable access to E-GPDs.

Conclusion: Exclusive dijet electroproduction is a viable and sensitive channel for extracting information on E-type GPDs—particularly the proton's transverse spatial and spin-orbit structure—at the Electron-Ion Collider.

Abstract: We calculate the contribution of the $E$-type generalized parton distributions (GPDs) to exclusive dijet electroproduction in $ep\to e'jjp$ in collinear QCD factorization, including both the proton helicity-flipping amplitude and the GPD-$E$ term in the helicity-conserving amplitude, using the double distribution approach for modeling. We include light sea and valence-quark exchanges as well as gluon exchanges and compare the proton helicity-conserving and helicity-flipping contributions for HERA-energy phase space and for Electron-Ion Collider (EIC) kinematics. We find that the helicity-flipping cross section is dominated by valence-quark exchange and has a markedly different dependence on $|t|$ and $x_{\mathbb P}$ from the helicity-conserving contribution. In the EIC kinematics considered here, the helicity-flip contribution becomes comparable to the helicity-conserving one at large $|t|$ and $x_{\mathbb P}$. The results show that exclusive dijet electroproduction can be sensitive to the $E$-type GPDs in the kinematic region accessible at the EIC.

</details>


### [119] [Machine-learning discovery potential for a heavy Higgs boson in $e^+e^-\to ZH$ at a future lepton collider](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02987)
*Majid Hashemi,Faezeh Nasirzadeh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the discovery potential of a CP-even heavy neutral Higgs boson H in the Type-I 2HDM at a 500 GeV e+e− collider via e+e−→ZH with H→WW and Z→ℓℓ/νν, comparing cut-based and multivariate analyses (1D BDT and novel 2D BDT×m_H template), finding significant sensitivity gains—especially in the 4j2ν channel—and deriving constraints on σ×BR and Type-I 2HDM parameter space.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enhance sensitivity to a heavy CP-even Higgs boson H in the Type-I 2HDM at a future 500 GeV e+e− collider, where traditional cut-based analyses suffer from declining efficiency at high m_H due to broadened mass windows and challenging final states (4j2ℓ, 4j2ν).

Method: Performs signal and background simulation for e+e−→ZH (H→WW, Z→ℓℓ/νν) at √s=500 GeV; implements and compares three analysis strategies: (i) cut-based selection, (ii) per-mass-point 1D Boosted Decision Tree (BDT), and (iii) novel two-dimensional BDT×m_H template fitted via profile likelihood; evaluates expected discovery significance and derives 95% CL upper limits on σ×BR.

Result: The 2D BDT×m_H template yields 2.5–3.6× higher discovery significance in the 4j2ℓ channel and 5–18× higher in the 4j2ν channel versus cut-based analysis—gains peak at high m_H where cut-based mass window efficiency drops sharply; exclusion limits on σ×BR are translated into constraints on Type-I 2HDM parameters (tanβ, m_H, cos(β−α)).

Conclusion: The proposed 2D template method significantly outperforms conventional approaches for heavy Higgs searches in semi-leptonic and fully hadronic WW final states at e+e− colliders; it enables robust, model-independent sensitivity across a wide m_H range and provides substantially improved reach for probing the Type-I 2HDM parameter space.

Abstract: We study the discovery potential for a CP-even heavy neutral Higgs boson $H$ in the Type-I two-Higgs-doublet model (2HDM) at a future $e^+e^-$ collider with $\sqrt{s}=500~\mathrm{GeV}$, focusing on the process $e^+e^-\to ZH$ with $H\to WW$, and with $Z\to\ell\ell$ or $Z\to\nu\nu$. The final states are four jets plus two leptons ($4j2\ell$) and four jets plus missing transverse momentum ($4j2\nu$). We compare a cut-based analysis with two multivariate strategies: a one-dimensional Boosted Decision Tree (BDT) trained per mass point, and a two-dimensional (BDT $\times$ $m_H$) template analysed with a profile likelihood. The 2D template improves the expected discovery significance by a factor of $2.5$--$3.6$ in the $4j2\ell$ channel and by a factor of $5$--$18$ in the $4j2\nu$ channel relative to the cut-based analysis, with the largest gains at high $m_H$ where the cut-based mass window loses efficiency. We translate the expected exclusions into $95\%$ CL upper limits on $\sigma \times$ BR and discuss the implications for the Type-I 2HDM parameter space.

</details>


### [120] [BESIII $X(3790)$ enhancement as a reflection of higher charmed mesons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03046)
*Peng-Yu Sun,Tian-Le Gao,Xiang Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: The paper argues that the X(3790) observed by BESIII is likely not a genuine resonance but an artifact ('reflection') from unaccounted charmed mesons—specifically predicted D(3 ³S₁) or D(2 ³D₁) states—in the D⁰D⁻ invariant mass spectrum; this interpretation contradicts both the experimental fit’s incompleteness and prevailing theoretical models (molecular and tetraquark).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reassess the physical nature of the X(3790) state reported by BESIII, motivated by suspected incompleteness in their partial-wave analysis—specifically, omission of theoretically predicted charmed mesons capable of producing kinematic reflections in the D⁰D⁻ spectrum.

Method: Theoretical spectroscopic analysis of charmed mesons using quark-model predictions; identification and evaluation of reflection contributions from higher-mass D-states (e.g., D(3 ³S₁), D(2 ³D₁)) to the D⁰D⁻ invariant mass spectrum; critical comparison with BESIII’s experimental fit and existing theoretical frameworks for X(3790) interpretations (DD̄ molecules, c̄c dū tetraquarks).

Result: A predicted charmed meson—either D(3 ³S₁) or D(2 ³D₁)—can generate a reflection peak near 3790 MeV in the D⁰D⁻ spectrum, plausibly mimicking X(3790); BESIII’s fit omitted these states, rendering their resonance claim unsupported; no compelling theoretical evidence favors X(3790) as either a hadronic molecule or compact tetraquark.

Conclusion: X(3790) is unlikely to be a genuine resonance; it is more plausibly a kinematic reflection artifact arising from unmodeled charmed-meson contributions. Its interpretation as exotic matter (molecule or tetraquark) lacks empirical and theoretical justification.

Abstract: In this work, we show that the $X(3790)$ reported by the BESIII Collaboration is unlikely to be a genuine resonance, a conclusion that follows from the incompleteness of the BESIII experimental analysis. Although BESIII included the established charmed mesons that couple to $D\pi$, as listed by the Particle Data Group, and incorporated their reflections in the $D^0D^-$ invariant mass spectrum, this fit to the experimental data does not exhaust the charmed mesons that can contribute to the $D^0D^-$ invariant mass spectrum through reflections, as can be explicitly seen from a theoretical charmed-meson spectroscopy. We find that a predicted charmed meson, either $D(3\,{}^3S_1)$ or $D(2\,{}^3D_1)$, can play a crucial role in mimicking the $X(3790)$ structure through the corresponding reflection. This warrants caution in interpreting the $X(3790)$ as a genuine resonance. Moreover, such an interpretation is not supported by most theoretical studies of $D\bar{D}$ hadronic molecules and compact $c\bar{c}d\bar{u}$ tetraquarks currently available.

</details>


### [121] [Second-order evolution of spin shear tensor for massive spin-1/2 particles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03086)
*Pushpa Panday,Samapan Bhadury,Sadhana Dash*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives a second-order evolution equation for the spin shear component of the spin tensor in spin-polarized fluids using classical spin description and semi-classical kinetic theory for massive spin-1/2 particles under relaxation time approximation, leading to a relaxation-type equation with new transport coefficients for dissipative spin-shear dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a consistent hydrodynamic framework for spin-polarized fluids by extending relativistic dissipative hydrodynamics to include spin degrees of freedom beyond equilibrium, particularly capturing non-equilibrium spin-shear dynamics.

Method: Classical description of spin degrees of freedom; semi-classical kinetic theory for massive spin-1/2 particles; relaxation time approximation; derivation of second-order evolution equation for the spin shear component of the spin tensor; identification of thermodynamic forces and high-order gradient corrections.

Result: A relaxation-type second-order evolution equation for the spin shear tensor; derivation of a new set of transport coefficients governing dissipative spin-shear dynamics; explicit coupling of spin shear to thermodynamic forces and gradient corrections.

Conclusion: The derived equation provides a foundational step toward a complete spin-hydrodynamic theory, enabling quantitative modeling of spin transport in strongly interacting quantum fluids such as quark-gluon plasma or polarized ultracold atomic gases.

Abstract: We present the second-order evolution equation for the spin shear component of the spin tensor in spin-polarized fluids, using a classical description of the spin degrees of freedom. Employing a semi-classical kinetic theory for the massive spin-1/2 particles within the relaxation time approximation we obtain a relaxation-type equation describing the evolution of spin shear driven by various thermodynamic forces and high-order gradient corrections. This equation yields a new set of transport coefficients that govern the dynamics of the dissipative spin-shear tensor.

</details>


### [122] [Electromagnetic Structure of $Ω_{ccc}^{++}$ and $Ω_{bbb}^{-}$ baryons from a covariant three-body Faddeev equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03149)
*Zhao-Qian Yao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the electromagnetic form factors of triply-heavy Ω_{ccc}^{++} and Ω_{bbb}^{-} baryons using a Poincaré-covariant Faddeev framework in rainbow-ladder truncation, finding harder (more compact) form factors for the bottom baryon, magnetic moments μ_{Ω_{ccc}^{++}} = 1.26 μ_N and μ_{Ω_{bbb}^{-}} = −0.207 μ_N, and convergence toward nonrelativistic heavy-quark limits with increasing quark mass.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically probe the electromagnetic structure of triply-heavy spin-3/2 baryons and assess how relativistic and QCD confinement effects evolve from charm to bottom quark masses, bridging lattice QCD results and heavy-quark symmetry expectations.

Method: Poincaré-covariant three-body Faddeev equation solved within the rainbow–ladder truncation of QCD's Dyson–Schwinger equations; full computation of elastic spin-3/2 multipole form factors G_E0, G_M1, G_E2, G_M3.

Result: G_E0 and charge radius of Ω_{ccc}^{++} agree with lQCD; G_M1(0) yields magnetic moments 1.26 μ_N and −0.207 μ_N; G_E2 shows stronger mass dependence than G_M3; both G_M1(0) and electric/magnetic radius ratio approach nonrelativistic heavy-quark limits for bottom sector.

Conclusion: The electromagnetic structure of Ω_{bbb}^{-} is significantly more compact and less relativistic than that of Ω_{ccc}^{++}, confirming the increasing validity of heavy-quark symmetry and nonrelativistic approximations with larger quark mass.

Abstract: We investigate the electromagnetic structure of the ground-state $\Omega_{ccc}^{++}$ and $\Omega_{bbb}^{-}$ baryons using a Poincaré-covariant three-body Faddeev equation within the rainbow--ladder truncation. We calculate the complete set of elastic spin-$3/2$ multipole form factors, $G_{E0}$, $G_{M1}$, $G_{E2}$, and $G_{M3}$. The leading electric and magnetic form factors become markedly harder from the charm to the bottom sector, revealing the substantially more compact electromagnetic structure of the $\Omega_{bbb}^{-}$. For $\Omega_{ccc}^{++}$, the electric form factor and charge radius at low momentum transfer agree well with the available lQCD results. From the magnetic-dipole form factor at zero momentum transfer, $G_{M1}(0)$, we obtain the magnetic moments $\mu_{\Omega_{ccc}^{++}}=1.26\,\mu_N$ and $\mu_{\Omega_{bbb}^{-}}=-0.207\,\mu_N$. For $G_{E2}$ and $G_{M3}$, the electric-quadrupole structure exhibits a stronger charm-to-bottom evolution than the charge-normalized magnetic-octupole structure. Finally, comparison of the charm and bottom systems shows that both $G_{M1}(0)$ and the relation between the electric and magnetic radii move closer to their leading nonrelativistic heavy-quark expectations as the quark mass increases.

</details>


### [123] [The quantum magic of top quarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03211)
*Chris D. White*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews the quantum information concept of 'magic' (non-stabiliserness) and its recent applications in high-energy physics, particularly collider physics and top quark production.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how quantum informational measures—specifically 'magic', a resource for quantum computational advantage—can provide new insights into high-energy physics phenomena, bridging quantum information theory and particle physics.

Method: Conceptual review and synthesis: traces the origin of magic in quantum computation, defines it as a measure of non-stabiliserness, and surveys its emerging applications in collider physics contexts, including top quark production.

Result: Demonstrates that magic serves as a novel diagnostic tool in high-energy physics, revealing quantum complexity beyond entanglement; preliminary applications suggest it can characterize dynamics and distinguish quantum features in scattering processes like top quark pair production.

Conclusion: Magic is a promising quantum resource-theoretic measure with growing relevance in high-energy physics, offering complementary information to entanglement and enabling new avenues for probing quantumness in collider observables.

Abstract: In recent years, a growing community of researchers has been applying ideas from Quantum Information theory to high energy physics. Entanglement is one such quantity, but there are by now many others. This contribution will examine the property of ``magic" (also known as ``non-stabliserness") which, roughly speaking, measures whether quantum systems have a genuine computational advantage over their classical counterparts. We will review the origin of this concept, before describing recent applications in collider physics, including top quark production.

</details>


### [124] [Probing aQGCs from dimension-eight operators via neutral triboson production at muon colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03218)
*Parimah Hosseini,Sara Khatibi,Hananeh Sadat Modirzadeh*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how well a future high-energy muon collider can constrain neutral anomalous quartic gauge couplings (aQGCs) via neutral triboson production (γγγ, γγZ, γZZ, ZZZ) within the SMEFT framework, using dimension-eight transverse operators; it projects 95% CL limits on Wilson coefficients (especially f_T8, f_T9, and newly f_T3, f_T4) at √s = 3 and 10 TeV, finding triphoton channel most sensitive due to absence of Z branching suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe beyond-Standard-Model electroweak gauge interactions via neutral anomalous quartic gauge couplings (aQGCs), which are highly suppressed in the SM and thus sensitive probes of new physics; muon colliders offer clean, high-energy environment ideal for such precision tests.

Method: Used SMEFT with genuine dimension-eight transverse operators (O_T0–O_T9); performed event simulation and optimized cut-and-count analysis for μ⁺μ⁻ → γγγ, γγZ, γZZ, ZZZ at √s = 3 TeV (1 ab⁻¹) and 10 TeV (10 ab⁻¹); employed χ² statistical framework with 5% systematic uncertainty to derive projected 95% CL limits on Wilson coefficients.

Result: Triphoton (γγγ) channel gives strongest bounds—especially on f_T8 and f_T9; first-ever constraints reported for f_T3 and f_T4 in neutral triboson processes; Z-involved channels (γγZ, γZZ, ZZZ) show weaker sensitivity due to Z→ℓ⁺ℓ⁻ branching ratio suppression.

Conclusion: Multi-TeV muon colliders provide unprecedented sensitivity to neutral aQGCs, particularly through the triphoton channel, enabling stringent tests of the electroweak gauge sector and discovery potential for dimension-eight SMEFT effects.

Abstract: We investigate the sensitivity of a future high-energy muon collider to neutral anomalous quartic gauge couplings (aQGCs) through neutral triboson production processes, specifically $\mu^+\mu^- \to \gamma\gamma\gamma$, $\gamma\gamma Z$, $\gamma ZZ$, and $ZZZ$. Within the Standard Model Effective Field Theory (SMEFT) framework, we focus on genuine dimension-eight transverse operators ($\mathcal{O}_{T_0}$--$\mathcal{O}_{T_9}$). The analysis is performed at prospective center-of-mass energies of $\sqrt{s} = 3\text{ TeV}$ and $10\text{ TeV}$ with integrated luminosities of $1\text{ ab}^{-1}$ and $10\text{ ab}^{-1}$, respectively. Employing an optimized cut-and-count analysis and a $\chi^2$ statistical framework incorporating a $5\%$ systematic uncertainty, we derive projected 95\% confidence level bounds on the corresponding Wilson coefficients. Our results demonstrate that the triphoton channel yields the most stringent constraints, particularly for the $f_{T_8}$ and $f_{T_9}$ coefficients, while channels involving $Z$ bosons exhibit reduced sensitivity owing to leptonic branching ratio suppressions. Furthermore, we report constraints on the $f_{T_3}$ and $f_{T_4}$ coefficients, established here for the first time in this neutral triboson context, highlighting the powerful physics reach of multi-TeV muon colliders in probing electroweak gauge sector interactions.

</details>


### [125] [Towards AI-Agent-Accelerated Research in High-Energy Physics: A Case Study in Monte Carlo Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03373)
*Tianqi Gao,Ruobing Jiang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper evaluates LLM-based agents in high-energy physics (HEP) research using a CEPC Bell-observable analysis ($e^+e^- \to ZH$, $Z \to q\bar q$, $H \to ZZ^* \to 4\mu$) as a case study. It tests two modes: (1) instruction-constrained reproduction—reproducing published $I_3$ central values to ~2% accuracy without accessing target numbers; and (2) open-ended extension—identifying muon isolation as the dominant contributor to hard-to-detector shifts via stage-resolved audit and diagnostics. The agent supports full workflow assistance (planning, coding, MC production, diagnosis, stats, reporting), while humans retain control over state-changing actions and interpretation. Results suggest AI agents accelerate scientific iteration—not just coding—and motivate systematic benchmarking of HEP analyses on correctness, human intervention, and researcher-time efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess the practical utility of contemporary LLM-based agents in multistep, reproducible, human-supervised high-energy physics research—beyond code generation—by evaluating their ability to reproduce and extend a real, published HEP analysis with scientific rigor and traceability.

Method: Two experimental modes are employed: (1) instruction-constrained reproduction—agent reconstructs a published CEPC Bell-observable analysis without access to target numerical results, using fixed analysis definitions and publicly available technical information; (2) open-ended extension—agent explores detector-level effects on the observable using 200,000 fast-simulated events, applying stage-resolved auditing, diagnostics, and iterative hypothesis testing (e.g., isolating muon isolation inefficiencies linked to angular dependence, proximity to $Z \to q\bar q$, and electromagnetic activity from FSR/hadronic decays). Human supervision governs final decisions, interpretation, and state changes; agent handles workflow orchestration, code/config inspection, Monte Carlo production, anomaly diagnosis, control region construction, statistical analysis, and result organization.

Result: In reproduction mode, agent achieves ~2% agreement with four published $I_3$ benchmark points without retuning. In extension mode, it identifies muon isolation as the largest single contributor to the hard-to-detector shift and correctly attributes it to angle-dependent efficiency, spatial correlation with the $q\bar q$ system, and mixed EM activity from muon final-state radiation and hadron decays. Agent fully supports end-to-end analysis workflow under human supervision, with no autonomous state changes or interpretation.

Conclusion: LLM-based agents can meaningfully accelerate scientific iteration in HEP—not merely code writing—when embedded in human-supervised, reproducible workflows. Their value lies in rapid hypothesis exploration, diagnostic agility, and cross-task orchestration, motivating systematic benchmarking across historical HEP analyses using correctness, human intervention cost, and researcher-time efficiency as core metrics.

Abstract: We investigate how far contemporary LLM-based agents can participate in multistep computational high-energy-physics research under reproducible human supervision. Using a previously published CEPC Bell-observable analysis in $e^+e^- \to ZH$, $Z \to q\bar q$, $H \to ZZ^* \to 4\mu$ as a case study, we test two capabilities. First, the agent performs an instruction-constrained reproduction: it is explicitly instructed not to use the published numerical results as analysis targets, although no technical information-access barrier is imposed. The analysis definition is fixed before the formal comparison, and the resulting detector-level $I_3$ central values reproduce the four published benchmark points to within about 2% without retuning to those values. Second, the agent is used for an open-ended extension with no target answer supplied. Using 200,000 fast-simulated events, a stage-resolved audit identifies muon isolation as the largest single contribution to the hard-to-detector shift, and subsequent diagnostics connect the effect to angle-dependent isolation efficiency, proximity to the hard $Z \to q\bar q$ system, and mixed electromagnetic activity from muon final-state radiation and hadron decays. Throughout, the agent assists with workflow planning, code and configuration inspection, Monte Carlo production, anomaly diagnosis, control construction, statistical analysis, and result organization, while state-changing actions and final interpretation remain under human control. The study shows that the value of AI agents in HEP may lie not only in faster code generation, but in faster scientific iteration, and motivates systematic benchmarking across historical HEP analyses using correctness, human intervention, and researcher-time efficiency as explicit metrics.

</details>


### [126] [Polarization in $Λ\to pμ^-\barν_μ$ as a probe of CP-violating new physics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03375)
*Qazi Maaz Us Salam,Ishtiaq Ahmed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies the normal muon polarization in Λ→pμ⁻ν̄_μ decay as a CP-violating probe of new physics, finding that only a specific pseudoscalar coupling with particular phase can produce a signal exceeding the SM electromagnetic final-state interaction; the Λ–Λ̄ polarization difference isolates new physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Normal muon polarization in Λ→pμ⁻ν̄_μ vanishes at tree level in the Standard Model, making it a clean probe of CP-violating new physics beyond the SM.

Method: Uses an effective Lagrangian framework and lattice QCD–based hadronic form factors to compute sensitivity of the normal μ polarization to imaginary parts of new physics couplings, while accounting for SM electromagnetic final-state interactions.

Result: Under current constraints from kaon decays and LHC (one-coupling-at-a-time), only a pseudoscalar coupling with a specific relative phase yields a normal polarization several times larger than the SM electromagnetic final-state interaction contribution; all other couplings yield subdominant effects.

Conclusion: The difference in normal μ polarization between Λ and Λ̄ decays provides a robust, background-suppressed observable to isolate CP-violating new physics, with pseudoscalar interactions being the most promising channel.

Abstract: We investigate the normal $\mu$ polarization in the hyperon decay $\Lambda\to p\mu^-\bar\nu_\mu$ as a probe of new physics that violates CP, since this polarization vanishes at tree level in the Standard Model. Using an effective Lagrangian and lattice QCD form factors, we compute its sensitivity to the imaginary parts of the couplings. With the present bounds from kaon decays and the Large Hadron Collider, one coupling at a time, this sensitivity gives an effect below the Standard Model contribution from the electromagnetic final-state interaction. Only a pseudoscalar coupling with a particular phase can exceed this contribution, by several times. The final-state interaction gives the same polarization in $\Lambda$ and $\bar\Lambda$ decays, so the difference between the two decays isolates the new physics effect.

</details>


### [127] [Non-Global and Super-Leading Logarithms in Higgs Production through Vector Boson Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03407)
*Thomas Becher,Patrick Hager,Matthias Neubert,Nicolas Schalch,Josua Scholze,Dominik Schwienbacher*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs the first combined resummation of non-global and super-leading logarithms in vector-boson-fusion Higgs production and its QCD dijet background, using Marzili (for non-global logs) and Glauber exchange (for super-leading logs), to assess their impact on jet-vetoed cross sections and signal/background suppression.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify correlated soft radiation and Glauber-induced color coherence violations in vector-boson-fusion Higgs production with a rapidity gap veto—a process sensitive to both non-global and super-leading logarithms.

Method: Resums leading non-global logarithms using the leading-color parton shower framework Marzili, and super-leading logarithms via Glauber exchanges at N_c = 3; combines both resummed contributions for phenomenological analysis.

Result: Quantifies the joint effect of non-global and super-leading logarithms on gap survival probabilities and reveals differential suppression between VBF Higgs signal and QCD Higgs+dijet background under jet vetoes.

Conclusion: The combined resummation is essential for precision predictions in jet-vetoed VBF Higgs analyses; non-global and super-leading effects are numerically significant and must be treated jointly to avoid bias in signal extraction and background modeling.

Abstract: We present the first combined phenomenological study of non-global and super-leading logarithms in vector-boson-fusion Higgs production and its QCD-induced Higgs-plus-dijet background. A veto on radiation between the widely separated tagging jets provides a natural setting to investigate correlated soft emission and Glauber-induced violations of color coherence. We resum the leading non-global logarithms with the leading-color parton shower framework Marzili and the super-leading series with Glauber exchanges at $N_c=3$. Combining the two resummed contributions, we study their impact on gap cross sections and on the relative suppression of signal and background.

</details>


### [128] [Higgs pair production and Higgs potential in arbitrary renormalisable extensions of the Higgs sector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03476)
*Henning Bahl,Johannes Braathen,Martin Gabelmann,Kateryna Radchenko Serdula,Georg Weiglein*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'anyHH', an automated tool for computing the di-Higgs production cross-section in gluon fusion within arbitrary renormalisable BSM Higgs sector extensions, enabling precise comparison between HL-LHC data and diverse beyond-Standard-Model theories.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable robust theoretical predictions for Higgs boson pair production — a key probe of the Higgs potential and trilinear self-coupling — across a broad class of BSM models where light new particles may invalidate standard EFT approximations.

Method: Development of 'anyHH', a fully automated computational tool that computes the gg → hh cross-section at leading order (including full top-quark and scalar loop effects) for arbitrary renormalisable extensions of the SM Higgs sector, supporting model-independent parameter scans and UV-complete calculations.

Result: A publicly available, flexible, and validated tool ('anyHH') capable of generating accurate di-Higgs cross-section predictions for generic two- or multi-Higgs-doublet models, singlet extensions, and other renormalisable Higgs-sector BSM scenarios, interfaced with standard Monte Carlo and spectrum generators.

Conclusion: The 'anyHH' tool bridges the gap between high-precision experimental measurements of di-Higgs production at the HL-LHC and theoretically consistent predictions from non-EFT BSM frameworks, significantly enhancing the sensitivity to deviations in the Higgs self-interaction structure.

Abstract: Higgs self-interactions are crucial for probing the up to now largely unknown form of the Higgs potential that is realised in nature. Higgs boson pair production at the (High-Luminosity) LHC is of prime importance in this context as it provides experimental access to the trilinear Higgs self-interaction that enters at leading order in this process. The predictions for the trilinear Higgs self-couplings and the shape of the Higgs potential can vastly differ from the predictions of the Standard Model (SM) as a consequence of physics beyond the SM (BSM). If additional particles are relatively light (but in agreement with all existing experimental and theoretical constraints), their effects may not necessarily be accurately described by established effective field theory methods. Because of the potentially large modifications compared to the SM predictions the di-Higgs process has a high sensitivity for probing BSM physics. In order to enable the comparison between experiment and theory for a wide range of BSM models, automatised predictions for double Higgs production are in order. In this contribution to the LHC Higgs Working Group Report 5 we present \texttt{anyHH}, a novel tool for calculating the Higgs boson pair production cross-section in gluon fusion, which is the dominant production process for two Higgs bosons at the (HL-)LHC, for arbitrary renormalisable extensions of the Higgs sector.

</details>


### [129] [Study on the charmless decay modes of $Z_{cs}(3985)$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03480)
*Yi-Zhuo Zhou,Shi-Dong Liu,Qi Wu,Qing-Ping Ji,Dian-Yong Chen,Gang Li*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies charmless decays of the hidden-charm tetraquark $Z_{cs}(3985)$, modeled as an $S$-wave $D_s^* D^0 / D_s D^{*0}$ molecular state with $I(J^P)=\frac{1}{2}(1^+)$, using triangle-loop diagrams and effective Lagrangians; predicts $\mathcal{B}(Z_{cs} \to \pi K^*) \sim 10^{-2}$ (detectable), highlights form-factor dependence, cutoff-insensitive ratios, and identifies $Z_{cs} \to \eta' K^*$ as a clean probe of mixing-angle-independent dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically predict and characterize the experimentally unobserved charmless decay modes of the newly observed strange hidden-charm tetraquark $Z_{cs}(3985)$, assess their detectability, and evaluate theoretical uncertainties—especially regarding form factor choice, cutoff dependence, and $\eta$-$\eta'$ mixing—thereby guiding future experimental searches at BESIII, LHCb, and Belle II.

Method: Effective Lagrangian approach combined with triangle-loop diagrams to compute $Z_{cs} \to \mathcal{PV}$ ($\mathcal{P}=\pi,K,\eta,\eta'$; $\mathcal{V}=\rho,\omega,K^*$) amplitudes; uses $Z_c(3900) \to \pi\omega$ upper limit to constrain dipole/tripole form factor cutoffs; incorporates $\eta$-$\eta'$ mixing via a single mixing angle $\phi$.

Result: Branching ratio $\mathcal{B}(Z_{cs} \to \pi K^*) \sim 10^{-2}$ (dipole FF), comparable to hidden-charm modes; $\mathcal{B}(Z_{cs} \to \eta K^*)$ strongly depends on $\phi$, while $\mathcal{B}(Z_{cs} \to \eta' K^*)$ is nearly insensitive; relative branching ratios are largely cutoff-independent but sensitive to dipole vs. tripole FF choice; tripole results inconsistent with dipole, indicating significant model dependence.

Conclusion: The charmless $\mathcal{PV}$ decays of $Z_{cs}(3985)$ are potentially observable, especially $\pi K^*$ and $\eta' K^*$; the latter serves as a theoretically clean channel due to insensitivity to $\eta$-$\eta'$ mixing; however, quantitative predictions remain subject to form-factor model dependence, urging caution in extraction of hadronic structure and calling for cross-checks with alternative approaches (e.g., QCD sum rules or lattice QCD).

Abstract: In this work, we investigate the charmless decay modes of the hidden-charm tetraquark state with strangeness, $Z_{cs}(3985)$, assuming it to be an $S$-wave hadronic molecule of $D_{s}^{*-}D^{0}$ and $D_{s}^{-}D^{*0}$ with quantum numbers $I(J^{P}) = \frac{1}{2}(1^{+})$. Using an effective Lagrangian approach, we calculate the decay amplitudes for $Z_{cs} \to \mathcal{PV}$ (where $\mathcal P$ and $\mathcal V$ denote light pseudoscalar and vector mesons, respectively) via triangle loop diagrams. We use the experimental upper limit on $Z_c(3900)\to \pi\omega$ to constrain the cutoff parameter for both the dipole and tripole form factors. Numerical results from the dipole form factor show that the branching ratios for $Z_{cs} \to \pi K^*$ can reach up to the order of $10^{-2}$, which is of the same order of magnitude as the hidden charm decay modes and may be detected in future measurements. The branching ratios from tripole form factor are not consistent with those from the dipole form factor, indicating a certain degree of model dependence in the present work. The relative ratios of the branching ratios for different decay channels are found to be almost independent of the cutoff parameter. However, some ratios are highly dependent on the choice of the dipole or tripole form factor. The branching ratio of $Z_{cs} \to \eta K^{*}$ is found to be highly sensitive to the $\eta$-$\eta^\prime$ mixing angle, while that for $Z_{cs} \to \eta^\prime K^{*}$ is almost independent of the mixing angle, making it a clean channel for future experimental measurements.

</details>


### [130] [Cherenkov radiation constraint on the photon mass from a two-form gauge potential](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03488)
*Patricio Gaete,José Helayël-Neto*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores massive electrodynamics—derived from $B \wedge F$ models and dualized Stueckelberg theories—with a $U(1)$ gauge field coupled to an Abelian 3-form, analyzes how photon mass modifies permittivity, susceptibility, and refractive index, and shows that charged-particle radiation in a medium mimics the Cherenkov effect; applying this to Super-Kamiokande data yields a photon mass bound of 1 eV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain the photon mass using electromagnetic radiation in massive antisymmetric tensor theories, particularly via modifications to dispersion and medium-induced radiation analogous to Cherenkov emission.

Method: Theoretical derivation of massive electrodynamics from dualized Stueckelberg-like theories with $B \wedge F$ coupling; analysis of dispersion relations incorporating mass-dependent permittivity and susceptibility; computation of electromagnetic radiation from a moving charge in a dielectric medium within this massive framework.

Result: Radiation from a charged particle in a medium under massive electrodynamics exhibits Cherenkov-like behavior; application to Super-Kamiokande Cherenkov data yields a photon mass upper bound of 1 eV ($10^{-36}$ kg).

Conclusion: The $B \wedge F$-inspired massive electrodynamics provides a consistent theoretical framework for deriving astrophysically competitive photon mass bounds, corroborating existing limits from superconductivity while extending the methodology to high-energy neutrino detectors.

Abstract: We investigate how electromagnetic radiation in massive antisymmetric tensor theories can be used to constrain the photon mass. In this context, massive electrodynamics constitutes one realization of the $B \wedge F$ class of models in $(3+1)$ dimensions and is obtained by dualizing Stueckelberg-like massive gauge theories. Notably, this model is characterized by the mixing of a $U(1)$ gauge potential with an Abelian $3$-form field of the type found in the topological sector of QCD. We then examine how mass affects permittivity and susceptibility and, consequently, modifies the refractive index through the dispersion relation. We subsequently investigate the electromagnetic radiation emitted by a moving charged particle as it interacts with a medium within the framework of massive electrodynamics. Our findings indicate that the medium through which the particle travels drives the radiation, in a manner analogous to the Cherenkov effect. Applying our results to data on Cherenkov radiation produced at Super-Kamiokande, we derive a photon mass bound of 1 eV ($ 10^ {- 36} $ kg), which is consistent with known estimates based on superconductors.

</details>


<div id='hep-lat'></div>

# hep-lat [[Back]](#toc)

### [131] [Precision determination of $B_c$ and $B_c^*$ meson decay constants from lattice QCD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01638)
*Navdeep Singh Dhindsa,Debsubhra Chakraborty,Nilmani Mathur*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper computes decay constants of heavy-heavy mesons (e.g., $B_c$, $\Upsilon$, $\eta_c$) using lattice QCD with HISQ fermions on MILC gauge ensembles, employing physical-mass charm and bottom valence quarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Precise determination of heavy-heavy meson decay constants is essential for testing QCD predictions, calibrating weak decay analyses (e.g., $B_c \\to J/\\psi \\ell \\nu$), and constraining new physics via leptonic and semileptonic processes.

Method: Lattice QCD simulation using the highly improved staggered quark (HISQ) action for both charm and bottom valence quarks on MILC Collaboration's $N_f=2+1+1$ HISQ gauge ensembles; pseudoscalar, vector, and tensor decay constants extracted from two-point correlation functions with appropriate interpolating operators.

Result: Preliminary values for $f_{B_c}$, $f_{B_c^*}$, $f_{\\eta_c}$, $f_{\\eta_b}$, and $f_{\\Upsilon}$ are reported; tensor decay constants are also computed for the first time in this setup.

Conclusion: The HISQ action enables controlled continuum and chiral extrapolations for heavy-heavy systems; results provide benchmark nonperturbative inputs for phenomenology and future high-precision flavor physics.

Abstract: We present a lattice QCD study of heavy-heavy meson decay constants using the HISQ gauge ensemble generated by the MILC Collaboration. Employing the HISQ action for both charm and bottom valence quarks tuned to their physical masses, we determine pseudoscalar, vector, and tensor decay constants for heavy quarkonium and $B_c$ systems. Preliminary results are presented for the decay constants of the $B_c$, $B_c^*$, $\eta_c$, $\eta_b$, and $\Upsilon$ mesons.

</details>


### [132] [2+1 flavor light hadron spectrum with PACS10 configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02426)
*K.-I. Ishikawa,N. Ishizuka,Y. Kuramashi,Y. Nakamura,Y. Namekawa,K. Satoh,E. Shintani,Y. Taniguchi,N. Ukita,H. Watanabe,T. Yamazaki,T. Yoshié*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper presents continuum-limit results for the hadron spectrum, pseudoscalar meson decay constants, and quark mass ratio using PACS10 2+1-flavor QCD lattice configurations with O(a)-improved Wilson quarks and Iwasaki gauge action; it assesses finite-size effects via a smaller (5.4 fm)^4 lattice, employs mass reweighting to determine chiral expansion parameters, fine-tunes to the physical point using π, K, and Ξ masses under two isosymmetric QCD conventions, and compares final continuum results—including baryon masses and f_π, f_K—with pseudoexperimental values and FLAG.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain precise continuum-limit QCD predictions for key low-energy observables (hadron spectrum, decay constants, quark mass ratio) at the physical point, while systematically controlling discretization errors, finite-volume effects, and isospin-breaking conventions in 2+1-flavor lattice simulations.

Method: Uses PACS10 gauge ensembles at three lattice spacings (a ≈ 0.085–0.044 fm), all with physical-volume ≥ (10 fm)^4; includes an auxiliary (5.4 fm)^4 lattice at the coarsest spacing to quantify finite-size effects; computes hadron masses and decay constants; applies mass reweighting to extract chiral expansion coefficients near the physical point; performs physical-point tuning using π, K, and Ξ masses under two isosymmetric QCD conventions; extrapolates to the continuum limit using combined chiral–continuum fits.

Result: Finite-size effects are negligible for hadron effective masses but significant (>1σ) for pseudoscalar decay constants (f_π, f_K); chiral expansion parameters extracted from the small-volume ensemble enable robust physical-point tuning; continuum-limit results for octet baryon masses and f_π/f_K agree with pseudoexperimental values within uncertainties in both conventions; quark mass ratio m_u/m_d and m_s/m_l are consistent with the latest FLAG averages.

Conclusion: The study confirms the reliability of the PACS10 ensembles for precision QCD phenomenology at the physical point; highlights the sensitivity of decay constants—not masses—to finite volume; demonstrates that convention-dependent isospin treatment induces measurable but controlled differences in extracted quantities; provides high-precision continuum results compatible with current global averages.

Abstract: We present the results for the hadron spectrum, the pseudoscalar meson decay constants, and the quark mass ratio in the continuum limit using the ``PACS10'' gauge configurations generated with the $O(a)$-improved Wilson quark action and the Iwasaki gauge action. Numerical simulations are carried out at three lattice spacings keeping the space-time volumes over (10 fm$)^4$ in 2+1 flavor QCD at the physical point. We calculate physical quantities with two choices of conventions to define pseudoexperimental values expected in the isosymmetric QCD. In order to investigate the finite size effects on the physical quantities we have additionally generated the (5.4 fm$)^4$ lattice at the coarsest lattice spacing of $a=0.08487(14)$~fm, which is a hypercube with half the length in each direction compared to the PACS10 configurations. While the finite size effect is hardly observed in comparison of the hadron effective masses on the (10.9 fm$)^4$ and (5.4 fm$)^4$ lattices, the results for the pseudoscalar meson decay constants show discrepancies beyond the error bars. The (5.4 fm$)^4$ lattice also serves for determining the expansion parameters of the pseudoscalar meson masses and decay constants, and the octet baryon masses in terms of the quark masses around the physical point with the aid of the mass reweighting technique. The expansion parameters are used for the fine-tuning of the physical point employing the pseudoexperimental masses of the $\pi$ meson, $K$ meson, and the $\Xi$ baryon as physical inputs in each convention. We compare the results for the octet baryon masses and the pseudoscalar decay constants in the continuum limit with the corresponding pseudoexperimental values in both conventions. We also discuss the deviation between two results with different conventions. As for the quark mass ratio the result is compared with the recent FLAG summary.

</details>


### [133] [Generalizable Equivariant Diffusion Models for Non-Abelian Lattice Gauge Theory: Analytical Foundations and Numerical Scaling Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03530)
*Gert Aarts,Diaa E. Habibi,Andreas Ipp,David I. Müller,Thomas R. Ranner,Lingxiao Wang,Wei Wang,Qianteng Zhu*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper provides a rigorous analytical foundation for gauge-equivariant diffusion models applied to non-abelian lattice gauge theories, deriving the reverse-time SDE on group manifolds, proving gauge invariance of the denoising loss, and benchmarking scaling behavior with lattice volume and coupling β; it also gives exact analytical expressions for Wilson loops and topological charge susceptibility during the forward diffusion process.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a sound theoretical framework for gauge-equivariant diffusion models in lattice gauge theory—beyond prior empirical demonstrations—by addressing foundational aspects such as reverse-time dynamics on group manifolds, gauge symmetry preservation in training objectives, and quantitative scalability analysis.

Method: Rigorous derivation of the reverse-time stochastic differential equation (SDE) on compact Lie group manifolds (e.g., SU(N)); formal proof of gauge invariance of the denoising score-matching loss; numerical benchmarking of sampling efficiency versus lattice volume V and inverse coupling β; analytical computation of observables—including arbitrary Wilson loops and topological charge susceptibility—along the forward diffusion trajectory.

Result: A mathematically consistent formulation of diffusion on gauge field configuration spaces; proof that the denoising loss is gauge-invariant under local transformations; numerical evidence of favorable (sublinear) scaling of computational cost with lattice volume; closed-form analytical expressions for Wilson loop expectation values and topological charge susceptibility at all noise levels during forward diffusion.

Conclusion: Gauge-equivariant diffusion models admit a rigorous stochastic calculus foundation on group manifolds, preserve physical symmetries intrinsically, scale favorably with system size, and yield exact analytical predictions for key observables—thereby validating their use as principled generative tools for non-abelian lattice gauge theories.

Abstract: Gauge-equivariant diffusion models can be used to generate ensembles of field configurations in non-abelian lattice gauge theories. Previously, we demonstrated the feasibility of modeling these theories by combining L-CNN-based diffusion models with the Metropolis-adjusted annealed Langevin algorithm (MAALA). Here we focus on the underlying analytical framework. In particular, we present a rigorous derivation of the reverse-time stochastic differential equation on the group manifold, discuss the gauge invariance of the denoising loss function, and provide extensive numerical benchmarks regarding the scaling behavior with respect to the lattice volume and the inverse coupling $\beta$. Full analytical expressions for arbitrarily sized Wilson loops and the topological charge susceptibility during the forward process are given as well.

</details>


<div id='math-ph'></div>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [134] [Large $N$ limit of the ratio of Lukyanov integrals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00662)
*Dong Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes large-N asymptotics of Lukyanov integral ratios for real coupling b ≥ 1 using biorthogonal ensembles and vector Riemann–Hilbert methods; it derives logarithmic ratios via a Fredholm determinant, with separate even/odd N expansions, verifies hypotheses against thermodynamic Bethe ansatz, and discusses connections to QFT one-point functions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the large-N asymptotic behavior of Lukyanov integrals—central in integrable quantum field theories—and connect them rigorously to thermodynamic Bethe ansatz and quantum-field-theoretic observables.

Method: Exploits the biorthogonal ensemble representation of Lukyanov integrals and applies vector Riemann–Hilbert problem techniques; derives asymptotics via steepest descent analysis, isolates the constant term of the logarithmic ratio, and expresses it as a Fredholm determinant; distinguishes cases by parity of particle number N.

Result: A locally uniform (in real α) large-N asymptotic expansion of the logarithmic ratio of Lukyanov integrals, formulated separately for even and odd N, with the constant term given explicitly by a Fredholm determinant; verification that the physical ground-state TBA correction satisfies the required analytic hypotheses.

Conclusion: The asymptotic structure of Lukyanov integral ratios is fully characterized at large N under mild analytic assumptions; the Fredholm determinant formulation provides a rigorous bridge between integrable lattice models, thermodynamic Bethe ansatz, and quantum-field-theoretic one-point functions.

Abstract: We study the large-$N$ asymptotics of ratios of Lukyanov integrals for fixed real coupling $b\geq1$. Our approach uses their biorthogonal ensemble structure and vector Riemann--Hilbert techniques. Under stated analytic hypotheses, we derive the logarithmic ratio through its constant term, which is expressed by a Fredholm determinant. The expansion is locally uniform in the real parameter $\alpha$ and is formulated separately for even and odd particle numbers. We verify the hypotheses for the correction defined by the physical ground-state thermodynamic Bethe ansatz. The relation to quantum-field-theoretic one-point functions is discussed separately.

</details>


### [135] [On Emergent Geometry of the Generalized BGW Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01507)
*Dingkang Xiong,Jian Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes that the generalized Brézin-Gross-Witten (BGW) theory's multidifferentials satisfy the Eynard-Orantin topological recursion in a generic background, and clarifies its relation to the Witten-Kontsevich model, yielding a new proof of the Okuyama-Sakai conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand the mathematical structure of the generalized BGW theory and its relationship with other fundamental models in topological field theory and enumerative geometry, particularly the Witten-Kontsevich model.

Method: Application of statistical physics methods and rigorous analysis of topological recursion; derivation and comparison of multidifferentials; characterization of model transitions via coupling constant dependence.

Result: Proof that generalized BGW multidifferentials obey Eynard-Orantin topological recursion in generic background; explicit characterization of the transition from Witten-Kontsevich to generalized BGW; new proof of the Okuyama-Sakai conjecture.

Conclusion: The generalized BGW theory is fully governed by topological recursion, and shares deep structural unity with the Witten-Kontsevich model—revealing a broader integrable framework unifying these theories.

Abstract: We present a systematic study of the mathematical theory of the generalized Brézin-Gross-Witten (BGW) theory using methods from statistical physics. We prove the multidifferentials of the generalized BGW theory are given by the Eynard-Orantin topological recursion in a generic background with general coupling constants. Furthermore, we characterize the transition between the Witten-Kontsevich model and the generalized BGW theory. This approach yields a new proof of the Okuyama-Sakai conjecture, further elucidating the deep structural connections between these two fundamental models.

</details>


### [136] [Projective relativistic dynamics from a quantum Lorentz algebra: time-dependent deformed Hamiltonians on (anti-)de Sitter spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02242)
*Rutwig Campoamor-Stursberg,Oscar Carballal,Francisco J. Herranz*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs time-dependent quantum-deformed Hamiltonian systems on (1+1)-dimensional (anti-)de Sitter spacetimes using a non-standard quantum deformation of the Lorentz algebra 𝔰𝔬(1,3); the deformation parameter z is identified with the curvature κ, linking it to the cosmological constant Λ = −z, and yields deformed oscillators, Bateman, and Caldirola–Kanai systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Lie–Hamilton system theory via Poisson–Hopf deformations to curved spacetimes, enabling systematic construction of integrable, time-dependent quantum-deformed models with geometric interpretation of the deformation parameter.

Method: Applies Poisson–Hopf deformation theory to a non-standard quantum deformation of 𝔰𝔬(1,3), identifies projective canonical variables related to Beltrami coordinates via canonical transformation, and derives deformed Hamiltonian vector fields preserving the deformed Lorentz symmetry.

Result: A family of time-dependent deformed Hamiltonians on dS₂, AdS₂, and Minkowski spacetimes; identification z ≡ κ ≡ −Λ; explicit constructions of deformed harmonic oscillators, Bateman, and generalized coupled Caldirola–Kanai systems.

Conclusion: Quantum deformations of the Lorentz algebra provide a unifying algebraic framework for generating physically meaningful, curvature-dependent, time-dependent Hamiltonian systems—bridging quantum algebra, Poisson geometry, and relativistic mechanics in 1+1 dimensions.

Abstract: A (non-standard) quantum deformation of the Lorentz algebra $\mathfrak{so}(1,3)$ is used to construct novel time-dependent Hamiltonian systems on the (anti-)de Sitter spacetimes in $(1+1)$-dimensions. The specific deformation considered here contains, among others, the non-standard quantum deformation of $\mathfrak{sl}(2, \mathbb{R})$ and that of the `book' algebra $\mathfrak{b}_{2}$ as quantum subalgebras. The guiding idea is to apply the theory of Poisson--Hopf deformations of Lie--Hamilton systems to the Lorentz deformation, giving rise to a broad family of time-dependent deformed systems that share the same deformed Poisson--Hopf Lorentz algebra symmetry. In particular, this approach provides Hamiltonian vector fields expressed in terms of certain projective canonical variables, which, in turn, are related to the usual Beltrami variables via a canonical transformation. Under this construction, it is shown that the underlying Lorentizan spaces possess constant curvature $\kappa$ and the quantum deformation parameter $z$ can be identified with $\kappa$, such that the cosmological constant is $\Lambda=-z\equiv -\kappa$. Therefore, we deal with the anti-de Sitter, de Sitter and Minkowskian spaces for $z>0$, $z<0$ and $z=0$ (the undeformed case), respectively. Finally, as applications, we study time-dependent deformed oscillators on the (anti-)de Sitter spaces in detail, as well as time-dependent deformed Bateman and generalised coupled Caldirola--Kanai Hamiltonians.

</details>


### [137] [Phase-space dynamics and multicomponent wave-packet structures in the Dunkl anharmonic oscillator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00280)
*D. Ojeda-Guillén,R. D. Mota*

Main category: math-ph
