TL;DR: This paper analyzes phase-space dynamics and wave-packet structures of the Dunkl anharmonic oscillator (modeling a Kerr medium), deriving analytical expressions for Dunkl-Husimi Q-function and angular Husimi profile; it shows how the Dunkl parameter μ controls coherence, parity-dependent phases, angular anisotropy, and multimodal reconstruction times, recovering standard Kerr dynamics as μ→0.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how Dunkl deformation modifies quantum phase-space dynamics and wave-packet revival behavior in an anharmonic oscillator analog of the optical Kerr medium.

Method: Analytical derivation of time-evolved state, Dunkl-Husimi Q-function, and normalized angular Husimi profile; analysis of coherent-state weight modulation, parity-dependent phase accumulation, and Husimi pattern evolution under varying Dunkl parameter μ.

Result: Dunkl parameter μ induces angular anisotropy and asymmetric phase-space separation; μ=0.5 yields asymmetric multicomponent Husimi patterns with unequal peak heights and early reconstruction at t=π/λ; μ=1.0 preserves multimodality with broadening and unitary evolution; full reconstruction occurs at fundamental nonlinear revival time for selected parameter ratios; standard Kerr limit recovered as μ→0.

Conclusion: The Dunkl deformation enriches the phase-space structure and temporal dynamics of the anharmonic oscillator, enabling tunable asymmetry, anisotropy, and revival characteristics not present in the standard Kerr model.

Abstract: We study the phase-space dynamics and multicomponent wave-packet structures of the Dunkl anharmonic oscillator (Kerr medium). Starting from the time-evolved state, we derive analytical expressions for the Dunkl-Husimi $Q$-function and a normalized angular Husimi profile. The Dunkl parameter modifies the coherent-state excitation weights and the parity-dependent phase accumulation, producing angular anisotropy and asymmetric phase-space separation. For the half-integer deformation $\mu=0.5$, the dynamics generate asymmetric multicomponent Husimi patterns with unequal peak heights and an early reconstruction at $t=\pi/\lambda$. For the deformation $\mu=1.0$, the Husimi distribution exhibits broadening while retaining its multimodal structure and unitary evolution. At the fundamental nonlinear revival time, the wave packet is reconstructed for the selected parameter ratio. The standard Kerr dynamics are recovered in the limit $\mu\rightarrow 0$.

</details>


### [138] [Painlevé VI superintegrability and Heun-Racah algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00322)
*Ian Marquette,Sarah Post*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes two quantum superintegrable systems in the Euclidean plane separable in polar coordinates, with angular potentials governed by the full four-parameter Painlevé VI transcendent; one features an oscillator radial term, the other an extended-Coulomb term. It constructs their fourth-order integrals, cubic symmetry algebras (specializations of the Heun–Racah algebra), deformed-oscillator realizations, and relates them via Levi-Civita transformation and coupling-constant metamorphosis; classical Poisson analogs are also compared.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically extend quantum superintegrability beyond polynomial potentials to transcendental ones—specifically, to construct and algebraically characterize quantum systems whose angular dependence is governed by the full four-parameter Painlevé VI equation, generalizing earlier exceptional-Jacobi models.

Method: Explicit construction of fourth-order quantum integrals; derivation of cubic symmetry algebras and their Casimirs; deformed-oscillator algebraic realization; operator-level Levi-Civita transformation and coupling-constant metamorphosis to relate oscillator and Coulomb systems; comparison of quantum symmetry algebras with classical Poisson counterparts.

Result: Two quantum superintegrable systems are fully characterized: their fourth-order integrals, cubic symmetry algebras (both specializations of the Heun–Racah algebra at fixed energy), Casimirs, and deformed-oscillator realizations are derived; the angular structure function is identical (quartic, Painlevé VI–dependent), while radial factors distinguish oscillator/Coulomb dynamics; a direct parameter correspondence between the two systems is established; classical Poisson algebras are shown to parallel the quantum structures.

Conclusion: The full four-parameter Painlevé VI transcendent naturally governs the angular sector of quantum superintegrable systems in the Euclidean plane, yielding rich cubic symmetry algebras unifying oscillator and Coulomb dynamics through canonical transformations; this establishes Painlevé VI as a fundamental object in quantum superintegrability beyond special-function potentials.

Abstract: We study the two fourth-order quantum superintegrable systems in the Euclidean plane that separate in polar coordinates and whose angular potentials are determined by the general four-parameter sixth Painlevé transcendent. One system has an oscillator radial term and the other an extended-Coulomb radial term. For both systems we give the fourth-order quantum integral explicitly, derive the cubic symmetry algebra and its Casimir, and construct a deformed-oscillator realization. At fixed energy the two algebras are specializations of the Heun--Racah algebra. Their structure functions contain the same quartic angular factor, determined by the Painlevé~VI parameters, while different radial factors distinguish oscillator and Coulomb dynamics. The Coulomb integral is obtained by transforming the Cartesian Hermitian symmetry at the operator level. The lower-order terms generated by the polar coordinate change are essential for commutation with the Hamiltonian. The two quantum systems are also related by the Levi-Civita transformation and coupling-constant metamorphosis, which gives a direct correspondence between their Painlevé parameters, algebras, Casimirs and representations. Earlier exceptional-Jacobi models arise at special rational parameter values, whereas the results here apply to the full four-parameter Painlevé~VI family. We also compare the quantum results with the corresponding classical Poisson algebras.

</details>


### [139] [Stability of Solitons in the Parametrically Driven and Damped Nonlinear Schrödinger Equation in Arbitrary Dimension](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00346)
*Fernando Carreño-Navas*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes spectral stability of soliton solutions to the parametrically driven and damped nonlinear Schrödinger equation in N dimensions with power-law nonlinearity; it identifies two stationary solution families, proves one is always unstable, performs a complete angular-momentum-resolved spectral analysis for the other, derives an analytical instability criterion, reveals new phenomena for extreme values of the nonlinearity exponent κ, and provides numerical stability diagrams for dimensions 1–3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the spectral stability of solitons in the parametrically driven and damped nonlinear Schrödinger (PDDNLS) equation — a model relevant to driven-dissipative nonlinear wave systems — especially beyond the previously studied one-dimensional case and for general power-law nonlinearity and spatial dimension.

Method: Linearization around two identified stationary soliton families Ψ±; reduction to a dissipationless eigenvalue problem via ε-parametrization; spectral decomposition into angular momentum sectors Sl; application of Vakhitov–Kolokolov-type argument combined with local eigenvalue splitting analysis near zero; analytical derivation of instability criteria per sector; numerical computation of stability diagrams for N = 1, 2, 3.

Result: Ψ− is proven universally unstable; for Ψ+, instabilities are confined to l = 0 and l = 1 sectors; no complex quadruplets arise on S1; analytical instability thresholds derived for each Sl; novel instability behaviors uncovered for small and large κ, unobserved in prior 1D studies; full stability diagrams provided for N = 1, 2, 3.

Conclusion: The spectral stability of PDDNLS solitons is fully characterized: Ψ− is structurally unstable, while Ψ+ exhibits dimension- and nonlinearity-dependent stability governed by low-angular-momentum modes, with new regimes emerging for extreme κ — advancing both theoretical understanding and applicability to higher-dimensional driven-dissipative systems.

Abstract: We study the spectral stability of soliton solutions of the parametrically driven and damped nonlinear Schrödinger (PDDNLS) equation with power-law nonlinearity $|\Psi|^{2\kappa}\Psi$ in $N$ dimensions. We identify two families of stationary solutions, $\Psi_\pm$, whose existence is determined by two conditions: one involving the nonlinearity exponent~$\kappa$ and the spatial dimension~$N$, and another involving the forcing amplitude~$r$ and the damping coefficient~$\rho$. By linearising around these solutions and introducing a convenient parametrisation through~$\varepsilon$, we reduce the stability problem to a dissipationless eigenvalue problem for two Schrödinger-type operators, $L_0$ and $L_1$. We prove that $\Psi_-$ is always unstable, while for $\Psi_+$, we carry out a complete spectral analysis by decomposing the problem into angular momentum sectors $\mathcal{S}_l$. We show that modes with $l \geq 2$ generate no instabilities and that no complex quadruplets arise on~$\mathcal{S}_1$. We also derive an analytical instability criterion on $\mathcal{S}_l$ by combining a Vakhitov--Kolokolov-type argument with a local analysis that determines the direction of the eigenvalue splitting near the origin. In addition, we uncover new phenomena at both small and large values of $\kappa$ that were not investigated in earlier one-dimensional studies. Numerical stability diagrams for $N = 1, 2, 3$ are presented.

</details>


### [140] [A block-encoding approach to the Segawa-Suzuki spectral mapping theorem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00498)
*Yohei Tanaka*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper reformulates the Segawa--Suzuki spectral mapping theorem for Szegedy-type quantum walks using block-encoding and Schur decomposition, simplifying the original proof and extending applicability to spectrum, point spectrum, and Fredholm essential spectrum, with explicit characterization of endpoint eigenspaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simplify and generalize the Segawa--Suzuki spectral mapping theorem by leveraging block-encoding and chiral symmetry structure, avoiding technical invariant-subspace and approximate-eigenvector arguments.

Method: Uses a two-by-two block representation induced by the chiral symmetry involution; applies Schur decomposition to derive spectral mapping; extends analysis to spectrum, point spectrum, and Fredholm essential spectrum; provides block-theoretic characterization of endpoint (inherited and birth) eigenspaces.

Result: A simplified, unified proof of the non-endpoint spectral mapping theorem; extension to full spectral types (including essential spectrum); explicit block-structured description of endpoint eigenspaces; illustration via one-dimensional split-step quantum walk.

Conclusion: The block-encoding framework offers a more transparent, algebraically natural, and broadly applicable derivation of spectral mapping results for abstract Szegedy-type quantum walks, enhancing both conceptual clarity and technical utility.

Abstract: We revisit the Segawa--Suzuki spectral mapping theorem for abstract Szegedy-type quantum walks from a block-encoding viewpoint. Retaining both eigenspaces of the involution underlying the chiral symmetry yields a natural two-by-two block representation in which the usual discriminant operator and its counterpart form the diagonal blocks. The non-endpoint spectral mapping theorem then follows from an elementary Schur decomposition, substantially simplifying the original proof by avoiding its invariant-subspace decomposition and approximate-eigenvector arguments. The same framework establishes the result for the spectrum, point spectrum, and Fredholm essential spectrum and gives a direct block-theoretic description of the endpoint inherited and birth eigenspaces. We illustrate the construction with a one-dimensional split-step quantum walk.

</details>


### [141] [Boosted Physics-Informed Neural Networks for Solving Homogeneous and Non-Homogeneous Lane-Emden Type Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00701)
*Luke Oluwaseye Joel*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a hybrid method combining Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) with ensemble boosting regressors (LightGBM, RF, XGBoost, SVR) to correct PINN residual errors and improve accuracy and robustness in solving Lane-Emden equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard PINNs suffer from optimization difficulties and approximation errors when solving stiff or nonlinear differential equations like the Lane-Emden equation; there is a need for improved accuracy and generalization without sacrificing physical consistency.

Method: A two-stage framework: (1) train a standard PINN enforcing the Lane-Emden equation and boundary conditions via physics-informed loss; (2) compute residuals of the PINN solution, then train multiple regression models (LightGBM, Random Forest, XGBoost, SVR) on these residuals as targets; finally, aggregate their outputs to correct the PINN prediction.

Result: The boosted PINN achieves significantly lower prediction error (e.g., reduced L2 relative error) and better generalization across unseen domains compared to vanilla PINN, as validated quantitatively and via comparative analysis.

Conclusion: Integrating data-driven boosting into PINNs provides a flexible, high-fidelity, and physically consistent solver for nonlinear ODEs—particularly beneficial for astrophysical modeling where analytical solutions are scarce.

Abstract: The Lane-Emden Equation is a class of nonlinear differential equations commonly used to model phenomena in astrophysics and mathematical physics, such as stellar structure and thermal behavior in polytropic gas spheres. This study presents a novel hybrid framework that integrates Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) with boosting methods to enhance the accuracy and robustness of solutions for Lane-Emden type differential equations. Standard PINNs leverage physical laws encoded within the loss function, but their predictive performance can be constrained by optimization challenges and approximation errors. To address this, we introduce a post-processing boosting stage where the residual errors from the PINN prediction are modeled using some regression algorithms - including LightGBM, Random Forest, XGBoost, and Support Vector Regression (SVR). The outputs from these boosted models are aggregated to generate a corrected, high-fidelity prediction. The proposed methodology is systematically validated through quantitative metrics and comparative analysis against the standard PINN baseline. The results demonstrate that the boosted PINN framework significantly reduces prediction error, and improves generalization, offering a powerful and flexible approach for solving scientific and engineering problems governed by differential equations.

</details>


### [142] [A Representation Theorem for Fractional Viscoelastic Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00750)
*Andrea Giusti,Ivano Colombaro,Andrea Mentrelli,Francesco Mainardi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a representation theorem for finite series-parallel viscoelastic networks with springs and spring-pots (fractional elements) of order α ∈ (0,1), showing their complex shear modulus admits rational forms in an auxiliary variable, leading to non-negative fractional Prony series for relaxation and creep functions — thereby rigorously confirming a corrected Mainardi-Spada conjecture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous mathematical foundation for the Mainardi-Spada conjecture regarding fractional viscoelastic models, specifically for series-parallel networks containing spring-pots, which are widely used in modeling soft matter and biological tissues but lacked formal justification for their Prony-like representations.

Method: Analytical Laplace-domain analysis: derivation of the complex shear modulus for finite series-parallel networks of springs and spring-pots; transformation into an auxiliary variable to obtain rationality; partial-fraction decomposition with sign-controlled residues; inversion via Laplace transform to obtain time-domain fractional Prony forms.

Result: The complex shear modulus is rational in an auxiliary variable, with simple negative-real-axis poles and negative residues; its reciprocal has positive residues and possibly a pole at zero; both inverses yield finite fractional Prony series for relaxation modulus and creep compliance, with provably non-negative amplitudes.

Conclusion: A corrected version of the Mainardi-Spada conjecture is rigorously proven for finite series-parallel spring–spring-pot networks; the resulting fractional Prony representations are physically admissible (non-negative coefficients), enabling robust model identification and interpretation.

Abstract: We prove a representation theorem for finite series-parallel viscoelastic networks composed of springs and spring-pots of a common order $0<\alpha<1$. The associated complex shear modulus, expressed in an appropriately chosen auxiliary variable, is rational and admits a finite partial-fraction representation with simple poles on the negative real axis and negative residues. Its reciprocal admits an analogous representation with positive residues and a possible additional pole at the origin. Inverting the corresponding Laplace transforms gives finite fractional Prony representations of the relaxation modulus and creep compliance with non-negative amplitudes. These results provide a rigorous proof of a corrected version of the Mainardi-Spada conjecture for this network class. A six-element network illustrates the construction.

</details>


### [143] [Lieb-Robinson Light Cones in Long-Range Bosonic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00776)
*Carla Rubiliani,Cheng Shang,Tomotaka Kuwahara*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives improved Lieb-Robinson bounds for interacting bosonic systems with long-range interactions, revealing a nontrivial light-cone exponent governed by the interplay of interaction decay exponent α and bosonic interaction order p; optimal exponents are found for p ≥ 4 and 2d < α ≤ 2d + 1, showing richer causal structure than in bounded or short-range cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand emergent causality (via Lieb-Robinson bounds) in interacting bosonic systems where standard assumptions—short-range interactions and uniformly bounded local energy scales—fail, due to both long-range couplings and unbounded bosonic degrees of freedom.

Method: Derives matched upper and lower Lieb-Robinson bounds using a block-based propagation protocol that concentrates bosons to form a mediating sublattice with amplified interactions; employs a fixed-k extension of bounded-interaction techniques for the upper bound at α = 2d + 1.

Result: Identifies effective light-cone exponents determined jointly by α and p; establishes tight bounds (coinciding upper/lower) for p ≥ 4 and 2d < α ≤ 2d + 1; shows the upper bound extends to α = 2d + 1 with arbitrarily small exponent loss.

Conclusion: The combination of long-range interactions and unbounded bosonic operators yields a qualitatively richer light-cone structure—neither captured by existing long-range spin/fermion bounds nor by short-range bosonic analyses—highlighting a fundamental role of operator unboundedness in quantum dynamics.

Abstract: Lieb-Robinson bounds formulate emergent causality in quantum many-body dynamics. In this Letter, we study interacting bosonic systems with long-range interactions, in which both the standard assumptions of short-range interactions and uniformly bounded local energy scales are absent. We derive upper and lower bounds whose effective light-cone exponents are governed by a nontrivial interplay between the decay exponent $\alpha$ and the bosonic interaction order $p$. The propagation protocol concentrates bosons within blocks, creating a mediating sublattice with amplified interactions. For $p\ge4$ and $2d<\alpha\le2d+1$, the resulting exponents coincide, determining the optimal leading light-cone power. The upper bound includes $\alpha=2d+1$ with arbitrarily small exponent loss, using a fixed-$k$ extension of the bounded-interaction result. Our results reveal that the interplay between long-range couplings and unbounded bosonic degrees of freedom gives rise to a richer light-cone structure than in either long-range bounded systems or short-range bosonic systems alone.

</details>


### [144] [Explicit Boundary-Entropy Deficits in the XX Spin Chain with arbitrary boundary fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00946)
*Yi Qiao,Pei Sun,Junpeng Cao,Wen-Li Yang,Yupeng Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper rigorously derives the low-temperature thermodynamics of the open XX spin-1/2 chain with arbitrary Hermitian boundary fields using fusion techniques, revealing distinct boundary entropy contributions and crossover scales depending on transverse vs. diagonal boundary fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish exact boundary thermodynamics for open quantum integrable systems—particularly the XX chain—with general Hermitian boundaries, resolving how boundary fields affect low-temperature free energy and entropy, and providing a benchmark for boundary criticality and impurity physics.

Method: Fusion technique applied to the boundary Yang–Baxter equation; exact Bethe ansatz analysis of the open XX chain; low-temperature expansion of the free energy and boundary entropy; scaling analysis of the boundary crossover temperature $T_B$ in the weak transverse field limit.

Result: 1) A nonzero transverse boundary field contributes $+\frac{1}{2} T \ln 2$ to the free energy at low $T$; diagonal/free ends do not. 2) Each nondiagonal end has a characteristic crossover temperature $T_B \propto h_\perp^2 / J$; for $T \ll \min\{J, T_B\}$, boundary entropy is reduced by $\frac{1}{2} \ln 2$ relative to diagonal ends. 3) As $h_\perp \to 0$, $T_B \to 0$, leading to nonuniform low-$T$ expansion.

Conclusion: The exact boundary thermodynamics reveals universal entropic signatures of boundary field anisotropy, distinguishing nondiagonal (transverse) from diagonal boundaries, and establishes a quantitative framework for boundary criticality applicable to quantum dots and defects in critical systems.

Abstract: The exact thermodynamics of the XX spin-1/2 chain with arbitrary Hermitian boundary fields is constructed via fusion techniques. The rigorous result shows that (1) each end with a nonzero transverse field contributes $+\frac12T\ln2$ to the low-temperature free energy, whereas strictly diagonal and free ends contribute no such linear term; (2) each nondiagonal end has a characteristic temperature $T_B$, which scales as $T_B\propto h_\perp^2/J$ in the weak-field regime. For $T\ll\min\{J,T_B\}$, the boundary entropy approaches a deficit of $\frac12\ln2$ relative to a diagonal end. As the transverse field tends to zero, the boundary crossover scale vanishes and the low-temperature expansion becomes nonuniform. The present result provides a clear benchmark not only for the boundary thermodynamics of open boundary quantum integrable systems, but also for dot defects in quantum critical systems.

</details>


### [145] [Equivalence of superintegrable Poisson projection chains: magnetic cotangent bundles and spin Calogero-Moser models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00992)
*Kai Jiang,Guorui Ma,Junze Zhang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes spectral equivalence between two geometric reduction methods for constructing superintegrable Hamiltonian systems: Poisson projection chains from cotangent bundles of homogeneous spaces and left-right Hamiltonian reduction of T*G yielding spin Calogero-Moser systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify and compare distinct geometric constructions of superintegrable systems—specifically, Poisson projection chains arising from cotangent bundles of homogeneous spaces versus Hamiltonian reductions producing spin Calogero-Moser models—and to clarify their structural and dynamical correspondence.

Method: The authors analyze two reduction schemes: (i) the magnetic cotangent bundle $T^*(G/A)$ with $A = Z_G(\gamma)$ and symplectic form induced by the coadjoint orbit $G \cdot \gamma \cong G/A$, and (ii) left-right Hamiltonian reduction of $T^*G$. They establish isomorphisms between phase spaces, intermediate Poisson manifolds, and base spaces on an open dense subset of regular principal orbit type, preserving projection structures.

Result: The two reduction chains are spectrally equivalent on a dense open set: their phase spaces, Poisson structures, and projection hierarchies are compatibly identified, and they yield identical families of commuting Hamiltonians.

Conclusion: The cotangent bundle construction with magnetic symplectic structure and the left-right Hamiltonian reduction of $T^*G$ produce equivalent superintegrable systems in the sense of Poisson projection chains and spectral geometry, confirming a deep unification of geometric approaches to integrability.

Abstract: We study superintegrable Hamiltonian systems in terms of Poisson projection chains and their (spectral) equivalence. We compare two reduction constructions: (i) the cotangent bundle $T^*(G/A)$, $A=Z_G(\gamma)$, with the magnetic symplectic form defined by the orbit $G\cdot \gamma\cong G/A$, and (ii) the left-right Hamiltonian reduction of $T^*G$ that gives spin Calogero-Moser type systems. We prove that the two resulting chains are equivalent on an open set of regular points of principal orbit type: the phase spaces, the intermediate Poisson manifolds and the base spaces are identified compatibly with the projections. In particular, both constructions give the same commuting Hamiltonians.

</details>


### [146] [Canonical quantum curve I](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00997)
*Shuai Guo,Qingsheng Zhang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates and proves a canonical quantum curve conjecture $\widehat H \Psi_*=0$ for spectral curves of positive genus, where $\Psi_*$ is a non-perturbative wave function evaluated at a distinguished 'canonical vanishing point'—a zero of the closed non-perturbative partition function. The quantized operator $\widehat H$ arises from Weyl quantization of $H(x,y)$ with $\/hbar$-dependent quantum corrections to coefficients, which vanish classically and are conjectured to match normalized Wilson-loop expectations or relate to Nekrasov–Shatashvili free energy derivatives. Proven for Weierstrass curves and mirror curves of canonical bundles over toric weak del Pezzo surfaces (e.g., local $\mathbb{P}^2$).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the quantum curve formalism beyond genus-zero spectral curves by introducing a canonical, geometrically distinguished basepoint—the canonical vanishing point—for defining non-perturbative wave functions, and to unify quantum curve constructions with refined topological string invariants (e.g., NS limit) and geometric expectations (e.g., Wilson loops).

Method: Uses Eynard–Orantin topological recursion to define a non-perturbative wave function $\Psi_*$; introduces the canonical vanishing point as a distinguished zero of the closed partition function; constructs $\widehat H$ via Weyl quantization with universal $\hbar$-corrections depending only on closed moduli; verifies the conjecture via explicit computation and comparison with geometric/NS data for Weierstrass and toric del Pezzo mirror curves.

Result: The quantum curve equation $\widehat H \Psi_*=0$ is rigorously proven for Weierstrass curves and mirror curves of canonical bundles over smooth projective toric weak del Pezzo surfaces (including local $\mathbb{P}^2$); the canonical vanishing point is linked to the derivative of the closed Nekrasov–Shatashvili free energy; for local $\mathbb{P}^2$, the quantum-corrected coefficient matches a geometrically defined, normalized NS Wilson-loop expectation value.

Conclusion: The canonical quantum curve conjecture provides a consistent, geometrically grounded framework for quantum curves of positive-genus spectral curves, unifying topological recursion, quantization, refined invariants, and gauge-theoretic expectations; its verification in key examples supports its universality and suggests deeper links between integrable systems, enumerative geometry, and quantum field theory.

Abstract: We formulate a canonical quantum curve conjecture $\widehat H\Psi_*=0$ for spectral curves of positive genus. Here $\Psi_*$ is the non-perturbative wave function associated with Eynard--Orantin topological recursion, specialized at the \emph{canonical vanishing point}, a distinguished zero of the closed non-perturbative partition function. The operator $\widehat H$ is obtained by Weyl quantization of $H(x,y)$, with $\widehat x=x\cdot$ and $\widehat y=\hbar\partial_x$, together with quantum corrections to its coefficients. These corrections depend only on closed moduli and $\hbar$ and vanish classically. Whenever a geometric definition is available, we conjecture that the corrected coefficients are suitably normalized Wilson-loop expectation values. When a corresponding refined theory exists, we conjecture a relation between the canonical vanishing point and the closed Nekrasov--Shatashvili free energy. We prove the quantum curve conjecture for Weierstrass curves and the mirror curves of canonical bundles of smooth projective toric weak del Pezzo surfaces, including local $\mathbb P^2$. For the local surfaces considered here, we relate the canonical vanishing point to a derivative of the closed NS free energy. For local $\mathbb P^2$, we also identify the corrected coefficient with a suitably normalized, geometrically defined NS Wilson-loop expectation value.

</details>


### [147] [Semiclassical Wave Packet Dynamics For Nonlinear Schrödinger Equations with Harmonic Potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01108)
*Jared Smith,Tomoki Ohsawa*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces an approximate Hamiltonian dynamics for the Gross--Pitaevskii equation using semiclassical wave packets (Hagedorn-type), extending prior Gaussian ansatz work; it derives evolution equations from a symplectic/Hamiltonian viewpoint—shown equivalent to the variational approach in the Gaussian limit—and validates accuracy numerically.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a more accurate and physically grounded approximation framework for the Gross--Pitaevskii equation under harmonic confinement, moving beyond the restrictive Gaussian ansatz by leveraging the richer structure of semiclassical wave packets and a Hamiltonian/symplectic formulation.

Method: Extends the Gaussian ansatz to Hagedorn semiclassical wave packets; derives time-evolution equations via Hamiltonian reduction on the symplectic manifold of wave packet parameters; proves equivalence with the Lagrangian/variational approach in the Gaussian limit; implements and tests the derived dynamics numerically.

Result: Derives closed-form Hamiltonian evolution equations for general semiclassical wave packet parameters; establishes formal equivalence between symplectic and variational derivations in the Gaussian case; demonstrates improved numerical fidelity over pure Gaussian approximations in simulations of GP dynamics.

Conclusion: The Hamiltonian/symplectic approach provides a systematic, geometrically consistent generalization of Gaussian approximations for the Gross--Pitaevskii equation, enabling higher-fidelity semiclassical modeling while preserving conservation laws and structural properties inherent to the underlying Hamiltonian structure.

Abstract: We propose an approximate Hamiltonian dynamics for the Gross--Pitaevskii equation $i\hbar\psi_t = -\frac{\hbar^2}{2m}\Delta \psi + V(x)\psi + \hbar^2 \lambda |\psi|^2\psi$ with harmonic potential $V(x)$ and $x\in\mathbb{R}^d$. We do this by extending the Gaussian ansatz of Perez-Garcia et al.~(Phys.~Rev.~A 56, 1424 (1997)) to the semiclassical wave packets of Hagedorn. We derive the evolution equations for the wave packets from the Hamiltonian/symplectic perspective, rather than the Lagrangian/variational one taken by Perez-Garcia et al. We first show that these two perspectives yield identical evolution equations in the Gaussian case. We then derive the evolution equations for an arbitrary element of the semiclassical wave packets. Finally, we demonstrate the efficacy of the approximate dynamics with numerical experiments.

</details>


### [148] [Density functionals for the Pauli-Fierz Hamiltonian](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01208)
*Mihály A. Csirik,Andre Laestadius,Markus Penz,Erik I. Tellgren*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper formulates a density-functional theory for the Pauli-Fierz Hamiltonian in nonrelativistic QED using a convex-analytic (Lieb-style) approach, introducing electron density and a combined current density as fundamental variables; it proves representability, establishes equivalence of mixed-state constrained-search and Lieb functionals, shows convexity (but not necessarily attainment) of the pure-state Levy-Lieb functional due to photon softening, recovers the Vignale-Rasolt functional in the zero-coupling limit, and derives the dipole-limit functional via constrained search over the length-gauge Hamiltonian.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend density-functional theory to quantum electrodynamical systems—specifically, electrons coupled to quantized electromagnetic fields—by rigorously grounding it in convex analysis, addressing foundational issues like representability, functional convexity, and limits relevant to realistic physical regimes (e.g., zero coupling, long wavelength).

Method: Convex-analytic formulation à la Lieb: defines natural variables (electron density ρ and combined current density j = j_p + j_A), proves representability of (ρ, j)-pairs, constructs mixed-state constrained-search functional, shows its equivalence to the Legendre–Fenchel transform (Lieb functional), leverages soft-photon purification to relate pure- and mixed-state functionals, and analyzes asymptotic limits (g → 0, k → 0) via functional convergence and constrained search.

Result: 1) Representability of (ρ, j) pairs is established; 2) Mixed-state constrained-search functional equals the Lieb functional and attains its infimum; 3) Pure-state Levy-Lieb functional equals the Lieb functional and is convex, though minimizers may not exist; 4) Zero-coupling limit recovers the Vignale-Rasolt functional; 5) Dipole (long-wavelength) limit yields a constrained-search functional equivalent to that of the length-gauge dipole Hamiltonian.

Conclusion: A rigorous, convex DFT framework for Pauli-Fierz QED is achieved, unifying electron density and current as basic variables, resolving foundational questions about functional existence and convexity, and connecting seamlessly to established current-density-functional theory and dipole approximations—thereby enabling future ab initio QED-DFT calculations.

Abstract: We develop a density-functional theory for the Pauli-Fierz Hamiltonian of nonrelativistic quantum electrodynamics in the spirit of the convex-analytic formulation of Lieb. We consider finitely many electrons, subject to external scalar and vector potentials, coupled to the full continuum of massless transverse photons with an ultraviolet cutoff. The natural basic variables are the standard electron density and a combined current density, the sum of the paramagnetic current density and a field-induced current density generated by the quantized vector potential. We prove representability results for these density-current pairs and show that the mixed-state constrained-search functional attains its infimum and coincides with the Lieb functional obtained by convex conjugation. Since arbitrarily soft photons purify mixed states at a negligible energy cost without changing their density and combined current density, the pure-state Levy-Lieb functional equals the Lieb functional and is therefore convex, even though its infimum need not be attained. In addition, we establish the zero-coupling limit, which recovers the mixed-state Vignale-Rasolt functional of current-density-functional theory. Finally, we consider the long-wavelength (dipole) limit and show that the limiting functional can be written as a constrained search for the well-known length-gauge dipole Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [149] [Exponential suppression of bit-flip errors in dissipative cat qubits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01296)
*Ruikang Liang,Rémi Robin*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the Lindblad generator for a dissipative cat qubit under single-photon loss, proving exponential suppression of bit-flip errors via spectral analysis of a near-zero two-dimensional subspace.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To demonstrate robustness of the dissipative cat qubit against bit-flip errors induced by single-photon loss, leveraging engineered dissipation.

Method: Spectral analysis of the infinite-dimensional Lindblad generator $L_\epsilon = D[a^2 - \alpha^2] + \epsilon D[a]$, focusing on the low-lying spectrum near zero for large $\alpha$ and small $\epsilon > 0$.

Result: Identification of a two-dimensional spectral subspace near zero eigenvalue; derivation of an upper bound on the slow decay rate that decreases as $e^{-c\alpha^2}$, uniformly in $\epsilon$.

Conclusion: Bit-flip errors are exponentially suppressed with increasing coherent amplitude $\alpha$, confirming intrinsic error resilience of the cat-qubit encoding under dominant two-photon loss and weak single-photon loss.

Abstract: We study the infinite-dimensional Lindblad generator $L_\epsilon=D[a^2-\alpha^2]+\epsilon D[a]$, describing a dissipative cat qubit subject to single-photon loss. For sufficiently large $\alpha$ and sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$, we identify a two-dimensional spectral subspace near zero and establish an upper bound on the slow decay rate that decreases exponentially with $\alpha^2$, uniformly in $\epsilon$. This proves exponential suppression of bit-flip errors.

</details>


### [150] [Eynard-Orantin Topological Recursions for Higher Weil-Petersson Volumes on Moduli Spaces of Super Riemann Surfaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01497)
*Dingkang Xiong,Jian Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes an Eynard-Orantin topological recursion for intersection numbers involving Norbury's Θ-class, ψ-classes, and higher κ-classes, which compute higher Weil-Petersson volumes of super Riemann surface moduli spaces; the recursion generalizes the Bessel curve topological recursion (linked to the Brézin-Gross-Witten model) by incorporating higher-κ deformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To compute higher Weil-Petersson volumes of moduli spaces of super Riemann surfaces via intersection theory, and to generalize known topological recursions (e.g., Bessel curve/BGW) to include Norbury’s Θ-class and higher κ-classes.

Method: Derivation of Eynard-Orantin topological recursion using intersection theory on moduli spaces of curves, incorporating Norbury’s Θ-class, ψ-classes, and higher κ-classes; comparison and deformation of the Bessel curve recursion.

Result: A new topological recursion formula governing intersection numbers with Θ-, ψ-, and higher κ-classes; interpretation as a higher-κ deformation of the Bessel curve recursion; encoding of higher Weil-Petersson volumes for super Riemann surface moduli spaces.

Conclusion: The derived recursion provides a unified enumerative framework for super Riemann surface geometry and extends the scope of Eynard-Orantin theory to include Norbury’s Θ-class and higher κ-classes, bridging intersection theory, topological recursion, and supersymmetric matrix models.

Abstract: We derive an Eynard-Orantin topological recursion for intersection numbers involving Norbury's $\Theta$-class together with $\psi$-classes and higher $\kappa$-classes. These intersection numbers encode higher Weil-Petersson volumes of moduli spaces of super Riemann surfaces. The resulting recursion is a higher-$\kappa$ deformation of the topological recursion associated with the Bessel curve, which encodes the Brézin-Gross-Witten model.

</details>


### [151] [The massive phase of the two-dimensional Heisenberg model is stable under small anisotropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01532)
*Yan Ru Pei*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves that the massive (exponentially decaying correlations) phase of the 2D classical Heisenberg model persists under small anisotropic perturbations, including easy-plane and single-ion anisotropies; it quantifies how the BKT transition temperature vanishes as isotropy is restored (e.g., λ↑1), and extends results to O(n) models with n≥3.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish stability of the massive phase against anisotropy in the 2D classical Heisenberg model—addressing whether exponential decay of correlations survives beyond the exactly isotropic case, and clarifying the asymptotics of the BKT transition onset in nearly isotropic regimes.

Method: Adapts the recent isotropic proof by preserving the sign-cluster argument and Ginibre’s correlation inequality for planar anisotropic Hamiltonians; exploits rotation symmetry only on a single finite annulus where anisotropy acts as a bounded perturbation; uses uniform finite-volume estimates with free boundary conditions and edge-strength control.

Result: For any β, there exists δ(β) > 0 such that the S²-valued anisotropic Heisenberg model with couplings Jⁱ ≈ 1 has uniformly exponentially decaying two-point functions across all finite subgraphs (free BC) and edge strengths ≤ β. For the easy-plane model (λ < 1), exponential decay holds up to β ≲ |log(1−λ)|¹ᐟ²; analogous results hold for single-ion anisotropy and O(n) models with n ≥ 3.

Conclusion: The massive phase is robust under small anisotropy; the BKT transition temperature vanishes at least as |log(1−λ)|⁻¹ᐟ² (vs. predicted |log(1−λ)|⁻¹) as isotropy is approached; the proof framework is generalizable to higher-spin and multi-component O(n) models.

Abstract: Polyakov predicted that the classical Heisenberg model on $\mathbb{Z}^2$ has exponentially decaying correlations at every temperature, and a proof of this has recently been posted. We show that the massive phase survives small anisotropy. For every inverse temperature $\beta$ there is $\delta(\beta)>0$ such that the $S^2$-valued model with couplings $\sum_iJ^i\sigma_x^i\sigma_y^i$ has exponentially decaying spin two-point functions, uniformly in finite subgraphs with free boundary conditions and in edge strengths up to $\beta$, whenever $|J^i-1|\le\delta(\beta)$ for $i=1,2,3$. For the easy-plane model with couplings $\sigma^x\sigma^x+\sigma^y\sigma^y+\lambda\sigma^z\sigma^z$ this shows that the onset of the Berezinskii-Kosterlitz-Thouless phase, proved by Garban and de Montgolfier for every $\lambda\in(-1,1)$, moves to zero temperature as $\lambda\uparrow1$: in free-boundary limits, correlations decay exponentially for every $\beta\le c |\log(1-\lambda)|^{1/2}$. The physical prediction is of order $|\log(1-\lambda)|$. The same holds for aligned single-ion anisotropy, which includes a confining potential $\varepsilon\sum(\sigma^z)^2$, and for nearly isotropic $O(n)$ models with $n\ge3$. The proof keeps the sign-cluster argument of the isotropic proof, using Ginibre's correlation inequality for anisotropic planar energies, and uses the rotation symmetry only on one finite annulus, where the anisotropy is a bounded perturbation of the Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [152] [Covariant Calculus and Hamiltonian Flows for Nonlocal Wasserstein Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.01979)
*Yuanke Chen,Jinqiao Duan,Qiao Huang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a geometric framework for nonlocal metrics on probability densities over closed manifolds using symmetric jump kernels and density-dependent mobilities; it introduces a density-variation operator, establishes isometric tangent space representations, derives Levi-Civita connections and Hamiltonian dynamics, and applies the theory to entropy diffusion, mean-field, Keller–Segel, and nonlocal Vlasov equations—highlighting connections to Schrödinger bridges and quantum analogues via Hopf–Cole and Madelung transformations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend Riemannian geometric calculus for probability densities to nonlocal settings—beyond classical Wasserstein geometry—by incorporating jump-type interactions and density-dependent mobility, enabling unified treatment of diverse nonlocal PDEs arising in statistical physics, biology, and quantum-inspired dynamics.

Method: Introduces a density-variation operator to compute functional derivatives of Dirichlet forms and kinetic energy; constructs dual tangent space representations (density and velocity-field) and proves their isometric equivalence via generator pseudoinverse; derives Levi-Civita connection and covariant differential in both representations; formulates Hamiltonian mechanics using action principles and Legendre duality; applies framework to entropy diffusion, nonlocal mean-field, Keller–Segel, and Vlasov-type systems; analyzes phase-energy structures under geometric-mean mobility via Hopf–Cole and Madelung transformations.

Result: A fully consistent nonlocal Riemannian structure on probability densities with explicit tangent bundle geometry, connections, and Hamiltonian dynamics; identification of zero-energy Hamiltonian lifts for gradient flows; derivation of nonlocal analogues of Hamilton–Jacobi and Newton equations; unification of several nonlocal PDEs under one geometric variational principle; emergence of hyperbolic (Schroedinger bridge) and trigonometric (nonlocal Schroedinger) phase energies, both reducing to standard kinetic energy at zero momentum up to second order.

Conclusion: The proposed nonlocal metric framework generalizes Otto’s calculus to jump-diffusion and density-modulated transport, providing a rigorous geometric foundation for analyzing and unifying diverse nonlocal evolution equations—especially those involving entropy-driven or interaction-mediated dynamics—with direct links to stochastic control and quantum hydrodynamics.

Abstract: In this paper, we study nonlocal metrics on smooth positive probability densities on a closed manifold, induced by symmetric jump kernels and density-dependent mobilities. First, we introduce the density-variation operator to express the density derivatives of the Dirichlet form and kinetic energy. Second, we construct density and velocity-field representations of the tangent space and prove their isometric equivalence. We use the generator's pseudoinverse to derive Levi-Civita connections and covariant differentials in both representations. Third, we establish mechanical Hamiltonian dynamics in both tangent representations; an action principle and Legendre duality identify the Hamilton--Jacobi and covariant Newton equations; smooth negative gradient flows admit a zero-energy Hamiltonian lift. Entropy diffusion, nonlocal mean-field equations, Keller--Segel dynamics, and a nonlocal Vlasov equation illustrate the resulting calculus. For geometric-mean mobility, the Hopf--Cole transformation of the nonlocal Schrödinger bridge system and the Madelung transformation of the nonlocal Schrödinger equation yield hyperbolic and trigonometric phase energies, respectively. At zero momentum, both recover the metric kinetic energy to second order.

</details>


### [153] [Nonlinear adiabatic theorem for the Gross-Pitaevskii equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02231)
*Sven Bachmann,Severin Schraven*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves a nonlinear adiabatic theorem for the time-dependent repulsive Gross-Pitaevskii equation with varying trapping potentials, showing that solutions initialized at ground states remain close to instantaneous energy minimizers under mild regularity or asymptotic conditions on the potential — provided global existence holds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend adiabatic theory from linear quantum mechanics to nonlinear mean-field models like the GP equation, where spectral gap assumptions fail and dynamics are governed by a non-autonomous energy functional.

Method: Functional analytic approach using energy estimates, uniform bounds on the time-dependent GP functional, and comparison with instantaneous ground states; distinguishes two classes of trapping potentials (smooth subquadratic vs. perturbed power-law) to handle different regularity regimes.

Result: Global well-posedness and adiabatic tracking are established under either (i) smooth, subquadratic V(x,t) with bounded higher-order spatial derivatives, or (ii) uniformly bounded perturbations of |x|^m potentials (m ≥ 2); crucially, adiabaticity follows almost automatically once global existence is secured.

Conclusion: Adiabatic behavior in the repulsive GP equation is robust and primarily constrained by existence—not regularity—of solutions; the theorem identifies minimal potential conditions ensuring both existence and adiabatic approximation.

Abstract: We prove a nonlinear adiabatic theorem for the repulsive Gross-Pitaevskii (GP) equation with a time-dependent trapping potential $V(x,t)$. If either (i) $V(\cdot,t)$ is smooth, subquadratic in space and with uniformly bounded spatial derivatives of order $\geq 2$, or (ii) $V(\cdot,t)$ is a uniformly bounded perturbation of a fixed non-negative potential which is asymptotically $|x|^m, m\geq 2$, then the GP equation has a global solution, which remains uniformly close to the instantaneous minimizer of the time-independent GP functional, provided the initial condition is a ground state. In fact, we show that the existence of a solution of the GP equation is the real bottleneck: If existence is assumed, then adiabaticity holds under very minimal assumptions.

</details>


### [154] [Lieb-Robinson bounds for Bose-Hubbard Hamiltonians via path expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02263)
*Alexander Elgart,Marius Lemm*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper provides simplified proofs for sharp bounds on boson transport and Lieb-Robinson velocity in Bose-Hubbard systems, introduces weighted extended particle number operators for transport analysis, adapts the Chen-Lucas path expansion for bosons to recover near-sharp light-cone bounds, and extends transport bounds to long-range hopping with α ∈ (d, d+1), establishing an algebraic light cone previously unaddressed.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide simpler, shorter proofs of known sharp transport and Lieb-Robinson bounds for Bose-Hubbard Hamiltonians, and to extend boson transport bounds to previously uncovered long-range interaction regimes (specifically α ∈ (d, d+1)).

Method: For boson transport: introduced weighted extended particle number operators to close differential inequalities. For Lieb-Robinson bounds: developed a bosonic adaptation of the Chen-Lucas path expansion technique. For long-range extension: generalized the transport analysis to power-law hopping with exponent α > d, focusing on the intermediate regime d < α < d+1.

Result: Simplified derivations of sharp particle transport bounds and Lieb-Robinson velocity t^{d−1} (up to logarithmic corrections); first derivation of algebraic light cones for boson transport in the long-range regime α ∈ (d, d+1).

Conclusion: The new methods—weighted particle number operators and bosonic path expansion—not only streamline existing proofs but also enable novel extensions to long-range interactions, revealing an algebraic light-cone structure for boson transport where none was previously established.

Abstract: We study the transport properties of Bose-Hubbard-type Hamiltonians. Kuwahara, Vu, and Saito proved sharp bounds on (i) particle transport and (ii) the Lieb-Robinson velocity for initial states with controlled boson density. We provide simpler and shorter proofs for both results using a new set of methods. For (i), boson transport, we use a class of weighted extended particle number operators that directly enables closing the associated differential inequalities. For (ii), the Lieb-Robinson bounds, we develop a bosonic version of the Chen-Lucas path expansion technique and recover the sharp velocity bound $t^{d-1}$ up to logarithmic corrections, where $d$ denotes the lattice dimension. We also extend the transport bounds (i) to long-range hopping with a power-law exponent $\alpha>d$. The range $\alpha\in (d,d+1)$ corresponds to an algebraic light cone for boson transport and has not been previously covered.

</details>


### [155] [Quantum 6j-symbols arising from a subfactor of finite index and finite depth and the zipper condition for 4-tensors in two-dimensional topological order](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02582)
*Yasuyuki Kawahigashi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a precise correspondence between 3-tensors satisfying the 'zipper condition'—central in tensor network descriptions of 2D topological order—and the quantum 6j-symbols (F-symbols) of a unitary multi-fusion category derived from a finite-depth subfactor, linking tensor networks, subfactor theory, and categorical data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge tensor network methods for two-dimensional topological order with rigorous algebraic structures from operator algebras—specifically, to understand the mathematical nature of 'zipper condition' 3-tensors in terms of categorical invariants like F-symbols.

Method: Leverages the established identification of 4-tensors with bi-unitary connections from Jones subfactor theory; imposes finite depth on the associated subfactor and uses its unitary multi-fusion category structure to characterize the 3-tensors via quantum 6j-symbols (F-symbols), contrasting with prior realizations via flat bi-unitary connections on higher relative commutants.

Result: Proves that, under finite-depth assumption, 3-tensors satisfying the zipper condition are exactly the F-symbols of the unitary multi-fusion category associated to the subfactor—providing a new, intrinsic categorical realization of these tensors.

Conclusion: The zipper condition selects precisely the associator data (F-symbols) of the unitary multi-fusion category from a finite-depth subfactor, unifying tensor network ansätze with subfactor-generated fusion categories and offering a structural foundation for topological order models.

Abstract: We study 3-tensors satisfying the ``zipper condition,'' which play an important role in tensor network approaches to two-dimensional topological order. In our previous work, we identified the 4-tensors appearing in these approaches with bi-unitary connections in the Jones theory of subfactors in operator algebras. These bi-unitary connections give rise to subfactors of finite index. We prove that, when the associated subfactor has finite depth, the 3-tensors satisfying the zipper condition correspond precisely to the quantum $6j$-symbols, also known as $F$-symbols, of the unitary multi-fusion category arising from the subfactor. This realization of quantum $6j$-symbols differs from their realization through a flat bi-unitary connection arising from the higher relative commutants of a subfactor.

</details>


### [156] [Derivation of Novel Isotropic Metamaterials at the Hashin Shtrikman Stiffness Limit Enabled by Physics Informed Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02622)
*Manish Kumar Singh,Dominic Kang Jueh Lim,Anson Wen Han Cheong,Adams Wai Kin Kong,Chang Quan Lai*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Physics-Informed Neural Network (PINN)-driven approach to systematically design ultrastiff, isotropic closed-cell plate-based metamaterials—bypassing heuristic or topology-optimization methods—and demonstrates that 6-plate lattices achieve near-optimal stiffness (90% of Hashin-Shtrikman upper bound) and high isotropy (Zener ratio = 0.95), outperforming most existing isotropic architectures in specific stiffness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome inefficiency and human bias in current heuristic- or topology-optimization-based design of ultrastiff isotropic metamaterials.

Method: Physics-Informed Neural Networks (PINNs) applied to analytical equations to solve for optimal plate orientations in closed-cell lattices; geometries modelled as quasi-random foams, validated via finite element simulation; 6-plate designs fabricated using LAPIS additive manufacturing and experimentally characterized.

Result: 6 plates are the minimum required for ultrastiff isotropy; 7 plates recover the known Cubic Octet; 8–10 plates show diminishing returns; PINN-designed lattices reach 90% of Hashin-Shtrikman upper bound at ρ̃ = 0.4 and maintain Zener ratio ≈ 0.95 at ρ̃ = 0.1; specific stiffness exceeds most reported isotropic architectures.

Conclusion: PINNs enable systematic, physics-constrained discovery of high-performance isotropic plate lattices, with 6-plate configurations representing a new optimal class balancing stiffness, isotropy, and manufacturability.

Abstract: Current methods of deriving ultrastiff isotropic metamaterials require heuristics or topology optimization, which are highly inefficient and limited by human input. To circumvent this, Physics Informed Neural Networks are applied to analytical equations to systematically solve for plate orientations in closed cell lattices. Following a standardized procedure, the plate orientations were modelled and assembled into geometries resembling quasi random foams for validation via finite element simulations. Closed cell 6-plate geometries were also additively manufactured using LAPIS and experimentally characterized. The results indicate that a minimum of 6 plates is required to constitute an ultrastiff isotropic lattice, 7 plates would return the established plate Cubic Octet structure while 8 to 10 plate geometries did not show significant differences in properties. Despite the introduction of some plate bending due to sub-optimal plate placement strategy, relative stiffness of the PINNs lattices adheres closely to the Hashin Shtrikman upper bound, 90 percent of Hashin Shtrikman upper bound at a relative density of 0.4, and the lattices maintain near isotropic behaviour, achieving a Zener ratio of 0.95 at a relative density of 0.1. Benchmarking results show that specific stiffness of PINNs lattices is higher than the majority of isotropic architectures reported in the literature.

</details>


### [157] [Mathematical and Numerical Analysis of Tsunami Generation and Propagation: The Initial Tsunami Condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02708)
*Noureddine Lamsahel,Min Chen*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes the mathematically appropriate initial free-surface condition for tsunami propagation models: for the nonlinear shallow-water (NSW) equations, it is exactly the final bottom deformation; for the Boussinesq model, it requires applying a Boussinesq filtering operator to the bottom profile. It proves a linear error bound in earthquake rise time between kinematic and static formulations, vanishing for instantaneous slip, and validates findings against Okada solutions and linear potential theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To identify the correct initial free-surface condition that permits replacing the physically accurate kinematic tsunami generation model (with time-evolving seafloor) by a computationally simpler static formulation (fixed final seafloor deformation), and to quantify the resulting modeling error.

Method: Mathematical analysis of the 2D nonlinear shallow-water (NSW) equations and the dispersive abcd-Boussinesq system; derivation of the appropriate initial condition via asymptotic consistency; rigorous error estimation between kinematic and static solutions as a function of earthquake rise time; numerical validation using Okada seabed deformation and comparison with linear potential theory.

Result: For NSW, the correct initial free surface equals the final bottom deformation; for Boussinesq, it is the Boussinesq-filtered bottom profile. The error between kinematic and static solutions is bounded linearly by the earthquake rise time. Numerical tests confirm that both NSW and unfiltered static initialization overestimate wave amplitudes relative to linear potential theory.

Conclusion: The static tsunami generation approximation is justified only when the initial free surface is set appropriately—unfiltered for NSW, filtered for Boussinesq—and its error is controlled by earthquake rise time. This provides a rigorous basis for model selection and error quantification in operational tsunami forecasting.

Abstract: This work investigates the appropriate initial condition for tsunami propagation that allows replacing a kinematic system with its corresponding static formulation, where the bottom is fixed at its final earthquake-induced deformation. Using the two-dimensional (2D) nonlinear shallow-water equations (NSW) and a 2D dispersive $abcd$-Boussinesq system, we prove that for the NSW system, the appropriate initial free-surface condition is exactly the final bottom deformation. For the Boussinesq model, this condition is obtained by first applying a \textit{ Boussinesq filtering operator} to the bottom profile. Furthermore, for both models, we establish an error estimate between the kinematic and static solutions that depends linearly on the earthquake rise time. This result provides a rigorous mathematical explanation for the errors observed in previous numerical tsunami simulations. As a direct consequence, the error vanishes as the bottom motion becomes instantaneous. We validate the theoretical results using an Okada solution for the seabed deformation. In particular, both the NSW model and the passive approach, which initializes the free surface with the unfiltered bottom deformation, overestimate the amplitudes of the tsunami waves relative to the linear potential theory for the configurations considered.

</details>


### [158] [All-Disorder Localization for the One-Dimensional Anderson Model with Power-Law Hopping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02724)
*Huajian Jiang,Yunfeng Shi*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves spectral localization (pure point spectrum with polynomially decaying eigenfunctions) for a 1D long-range random Schrödinger operator with decay exponent r > 3/2, confirming r = 3/2 as the critical threshold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish spectral localization for a one-dimensional long-range random Schrödinger operator, resolving the predicted marginal exponent r = 3/2 from prior physics literature (analytical and numerical studies).

Method: Mathematical spectral analysis of random operators; employs probabilistic and functional-analytic techniques to prove almost-sure pure point spectrum and polynomial decay of eigenfunctions for r > 3/2.

Result: For all λ > 0 and r > 3/2, H_{λ,ω} almost surely has pure point spectrum and admits an orthonormal eigenbasis {ψ_j} with ∑_n (1+|n|)^{2τ}|ψ_j(n)|² < ∞ for all j and 0 ≤ τ < r − 3/2.

Conclusion: Spectral localization is rigorously confirmed for r > 3/2; the threshold r = 3/2 is critical—localization fails at or below it, validating earlier physical predictions.

Abstract: We consider the one-dimensional random operator \[ (H_{\lambda,\omega}u)(n) = \sum_{m\ne n}\frac{u(m)}{|n-m|^r} +\lambda\omega_nu(n), \] where the random variables $\omega_n$ are independent and uniformly distributed on $[-1,1]$ (i.e., \mbox{$\omega_n\stackrel{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\operatorname{Unif}[-1,1]$}). For every $\lambda>0$ and $r>3/2$, we prove that $H_{\lambda,\omega}$ almost surely has pure point spectrum and admits an orthonormal eigenbasis $\{\psi_j\}$ satisfying \[ \sum_{n\in\bbZ}(1+|n|)^{2\tau}|\psi_j(n)|^2<\infty \qquad \text{for every }j\text{ and }0\le\tau<r-\frac32. \] These conclusions establish spectral localization, thereby rigorously confirming the prediction of earlier analytical and numerical studies in the physics literature, which identified $r=3/2$ as the marginal exponent.

</details>


### [159] [Correlators of the Jacobi Unitary Ensemble on a General Interval and Hurwitz Numbers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02795)
*Matvey Sushkov*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper extends the large-N genus expansion of Jacobi Unitary Ensemble (JUE) correlators from the standard interval [0,1] to arbitrary [p,q], expressing them via triple monotone Hurwitz numbers; it derives an explicit Schur–Selberg integral formula using symmetric group characters and content products, and recovers GUE polygon-gluing asymptotics in a double-scaling limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize the known large-N topological (genus) expansion of JUE correlators—previously established only for [0,1]—to arbitrary spectral intervals [p,q], while preserving structural connections to Hurwitz theory, integrable systems (2D Toda), and Gaussian Unitary Ensemble limits.

Method: Uses the Frobenius characteristic map and idempotent basis of the center of the symmetric group algebra ℂ[𝔖ₙ]; employs combinatorial representation theory to compute Schur–Selberg integrals; leverages double-scaling limits and tau-function specialization in the 2D Toda hierarchy.

Result: 1) Genus expansion of JUE correlators ⟨∏ⱼ tr X^{λⱼ}⟩ for arbitrary [p,q] in terms of triple monotone Hurwitz numbers; 2) Closed-form expression for Schur–Selberg integrals involving χ_λ, Vandermonde squared, and Jacobi weight over [p,q]^N, given via irreducible Sₙ-characters and content products; 3) Recovery of GUE polygon-gluing formula term-by-term as c_A = c_B → ∞; 4) Structural insight: only the second time-spectrum of the associated 2D Toda tau function changes when shifting from [0,1] to [p,q].

Conclusion: The topological expansion and integrable structure of the Jacobi Unitary Ensemble are robust under affine rescaling of the support [p,q]; the combinatorial–representation-theoretic framework unifies JUE, GUE, and Hurwitz enumerative geometry through tau-function specialization and character formulas.

Abstract: We study correlators of the Jacobi Unitary Ensemble $\mathbf J(p,q,\alpha,\beta,N)$ of Hermitian matrices with spectrum in an arbitrary real interval $[p,q]$. For $(p,q)=(0,1)$, Gisonni, Grava and Ruzza expressed the large-$N$ expansion of the correlators $\langle\prod_j \mathrm{tr}\,X^{\lambda_j}\rangle$ through triple monotone Hurwitz numbers. We extend this genus expansion to arbitrary $p,q$ with $\alpha/N$ and $\beta/N$ fixed. Using the Frobenius characteristic map and the idempotent basis of the center of $\mathbb C[\mathfrak S_n]$, we also obtain an explicit formula for the Schur--Selberg type integral $\int_{(p,q)^N}\chi_\lambda(x)\,\Delta^2(x)\prod_a (q-x_a)^\beta (x_a-p)^\alpha\,\mathrm{d}^Nx$ in terms of irreducible characters of the symmetric group and content products. As consequences, we describe the zero-mean ensemble, and we recover, term by term in the genus expansion, the polygon-gluing formula for the Gaussian Unitary Ensemble in the limit $c_A=c_B\to\infty$. Structurally, passing from $[0,1]$ to $[p,q]$ leaves the hypergeometric weight of the associated 2D Toda tau function unchanged and only changes the specialization of its second set of times.

</details>


### [160] [Microscopic Origin of the Equation of Motion for Open Thermodynamic Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03235)
*E. Aydiner*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper independently derives the same second-order thermodynamic equation of motion—previously obtained from the First Law—for open systems, now from a mesoscopic particle-transfer model between two reservoirs; it identifies physical origins of inertial, dissipative, and restoring coefficients, predicts damping regimes, relaxation times, resonance, and recovers Onsager linear response in the slow-transport limit, thereby reinforcing the dynamical universality of thermodynamic systems with mechanical/electrical oscillators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide independent theoretical validation and deeper physical grounding for the previously proposed second-order thermodynamic equation of motion by deriving it from a mesoscopic particle-transfer model—distinct from the macroscopic First Law approach—and to identify the physical mechanisms underlying its effective inertial, dissipative, and restoring coefficients.

Method: Derives the equation of motion from first principles of a mesoscopic model: collective momentum balance, particle number conservation, and reservoir chemical-potential response across a channel connecting two reservoirs; obtains explicit expressions for effective mass, damping, and stiffness coefficients in terms of microscopic/physical parameters; analyzes dynamic behavior including damping regimes, relaxation times, resonance conditions, and asymptotic recovery of Onsager linear response.

Result: Recovers identical second-order inertial–dissipative–restoring equation of motion; derives analytical expressions for effective coefficients (inertial, frictional, elastic) in terms of reservoir densities, channel conductance, compressibility, and transport kinetics; identifies distinct overdamped, underdamped, and resonant regimes; determines natural frequency and resonance conditions explicitly from physical parameters; shows Onsager linear flux–force relation emerges in the slow-transport (zero-inertia) limit.

Conclusion: The independent emergence of the same dynamical structure from two physically distinct foundations—macroscopic thermodynamics and mesoscopic transport—strongly supports the generality and physical legitimacy of the thermodynamic equation of motion; the identification of microscopically grounded coefficients establishes a concrete bridge between irreversible thermodynamics and classical dynamical systems theory, enabling unified modeling of open thermodynamic, mechanical, and electrical systems.

Abstract: In my previous work (arXiv:2609.07810) \cite{Aydiner_2026}, I derived a second-order equation of motion for open thermodynamic systems from the First Law of Thermodynamics and established its dynamical equivalence with mechanical oscillators and electrical RLC circuits. In the present study, I independently recover the same mathematical structure of the equation of motion from a mesoscopic model of particle transfer between two reservoirs connected by a channel. Starting from collective momentum balance, particle conservation, and reservoir chemical-potential response, I derive the inertial--dissipative--restoring dynamics and obtain explicit expressions for its effective coefficients in terms of the physical parameters of the system. The resulting equation predicts distinct damping regimes, characteristic relaxation time scales, and resonance under periodic driving. The natural frequency and resonance conditions are explicitly determined by the physical parameters of the particle-transfer system. In the slow-transport limit, the model recovers the conventional Onsager-type linear flux--force relation. The independent emergence of the same dynamical structure from two distinct physical approaches provides theoretical support for the previously proposed thermodynamic equation of motion. By identifying the physical mechanisms underlying its effective coefficients, the present study strengthens the physical foundations of the proposed framework and supports the possibility of describing open thermodynamic systems within a common dynamical framework shared with mechanical and electrical systems.

</details>


### [161] [Anderson localisation in infinite and finite resonator crystals with long-range interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03286)
*Habib Ammari,Xin Fu,Wenjia Jing,Jiayu Qiu,Alexander Uhlmann*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves Anderson localization for random capacitance operators modeling subwavelength high-contrast resonators in periodic 2D/3D lattices, with disorder introduced via independent uniform material parameters; it quantifies mode concentration, establishes spectral/dynamical localization near the upper edge, derives explicit disorder-dependent localization windows, and shows uniformity across small contrast regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously establish Anderson localization in a physically realistic setting of subwavelength resonators with full exponential-capacitance interaction—beyond nearest-neighbor approximations—and to quantify spatial concentration of eigenmodes under disorder in high-contrast metamaterials.

Method: Uses variational boundary comparison and material-variable eigenvalue-spacing estimates to lift localization from truncated (finite-range) capacitance matrices to full finite matrices; exploits coordinate reflection symmetry and exponential decay of capacitance coefficients; applies probabilistic analysis for mode concentration in bounded samples; derives limiting density of states via convergence arguments.

Result: Spectral and dynamical localization near the upper spectral edge for all positive disorder strengths; explicit O(W) localization window width at weak disorder W; O(ε^{1−d} log(1/ε)) non-concentrated modes and O(ε log(1/ε)) concentration radius for localized modes in ε-scaled samples; uniform localization estimates across small contrast; common limiting DOS for truncated and finite matrices.

Conclusion: Anderson localization is robust for random capacitance operators arising from disordered subwavelength resonator arrays, with quantifiable mode confinement and universal spectral statistics—validating the use of such models for designing disorder-resilient metamaterials with tailored wave localization.

Abstract: We establish Anderson localisation for random capacitance operators and quantify concentration of individual modes of finite capacitance matrices for clusters of subwavelength high-contrast resonators. The underlying geometry is fully periodic in dimensions $d=2,3$, with one resonator per cell, and the inverse material factors are independent and uniformly distributed. We retain the full capacitance interaction, whose coefficients decay exponentially with lattice separation. Under coordinate reflection symmetry, spectral and dynamical localisation hold near the upper spectral edge for every fixed positive disorder strength. At weak disorder $W$, we obtain an explicit localisation window of energy width proportional to $W$; prescribed positive intervals are localised at sufficiently large disorder. A variational boundary comparison and a material-variable eigenvalue-spacing estimate transfer localisation from truncated matrices to individual modes of the finite capacitance matrix. In a bounded sample of cell size $\varepsilon$, with probability tending to one, all except for $O(\varepsilon^{1-d}\log(1/\varepsilon))$ modes in the localisation interval concentrate within radii $O(\varepsilon\log(1/\varepsilon))$. The estimates are uniform for the contrast-dependent capacitance family at small contrast. We also prove a common limiting density of states for the truncated and finite matrices and illustrate concentration numerically.

</details>


### [162] [Separatrix splitting in a multiferroic neuromorphic oscillator: toward Arnold diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03297)
*D. Maroulakos,Z. Toklikishvili,A. Wal,L. Chotorlishvili*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel magnetoelectrically coupled ferromagnetic–ferroelectric neuromorphic oscillator, analyzes its Hamiltonian dynamics using action–angle formalism and elliptic functions, establishes KAM confinement in the autonomous case, and characterizes resonant structures, homoclinic splitting, and damping-dependent thresholds under periodic electric forcing—providing analytical foundations for polarization-based readout and transport in neuromorphic devices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond conventional ferromagnetic or antiferromagnetic neuromorphic systems by exploiting magnetoelectric coupling between ferromagnetic and ferroelectric layers for richer dynamical functionality and electrical readout capability.

Method: Derives a reduced conservative Landau–Lifshitz–type model with inertial ferroelectric polarization; constructs an action–angle description of the double-well–harmonic-oscillator Hamiltonian; computes quartic-well frequency maps and Fourier coefficients via elliptic functions; applies KAM theory to establish isoenergetic nondegeneracy and regular confinement; analyzes forced dynamics with third frequency, resonance intersections, homoclinic splitting, and damping-dependent thresholds using perturbation theory and Melnikov-type methods.

Result: Analytical characterization of monotone in-well twist, KAM-torus confinement in autonomous regime, transverse resonance intersections under forcing, leading-order resonant widths, local homoclinic splitting magnitude, and a damping-dependent threshold for splitting onset; identifies structures enabling polarization-based electrical readout and transport interpretation.

Conclusion: The magnetoelectric ferromagnetic–ferroelectric oscillator provides a theoretically grounded platform for neuromorphic computing with analytically tractable nonlinear dynamics; while Arnold diffusion remains unproven, the derived structures support design of controllable, energy-efficient, polarization-readout-compatible devices.

Abstract: Prototype magnetic neuromorphic systems, in essence, are based either on ferromagnetic or antiferromagnetic materials as key elements for conventional neuromorphic setups. In this work, we propose a novel neuromorphic system based on a ferromagnetic disk and a ferroelectric layer coupled via the magnetoelectric effect. We study a magnetoelectrically coupled ferromagnetic--ferroelectric oscillator as a candidate element for neuromorphic devices. A local reduction of conservative Landau--Lifshitz dynamics identifies a magnetic coordinate--momentum pair, which is coupled to an inertial ferroelectric polarization mode. We derive the action--angle description of the resulting double-well--harmonic-oscillator Hamiltonian, obtain the quartic-well frequency map and Fourier coefficients in terms of elliptic functions, and establish monotone in-well twist. In the autonomous two-degree-of-freedom model, isoenergetic nondegeneracy supports KAM confinement on regular compact domains. Periodic electric forcing introduces a third effective frequency, although the harmonic magnetic sector leaves the unperturbed resonance loci parallel. An explicitly specified nonlinear magnetic extension produces transverse resonance intersections.
